# K1709 — Codex 獨立審查（2026-07-14 12:23 台灣時間）

**審查者**: Codex CLI (gpt-5.6-sol, ultra reasoning) via `scripts/codex_exec_bounded.sh`
**受審對象**: `experiments/k1709/k1709.py` (worktree `dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709`, commit 9be94342d)
**結論**: **FAIL** — 兩個 CRITICAL 打掉招牌宣稱；不得寫入 knowledge.json，不得發文。

## Codex 的核心判決（原文）

> 可以接受：「在現有 expanding OOS 點估與診斷中，未發現 ETF flow 的穩健增量預測證據。」
>
> **不能**接受：「NULL 已成立」或「可排除每 1sd flow shock 帶來 ≥16% RV 效果」。

## 交叉驗證：專案自己的機械 gate 同意 Codex

`scripts/tests/test_nested_dm_misuse_ratchet.py::test_no_new_nested_raw_dm_sites` 對本檔 **FAIL**，
訊息與 Codex CRITICAL #1 完全一致（巢狀模型不得用 raw DM 承載 verdict；CW 只修正 MSPE，不能貼標成 QLIKE general-loss inference）。
K1709 重犯 2026-07-13 05:47 才修好的 K1701 教訓（見 docs/error_log.md）。

## 完整審查輸出

```

  它只檢查同一段程式人工 `.shift(pub_lag)` 出來的 `src_date`，無法證明各 predictor 的真實 provenance。我把同列 `y` 直接複製進 `z`，assert 仍通過；刪掉一個 Sunday 時，違規列也可能先被 `dropna(z)` 刪掉，h=5 的五列 rolling target 會跨成六個日曆日而 assert 仍通過。

  具體修法：在任何 rolling/shift 前檢查 index 完整、排序、無重複且等於完整 daily range；分開保存每組 predictor 的 source date；要求 `y_end_date == target_date + horizon - 1`；加入 future-mutation 與「刪 Sunday」測試。

實際主規格仍是乾淨的：rolling SD 在 [k1709.py:324–371](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:324) 有 `.shift(1)`；forward target 在 [k1709.py:426–430](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:426) 正確對應 `[τ, τ+h−1]`，沒有 target 混入 X。

### 2. 日曆對齊

- [MAJOR] 休市日 `Total=0.0` 被誤當成真實 flow day。[k1709.py:138–167](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:138)

  現行 Farside 資料中，BTC 有 16 個、ETH 有 10 個美股非交易日被保留為零 flow，包括 MLK、Good Friday、Memorial Day、Juneteenth、Thanksgiving、Christmas。`sum(skipna=True)` 會把全為 dash/NaN 的基金欄加成 0，因此 parser cross-check 也抓不到；這些休市日還會進入「20 flow-day」rolling SD。

  修法：在標準化前用 US equity session calendar 過濾非交易日，休市列記為 missing；只保留交易日上的 genuine zero，並記錄 dropped dates/count，再完整重跑。我的 session-only 診斷未翻轉主結論，但統計量確實有位移，例如 BTC h=1 H1 的 DM 由 0.140 變 1.542。

- [MAJOR] `pub_lag=2` 穩健性把 HAR/return controls 也錯誤 lag 2。[k1709.py:415–424](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:415)、[k1709.py:973–980](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:973)

  若 flow_t 到 t+1 日終才可用並預測 t+2，則 `RV_{t+1}`、`ret_{t+1}` 已知；只有 flow 應 lag 2，state controls 仍應 lag 1。現行 robustness 使用過時 HAR baseline。

  修法：拆成 `state_lag=1`、`flow_lag=pub_lag`，並分別驗證 `state_src_date`、`flow_src_date`。

基準 `pub_lag=1` 的週五 flow 正確映到週六，沒有被當成週日 lag-1，也沒有 weekend forward-fill。

### 3. OOS

未發現全樣本 leakage。[k1709.py:527–584](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:527) 每個 origin 都重新估 beta 與 training residual variance；h=5 嚴格要求 `y_end_date < forecast_origin`。沒有全樣本 scaler、參數或選變數。

另外，修改 cutoff 後的 RV/flow 不會改變 cutoff 前 forecast；26/26 tests 通過。

### 4. 統計推論

- [CRITICAL] QLIKE、raw DM 與 Clark-West 被混成同一個 NULL gate。[k1709.py:629–683](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:629)、[k1709.py:1161–1215](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:1161)

  DM 用的是 QLIKE loss；CW helper 實際計算 variance-level squared forecast errors。Clark-West 原始方法是 MSPE adjustment，不是 QLIKE general-loss test。[Clark–West 原文](https://www.nber.org/papers/t0326)

  因此 CW 公式與方向雖正確，卻只能標成 MSPE 證據，不能被稱為「QLIKE nested-correct」並單獨承載 NULL。這也直接重犯專案已記錄的 K1701 教訓：[error_log.md:511–536](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/docs/error_log.md:511)。

  修法：使用共同 complete-case、共同 training dates 的 fixed rolling window，再對 Patton QLIKE 跑 Giacomini–White；raw expanding DM 降為 diagnostic-only。CW 可保留為另一个 MSPE estimand。

- [CRITICAL] 現行 MDE 不是功效分析，也不能推出「沒有 ≥16% 效果」。[k1709.py:693–768](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:693)

  每個 β 只在同一條實際噪音路徑注入一次，再取第一個 crossing；沒有重複模擬、預設 80%/90% power、size 校準或 CI。結果還不單調：BTC CW 在 β=.15 時為 1.713，但 β=.8 時降回 1.623，[results.json:1948–2005](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709_results.json:1948)。所有 `detected_both` 也都是 false，和 docstring 宣稱的「BOTH gates」矛盾。

  此外 β=.15 的 BTC/ETH CW 單尾 p 約 0.043/0.031；光校正這兩格後 Holm p 就約 0.061，並未通過 5%。而 MDE 只算 H1、h=1，不能外推至 H2、h=5、H4。

  修法：若要正式 MDE，固定 seed，以 block/wild bootstrap 至少 1,000 次，每次完整重估 OOS path，按預先指定的整體 gate 計算 rejection rate。若要宣稱效果上界，必須另做預先指定 material margin 的一側 exclusion/equivalence test或反演信賴區間；power 本身不能證明效果小於 MDE。

- [MAJOR] 這不是 Harvey–Leybourne–Newbold modified DM。[model_evaluation.py:89–117](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/src/volpred/stats/model_evaluation.py:89)

  現行只有 HAC-DM，再套 Harvey–Liu–Zhu 2016 的 `|t|>3` heuristic；沒有 HLN finite-sample factor。單尾 p 又在 [k1709.py:672–674](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:672) 改用 normal CDF，與 helper 的 Student-t p 不一致。[HLN 原文](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0169207096007194)

  修法：實作 HLN modified-DM 並統一 p-value 分布，或誠實改名為「HAC-DM + t>3 gate」。本樣本 h=5 修正約 0.98–0.99，不會單獨翻轉結果。

- [MINOR] Holm 演算法本身正確，但 caller 使用已四捨五入至四位的 p，且所謂「EVERY DM test」漏掉 no/shared-smearing 的 8 個 DM tests。[k1709.py:773–781](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:773)、[k1709.py:1097–1119](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:1097)

  修法：保留 raw p 到最終 serialization，並由單一 test registry 產生完整 family。

### 5. NULL 誠實度

Flow 不是全零、全 NaN 或被 shift 掉：BTC/ETH 各有 642/504 筆，標準差約 342/146 百萬美元；面板 621/483 筆，OOS 371/233 筆。主 `verdict` 也確實由 OOS comparisons 而非 in-sample coefficient 產生。

但因上述正式推論錯誤，目前只能接受：

> 「在現有 expanding OOS 點估與診斷中，未發現 ETF flow 的穩健增量預測證據。」

不能接受：

> 「NULL 已成立」或「可排除每 1sd flow shock 帶來 ≥16% RV 效果」。

+Inference design (this is what v1 got wrong)
+--------------------------------------------
+`HAR+ctrl` and `HAR+ctrl+flow` are STRICTLY NESTED. Under the nested null the

+interpretation. v1 pushed an expanding-window QLIKE loss through a raw
+Diebold-Mariano statistic and then bolted on a Clark-West helper that actually

+
+What the claim actually rests on:
+  * Giacomini-White (2006) equal-unconditional-predictive-ability test on Patton
+    QLIKE, computed from a PAIRED FIXED ROLLING WINDOW. GW keeps estimation
+    uncertainty in the limiting experiment, which is exactly what makes it legal
+    for nested *methods* where the ordinary loss-difference statistic is not.
+  * A pre-specified one-sided MATERIAL-GAIN EXCLUSION test. Failing to reject
+    equal accuracy is not evidence of equality; only this test can license a
+    bounded null, and only in QLIKE-loss space.
+  * Clark-West is retained but reported strictly as evidence about a SEPARATE
+    MSPE estimand. It is never relabelled as a QLIKE general-loss test.
+
+Power is reported (>=1000 simulated OOS paths, size-calibrated) but it is a
+statement about the DESIGN, not about the effect: power cannot prove that the
+effect is smaller than the minimum detectable effect. v1 made exactly that
+inversion.
+
 Information set / lookahead
 ---------------------------
 Farside publishes day-t flows after the US close (~20:00-21:00 UTC on day t).

+# --- Giacomini-White design constants -------------------------------------
+# GW's asymptotics require BOUNDED estimator memory: the forecasting *method*
+# must re-estimate on a fixed-length rolling window, not an expanding one. 250
+# flow days (~1 trading year) is the longest window that still leaves ETH -- the
+# shorter series -- with enough origins for the test's n>=60 requirement.

+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+# 0. Test registry — the single source of the multiple-testing family (C6)
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+@dataclass
+class TestRecord:
+    """One statistical test. The family is DERIVED from this list, never hand-listed.
+
+    v1 hand-enumerated "EVERY DM test" and silently missed the 8 smearing tests.
+    Every test in this study now registers itself here, and the multiple-testing
+    families are built by filtering this registry.
+
+    `feeds_gate` is the honest bit: only tests whose inference is valid for a
+    nested comparison are allowed to reach the verdict. Raw Diebold-Mariano
+    records are registered with feeds_gate=False so they show up in the audit
+    trail without contaminating the claim.
+
+    `p_one_sided` is stored RAW (unrounded). v1 fed 4-decimal rounded p-values
+    into Holm.
+    """
+
+    family: str
+    cell: str
+    asset: str
+    horizon: int
+    base: str
+    alt: str
+    inference: str
+    estimand: str
+    scheme: str
+    stat: float
+    p_one_sided: float
+    feeds_gate: bool
+    qlike_improve_pct: float | None = None
+    n: int | None = None
+
+
+REGISTRY: list[TestRecord] = []
+
+# Paired QLIKE loss streams, keyed by cell. Kept so a confidence bound can be
+# re-inverted at any alpha AFTER the family size is known (the Bonferroni level
+# depends on how many cells there turned out to be). Never serialized.
+LOSS_CACHE: dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, int]] = {}
+
+
+def register(rec: TestRecord) -> TestRecord:
+    REGISTRY.append(rec)
+    return rec
+
+
+def qlike_gain_upper_bound_from_cell(cell: str, alpha: float) -> float | None:
+    la, lb, h = LOSS_CACHE[cell]
+    return qlike_gain_upper_bound(la, lb, h, alpha=alpha)
+
+
+def holm(pvals: list[float]) -> list[float]:
+    """Holm step-down. Takes RAW p-values; rounding happens only at serialization."""
+    m = len(pvals)
+    if m == 0:
+        return []
+    order = np.argsort(pvals)
+    adj = np.empty(m)
+    running = 0.0
+    for rank, idx in enumerate(order):
+        running = max(running, (m - rank) * pvals[idx])
+        adj[idx] = min(running, 1.0)
+    return [float(a) for a in adj]
+
+
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+# 1. US equity session calendar (C3)
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+def us_equity_sessions(start: pd.Timestamp, end: pd.Timestamp) -> pd.DatetimeIndex:
+    """NYSE session days over [start, end].
+
+    Farside keeps a row for US market holidays with every fund column showing a
+    dash and `Total` showing 0.0. `sum(skipna=True)` over an all-NaN row is 0.0,
+    so the parser cross-check cannot see it either: the holiday looks exactly
+    like a genuine zero-flow day. Those fake zeros then enter the 20-flow-day
+    rolling sd that scales every shock.
+
+    A "flow day" is by construction a day the ETFs could trade, i.e. an NYSE
+    session. Anything else is MISSING, not zero.
+    """
+    cal = xcals.get_calendar("XNYS")
+    sess = cal.sessions_in_range(
+        pd.Timestamp(start).normalize(), pd.Timestamp(end).normalize()
+    )
+    idx = pd.DatetimeIndex(sess)
+    if idx.tz is not None:
+        idx = idx.tz_convert("UTC").tz_localize(None)
+    return idx.normalize()
+
+
+def filter_to_sessions(df: pd.DataFrame) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, dict]:
+    """Keep only NYSE session rows; report exactly what was dropped.
+
+    Split out of `fetch_flows` so it can be tested without hitting the network:
+    a data-integrity rule that only runs behind an HTTP call is a rule nobody
+    ever tests.
+    """
+    sessions = us_equity_sessions(df.index.min(), df.index.max())
+    is_session = df.index.isin(sessions)
+    dropped = df.index[~is_session]
+    detail = [
+        {
+            "date": str(d.date()),
+            "weekday": d.day_name(),
+            "farside_total_musd": float(df.loc[d, "flow"]),
+            "all_fund_columns_dash": (
+                bool(df.loc[d, "all_dash"]) if "all_dash" in df.columns else None
+            ),
+        }
+        for d in dropped
+    ]
+    diag = {
+        "n_nonsession_rows_dropped": len(detail),
+        "n_nonsession_rows_with_nonzero_total": int(
+            (df.loc[~is_session, "flow"].abs() > 1e-9).sum()
+        ),
+        "nonsession_rows_dropped": detail,
+    }
+    keep = df[is_session]
+    if "all_dash" in keep.columns:
+        keep = keep.drop(columns=["all_dash"])
+    return keep, diag

# K1709 — Codex 獨立審查（2026-07-14 12:23 台灣時間）

**審查者**: Codex CLI (gpt-5.6-sol, ultra reasoning) via `scripts/codex_exec_bounded.sh`
**受審對象**: `experiments/k1709/k1709.py` (worktree `dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709`, commit 9be94342d)
**結論**: **FAIL** — 兩個 CRITICAL 打掉招牌宣稱；不得寫入 knowledge.json，不得發文。

## Codex 的核心判決（原文）

> 可以接受：「在現有 expanding OOS 點估與診斷中，未發現 ETF flow 的穩健增量預測證據。」
>
> **不能**接受：「NULL 已成立」或「可排除每 1sd flow shock 帶來 ≥16% RV 效果」。

## 交叉驗證：專案自己的機械 gate 同意 Codex

`scripts/tests/test_nested_dm_misuse_ratchet.py::test_no_new_nested_raw_dm_sites` 對本檔 **FAIL**，
訊息與 Codex CRITICAL #1 完全一致（巢狀模型不得用 raw DM 承載 verdict；CW 只修正 MSPE，不能貼標成 QLIKE general-loss inference）。
K1709 重犯 2026-07-13 05:47 才修好的 K1701 教訓（見 docs/error_log.md）。

## 完整審查輸出

```

  它只檢查同一段程式人工 `.shift(pub_lag)` 出來的 `src_date`，無法證明各 predictor 的真實 provenance。我把同列 `y` 直接複製進 `z`，assert 仍通過；刪掉一個 Sunday 時，違規列也可能先被 `dropna(z)` 刪掉，h=5 的五列 rolling target 會跨成六個日曆日而 assert 仍通過。

  具體修法：在任何 rolling/shift 前檢查 index 完整、排序、無重複且等於完整 daily range；分開保存每組 predictor 的 source date；要求 `y_end_date == target_date + horizon - 1`；加入 future-mutation 與「刪 Sunday」測試。

實際主規格仍是乾淨的：rolling SD 在 [k1709.py:324–371](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:324) 有 `.shift(1)`；forward target 在 [k1709.py:426–430](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:426) 正確對應 `[τ, τ+h−1]`，沒有 target 混入 X。

### 2. 日曆對齊

- [MAJOR] 休市日 `Total=0.0` 被誤當成真實 flow day。[k1709.py:138–167](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:138)

  現行 Farside 資料中，BTC 有 16 個、ETH 有 10 個美股非交易日被保留為零 flow，包括 MLK、Good Friday、Memorial Day、Juneteenth、Thanksgiving、Christmas。`sum(skipna=True)` 會把全為 dash/NaN 的基金欄加成 0，因此 parser cross-check 也抓不到；這些休市日還會進入「20 flow-day」rolling SD。

  修法：在標準化前用 US equity session calendar 過濾非交易日，休市列記為 missing；只保留交易日上的 genuine zero，並記錄 dropped dates/count，再完整重跑。我的 session-only 診斷未翻轉主結論，但統計量確實有位移，例如 BTC h=1 H1 的 DM 由 0.140 變 1.542。

- [MAJOR] `pub_lag=2` 穩健性把 HAR/return controls 也錯誤 lag 2。[k1709.py:415–424](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:415)、[k1709.py:973–980](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:973)

  若 flow_t 到 t+1 日終才可用並預測 t+2，則 `RV_{t+1}`、`ret_{t+1}` 已知；只有 flow 應 lag 2，state controls 仍應 lag 1。現行 robustness 使用過時 HAR baseline。

  修法：拆成 `state_lag=1`、`flow_lag=pub_lag`，並分別驗證 `state_src_date`、`flow_src_date`。

基準 `pub_lag=1` 的週五 flow 正確映到週六，沒有被當成週日 lag-1，也沒有 weekend forward-fill。

### 3. OOS

未發現全樣本 leakage。[k1709.py:527–584](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:527) 每個 origin 都重新估 beta 與 training residual variance；h=5 嚴格要求 `y_end_date < forecast_origin`。沒有全樣本 scaler、參數或選變數。

另外，修改 cutoff 後的 RV/flow 不會改變 cutoff 前 forecast；26/26 tests 通過。

### 4. 統計推論

- [CRITICAL] QLIKE、raw DM 與 Clark-West 被混成同一個 NULL gate。[k1709.py:629–683](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:629)、[k1709.py:1161–1215](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:1161)

  DM 用的是 QLIKE loss；CW helper 實際計算 variance-level squared forecast errors。Clark-West 原始方法是 MSPE adjustment，不是 QLIKE general-loss test。[Clark–West 原文](https://www.nber.org/papers/t0326)

  因此 CW 公式與方向雖正確，卻只能標成 MSPE 證據，不能被稱為「QLIKE nested-correct」並單獨承載 NULL。這也直接重犯專案已記錄的 K1701 教訓：[error_log.md:511–536](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/docs/error_log.md:511)。

  修法：使用共同 complete-case、共同 training dates 的 fixed rolling window，再對 Patton QLIKE 跑 Giacomini–White；raw expanding DM 降為 diagnostic-only。CW 可保留為另一个 MSPE estimand。

- [CRITICAL] 現行 MDE 不是功效分析，也不能推出「沒有 ≥16% 效果」。[k1709.py:693–768](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:693)

  每個 β 只在同一條實際噪音路徑注入一次，再取第一個 crossing；沒有重複模擬、預設 80%/90% power、size 校準或 CI。結果還不單調：BTC CW 在 β=.15 時為 1.713，但 β=.8 時降回 1.623，[results.json:1948–2005](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709_results.json:1948)。所有 `detected_both` 也都是 false，和 docstring 宣稱的「BOTH gates」矛盾。

  此外 β=.15 的 BTC/ETH CW 單尾 p 約 0.043/0.031；光校正這兩格後 Holm p 就約 0.061，並未通過 5%。而 MDE 只算 H1、h=1，不能外推至 H2、h=5、H4。

  修法：若要正式 MDE，固定 seed，以 block/wild bootstrap 至少 1,000 次，每次完整重估 OOS path，按預先指定的整體 gate 計算 rejection rate。若要宣稱效果上界，必須另做預先指定 material margin 的一側 exclusion/equivalence test或反演信賴區間；power 本身不能證明效果小於 MDE。

- [MAJOR] 這不是 Harvey–Leybourne–Newbold modified DM。[model_evaluation.py:89–117](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/src/volpred/stats/model_evaluation.py:89)

  現行只有 HAC-DM，再套 Harvey–Liu–Zhu 2016 的 `|t|>3` heuristic；沒有 HLN finite-sample factor。單尾 p 又在 [k1709.py:672–674](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:672) 改用 normal CDF，與 helper 的 Student-t p 不一致。[HLN 原文](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0169207096007194)

  修法：實作 HLN modified-DM 並統一 p-value 分布，或誠實改名為「HAC-DM + t>3 gate」。本樣本 h=5 修正約 0.98–0.99，不會單獨翻轉結果。

- [MINOR] Holm 演算法本身正確，但 caller 使用已四捨五入至四位的 p，且所謂「EVERY DM test」漏掉 no/shared-smearing 的 8 個 DM tests。[k1709.py:773–781](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:773)、[k1709.py:1097–1119](/Users/yhlai0911/volpred-research/.claude/worktrees/dispatch-slot-2-c873d04d-k1709/experiments/k1709/k1709.py:1097)

  修法：保留 raw p 到最終 serialization，並由單一 test registry 產生完整 family。

### 5. NULL 誠實度

Flow 不是全零、全 NaN 或被 shift 掉：BTC/ETH 各有 642/504 筆，標準差約 342/146 百萬美元；面板 621/483 筆，OOS 371/233 筆。主 `verdict` 也確實由 OOS comparisons 而非 in-sample coefficient 產生。

但因上述正式推論錯誤，目前只能接受：

> 「在現有 expanding OOS 點估與診斷中，未發現 ETF flow 的穩健增量預測證據。」

不能接受：

> 「NULL 已成立」或「可排除每 1sd flow shock 帶來 ≥16% RV 效果」。

     1	"""K1709-rev — Spot BTC/ETH ETF net flow shocks and next-day realized volatility.
     2	
     3	Research question
     4	-----------------
     5	Do daily *net creation/redemption flows* of spot Bitcoin/Ethereum ETFs carry
     6	incremental predictive content for BTC/ETH realized volatility at t+1 and t+5,
     7	once the standard HAR-RV volatility dynamics are controlled for?
     8	
     9	Orthogonality declaration
    10	-------------------------
    11	This is NOT the "ETF-ization changed the trading clock / session structure"
    12	line of work. The treatment here is the *flow* itself (dollar creations minus
    13	redemptions), not the calendar/microstructure of ETF trading hours.
    14	
    15	Inference design (this is what v1 got wrong)
    16	--------------------------------------------
    17	`HAR+ctrl` and `HAR+ctrl+flow` are STRICTLY NESTED. Under the nested null the
    18	augmented method's extra estimated coefficients inject pure forecast noise, so
    19	an ordinary loss-difference statistic does not have its usual equal-accuracy
    20	interpretation. v1 pushed an expanding-window QLIKE loss through a raw
    21	Diebold-Mariano statistic and then bolted on a Clark-West helper that actually
    22	scores *variance-level squared error* -- three different estimands fused into a
    23	single NULL gate. See `codex_review_20260714.md` and the K1701 lesson in
    24	`docs/error_log.md` (2026-07-13 05:47).
    25	
    26	  nested-dm: diagnostic-only  -- every raw Diebold-Mariano number in this file is
    27	  descriptive and is tagged `feeds_gate=False`. None of them reaches a verdict.
    28	
    29	What the claim actually rests on:
    30	  * Giacomini-White (2006) equal-unconditional-predictive-ability test on Patton
    31	    QLIKE, computed from a PAIRED FIXED ROLLING WINDOW. GW keeps estimation
    32	    uncertainty in the limiting experiment, which is exactly what makes it legal
    33	    for nested *methods* where the ordinary loss-difference statistic is not.
    34	  * A pre-specified one-sided MATERIAL-GAIN EXCLUSION test. Failing to reject
    35	    equal accuracy is not evidence of equality; only this test can license a
    36	    bounded null, and only in QLIKE-loss space.
    37	  * Clark-West is retained but reported strictly as evidence about a SEPARATE
    38	    MSPE estimand. It is never relabelled as a QLIKE general-loss test.
    39	
    40	Power is reported (>=1000 simulated OOS paths, size-calibrated) but it is a
    41	statement about the DESIGN, not about the effect: power cannot prove that the
    42	effect is smaller than the minimum detectable effect. v1 made exactly that
    43	inversion.
    44	
    45	Information set / lookahead
    46	---------------------------
    47	Farside publishes day-t flows after the US close (~20:00-21:00 UTC on day t).
    48	We therefore set the forecast origin at 00:00 UTC ending calendar day t:
    49	    - flow_t          known (published ~21:00 UTC, i.e. before 24:00 UTC)
    50	    - RV_t (UTC day)  known (the UTC day just closed)
    51	    - target RV_{t+1} lies entirely in the future
    52	State controls and the flow carry SEPARATE lags (`state_lag`, `flow_lag`): if a
    53	flow only becomes usable one day later, that does not un-observe yesterday's
    54	realized volatility.
    55	
    56	Run:  uv run python experiments/k1709/k1709.py
    57	"""

   134	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   135	# 0. Test registry — the single source of the multiple-testing family (C6)
   136	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   137	@dataclass
   138	class TestRecord:
   139	    """One statistical test. The family is DERIVED from this list, never hand-listed.
   140	
   141	    v1 hand-enumerated "EVERY DM test" and silently missed the 8 smearing tests.
   142	    Every test in this study now registers itself here, and the multiple-testing
   143	    families are built by filtering this registry.
   144	
   145	    `feeds_gate` is the honest bit: only tests whose inference is valid for a
   146	    nested comparison are allowed to reach the verdict. Raw Diebold-Mariano
   147	    records are registered with feeds_gate=False so they show up in the audit
   148	    trail without contaminating the claim.
   149	
   150	    `p_one_sided` is stored RAW (unrounded). v1 fed 4-decimal rounded p-values
   151	    into Holm.
   152	    """
   153	
   154	    family: str
   155	    cell: str
   156	    asset: str
   157	    horizon: int
   158	    base: str
   159	    alt: str
   160	    inference: str
   161	    estimand: str
   162	    scheme: str
   163	    stat: float
   164	    p_one_sided: float
   165	    feeds_gate: bool
   166	    qlike_improve_pct: float | None = None
   167	    n: int | None = None
   168	
   169	
   170	REGISTRY: list[TestRecord] = []
   171	
   172	# Paired QLIKE loss streams, keyed by cell. Kept so a confidence bound can be
   173	# re-inverted at any alpha AFTER the family size is known (the Bonferroni level
   174	# depends on how many cells there turned out to be). Never serialized.
   175	LOSS_CACHE: dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, int]] = {}
   176	
   177	
   178	def register(rec: TestRecord) -> TestRecord:
   179	    REGISTRY.append(rec)
   180	    return rec
   181	
   182	
   183	def qlike_gain_upper_bound_from_cell(cell: str, alpha: float) -> float | None:
   184	    la, lb, h = LOSS_CACHE[cell]
   185	    return qlike_gain_upper_bound(la, lb, h, alpha=alpha)
   186	
   187	
   188	def holm(pvals: list[float]) -> list[float]:
   189	    """Holm step-down. Takes RAW p-values; rounding happens only at serialization."""
   190	    m = len(pvals)
   191	    if m == 0:
   192	        return []
   193	    order = np.argsort(pvals)
   194	    adj = np.empty(m)
   195	    running = 0.0
   196	    for rank, idx in enumerate(order):
   197	        running = max(running, (m - rank) * pvals[idx])
   198	        adj[idx] = min(running, 1.0)
   199	    return [float(a) for a in adj]
   200	
   201	
   202	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   203	# 1. US equity session calendar (C3)
   204	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   205	def us_equity_sessions(start: pd.Timestamp, end: pd.Timestamp) -> pd.DatetimeIndex:
   206	    """NYSE session days over [start, end].
   207	
   208	    Farside keeps a row for US market holidays with every fund column showing a
   209	    dash and `Total` showing 0.0. `sum(skipna=True)` over an all-NaN row is 0.0,
   210	    so the parser cross-check cannot see it either: the holiday looks exactly
   211	    like a genuine zero-flow day. Those fake zeros then enter the 20-flow-day
   212	    rolling sd that scales every shock.
   213	
   214	    A "flow day" is by construction a day the ETFs could trade, i.e. an NYSE
   215	    session. Anything else is MISSING, not zero.
   216	    """
   217	    cal = xcals.get_calendar("XNYS")
   218	    sess = cal.sessions_in_range(
   219	        pd.Timestamp(start).normalize(), pd.Timestamp(end).normalize()
   220	    )
   221	    idx = pd.DatetimeIndex(sess)
   222	    if idx.tz is not None:
   223	        idx = idx.tz_convert("UTC").tz_localize(None)
   224	    return idx.normalize()
   225	
   226	
   227	def filter_to_sessions(df: pd.DataFrame) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, dict]:
   228	    """Keep only NYSE session rows; report exactly what was dropped.
   229	
   230	    Split out of `fetch_flows` so it can be tested without hitting the network:
   231	    a data-integrity rule that only runs behind an HTTP call is a rule nobody
   232	    ever tests.
   233	    """
   234	    sessions = us_equity_sessions(df.index.min(), df.index.max())
   235	    is_session = df.index.isin(sessions)
   236	    dropped = df.index[~is_session]
   237	    detail = [
   238	        {
   239	            "date": str(d.date()),
   240	            "weekday": d.day_name(),
   241	            "farside_total_musd": float(df.loc[d, "flow"]),
   242	            "all_fund_columns_dash": (
   243	                bool(df.loc[d, "all_dash"]) if "all_dash" in df.columns else None
   244	            ),
   245	        }
   246	        for d in dropped
   247	    ]
   248	    diag = {
   249	        "n_nonsession_rows_dropped": len(detail),
   250	        "n_nonsession_rows_with_nonzero_total": int(
   251	            (df.loc[~is_session, "flow"].abs() > 1e-9).sum()
   252	        ),
   253	        "nonsession_rows_dropped": detail,
   254	    }
   255	    keep = df[is_session]
   256	    if "all_dash" in keep.columns:
   257	        keep = keep.drop(columns=["all_dash"])
   258	    return keep, diag
   259	
   260	
   261	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   262	# 2. Farside flow parsing (the traps live here)
   263	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   264	def _parse_money(x) -> float:
   265	    """Farside cell -> float ($M).
   266	
   267	    Traps handled:
   268	      '(95.1)'  -> -95.1      negative flows are written in parentheses
   269	      '(27,332)'-> -27332.0   thousands separators
   270	      '-' / '–' -> NaN        fund not trading / no data  (NOT zero)
   271	      '0.0'     -> 0.0        a genuine zero flow  (NOT missing)
   272	    """
   273	    if x is None or (isinstance(x, float) and np.isnan(x)):
   274	        return np.nan
   275	    s = str(x).strip()
   276	    # em/en dash and bare hyphen mean "no data", not zero.
   277	    if s in {"", "-", "–", "—", "nan", "NaN"}:
   278	        return np.nan
   279	    neg = s.startswith("(") and s.endswith(")")
   280	    s = s.strip("()").replace(",", "").replace("$", "").replace("*", "")
   281	    try:
   282	        v = float(s)
   283	    except ValueError:
   284	        return np.nan
   285	    return -v if neg else v
   286	
   287	
   288	def fetch_flows(asset: str) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, dict]:
   289	    """Download and parse the Farside all-data flow table for `asset`."""
   290	    resp = requests.get(FARSIDE[asset], headers=UA, timeout=60)
   291	    resp.raise_for_status()
   292	    tables = pd.read_html(io.StringIO(resp.text))
   293	    raw = max(tables, key=lambda t: t.shape[0] * t.shape[1])
   294	    n_raw = len(raw)
   295	
   296	    # ETH ships a MultiIndex header (issuer / ticker / fee). Keep the ticker row.
   297	    if isinstance(raw.columns, pd.MultiIndex):
   298	        raw.columns = [
   299	            c[1] if not str(c[1]).startswith("Unnamed") else c[0] for c in raw.columns
   300	        ]
   301	    raw.columns = [str(c).strip() for c in raw.columns]
   302	    date_col = raw.columns[0]
   303	    raw = raw.rename(columns={date_col: "Date"})
   304	
   305	    # Drop the 'Total' footer row and the 'Seed' row (seed capital, not daily flow).
   306	    lbl = raw["Date"].astype(str).str.strip()
   307	    n_total_rows = int(lbl.str.fullmatch("Total", case=False).sum())
   308	    n_seed_rows = int(lbl.str.fullmatch("Seed", case=False).sum())
   309	    raw = raw[~lbl.str.fullmatch("Total|Seed", case=False)]
   310	
   311	    dt = pd.to_datetime(raw["Date"], format="%d %b %Y", errors="coerce")
   312	    n_unparsed = int(dt.isna().sum())
   313	    raw = raw.assign(date=dt).dropna(subset=["date"])
   314	
   315	    fund_cols = [c for c in raw.columns if c not in {"Date", "date", "Total"}]
   316	    parsed = raw[fund_cols].map(_parse_money)
   317	    total = raw["Total"].map(_parse_money)
   318	
   319	    # Parser self-check: Farside's own Total must equal the sum of the fund
   320	    # columns (NaN = fund not trading = contributes nothing). A mismatch means
   321	    # the paren/comma/dash handling above is wrong -- fail loud, do not proceed.
   322	    #
   323	    # NOTE this check is BLIND to the market-holiday trap: an all-dash row sums
   324	    # to 0.0 and Farside's own Total also reads 0.0, so the residual is 0. That
   325	    # is precisely why the session-calendar filter below is a separate layer.
   326	    recomputed = parsed.sum(axis=1, skipna=True)
   327	    resid = (total - recomputed).abs()
   328	    max_resid = float(resid.max())
   329	    if max_resid > 1.0:  # $1M tolerance for Farside's own rounding
   330	        worst = resid.idxmax()
   331	        raise AssertionError(
   332	            f"[{asset}] flow parser failed cross-check: max |Total - sum(funds)| = "
   333	            f"{max_resid:.3f} $M on {raw.loc[worst, 'date'].date()}"
   334	        )
   335	
   336	    # Gross churn = sum of |fund-level flow|. Net flow can be ~0 while one issuer
   337	    # creates and another redeems heavily -- that is a real liquidity event that
   338	    # the NET series is blind to. Keeping it lets us test whether the null is an
   339	    # artifact of netting (Warther 1995: only flow INNOVATIONS should inform).
   340	    gross = parsed.abs().sum(axis=1, skipna=True)
   341	    all_dash = parsed.isna().all(axis=1)
   342	
   343	    df = pd.DataFrame(
   344	        {"flow": total.values, "gross": gross.values, "all_dash": all_dash.values},
   345	        index=pd.DatetimeIndex(raw["date"].values),
   346	    )
   347	    df = df.sort_index()
   348	    df = df[~df.index.duplicated(keep="last")]
   349	    df = df.dropna(subset=["flow"])
   350	
   351	    df, session_diag = filter_to_sessions(df)
   352	    dropped_detail = session_diag["nonsession_rows_dropped"]
   353	    n_dropped_nonzero = session_diag["n_nonsession_rows_with_nonzero_total"]
   354	
   355	    diag = {
   356	        "source_url": FARSIDE[asset],
   357	        "raw_rows": n_raw,
   358	        "dropped_total_rows": n_total_rows,
   359	        "dropped_seed_rows": n_seed_rows,
   360	        "dropped_unparseable_dates": n_unparsed,
   361	        "session_calendar": "XNYS (exchange_calendars)",
   362	        "n_nonsession_rows_dropped": len(dropped_detail),
   363	        "n_nonsession_rows_with_nonzero_total": n_dropped_nonzero,
   364	        "nonsession_rows_dropped": dropped_detail,
   365	        "session_filter_note": (
   366	            "Farside emits a row for every US market holiday with all fund "
   367	            "columns dashed and Total = 0.0. skipna summation makes the parser "
   368	            "cross-check read 0.0 - 0.0 = 0, so it cannot detect them. These are "
   369	            "MISSING flow days, not genuine zero-flow days, and they were "
   370	            "polluting the 20-flow-day rolling sd used to scale every shock."
   371	        ),
   372	        "n_obs": int(len(df)),
   373	        "date_min": str(df.index.min().date()),
   374	        "date_max": str(df.index.max().date()),
   375	        "parser_crosscheck_max_abs_resid_musd": round(max_resid, 4),
   376	        "total_flow_musd": {
   377	            "mean": round(float(df["flow"].mean()), 3),
   378	            "std": round(float(df["flow"].std()), 3),
   379	            "min": round(float(df["flow"].min()), 3),
   380	            "max": round(float(df["flow"].max()), 3),
   381	            "median": round(float(df["flow"].median()), 3),
   382	        },
   383	        "share_negative": round(float((df["flow"] < 0).mean()), 4),
   384	        "share_exact_zero_on_session_days": round(float((df["flow"] == 0).mean()), 4),
   385	        "n_fund_columns": len(fund_cols),
   386	    }
   387	    return df, diag
   388	
   389

   390	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   391	# 3. Realized volatility proxies
   392	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   393	def fetch_rv(asset: str) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, dict]:
   394	    """Calendar-daily (UTC) RV proxies for `asset`.
   395	
   396	    Primary  : Garman-Klass variance from OHLC (spans the full sample).
   397	    Robust 1 : true realized variance from 24 hourly log returns (2024-07-15+).
   398	    Robust 2 : squared daily close-to-close log return.
   399	
   400	    Note on the hourly RV: for a 24/7 market there is no session gap, so the
   401	    return spanning the UTC midnight boundary is a genuine return, not an
   402	    overnight jump. We therefore diff the *continuous* hourly series and assign
   403	    each return to the UTC date of its end stamp (24 returns/day). This is the
   404	    opposite of the 2026-07-11 0050.TW fix, and deliberately so: that market
   405	    closes overnight, this one does not. Days without exactly 24 returns are
   406	    dropped to avoid a scaling bias.
   407	    """
   408	    px = yf.download(
   409	        TICKER[asset], start="2023-06-01", interval="1d", progress=False, auto_adjust=False
   410	    )
   411	    if isinstance(px.columns, pd.MultiIndex):
   412	        px.columns = px.columns.get_level_values(0)
   413	    px = px[["Open", "High", "Low", "Close"]].dropna()
   414	    px.index = pd.DatetimeIndex(px.index).tz_localize(None).normalize()
   415	
   416	    # (a) Drop the still-open current UTC day. Yahoo serves a PARTIAL bar for
   417	    #     today, whose High/Low have not finished widening -- it systematically
   418	    #     understates GK variance. Only fully-closed UTC days may enter.
   419	    today_utc = pd.Timestamp.utcnow().tz_localize(None).normalize()
   420	    n_open_bar = int((px.index >= today_utc).sum())
   421	    px = px[px.index < today_utc]
   422	
   423	    # (b) Reindex onto a COMPLETE daily calendar. BTC/ETH trade 24/7, so every
   424	    #     calendar day should exist -- but Yahoo does drop days (2026-07-13 was
   425	    #     missing at the time of writing). Without this, `.shift(1)` would shift
   426	    #     by ROW POSITION, not by one calendar day, and a gap would silently turn
   427	    #     a t+1 target into a t+2 target. Missing days become NaN and are dropped
   428	    #     from the panel, instead of compressing the timeline.
   429	    full = pd.date_range(px.index.min(), px.index.max(), freq="D")
   430	    n_missing = int(len(full.difference(px.index)))
   431	    missing_days = [str(d.date()) for d in full.difference(px.index)]
   432	    px = px.reindex(full)
   433	
   434	    ln_hl = np.log(px["High"] / px["Low"])
   435	    ln_co = np.log(px["Close"] / px["Open"])
   436	    gk = 0.5 * ln_hl**2 - (2 * np.log(2) - 1) * ln_co**2
   437	    park = ln_hl**2 / (4 * np.log(2))
   438	    ret = np.log(px["Close"]).diff()
   439	
   440	    out = pd.DataFrame(
   441	        {"rv_gk": gk, "rv_park": park, "rv_r2": ret**2, "ret": ret}, index=px.index
   442	    )
   443	
   444	    # --- hourly true RV -----------------------------------------------------
   445	    hr = yf.download(
   446	        TICKER[asset], interval="1h", period="730d", progress=False, auto_adjust=False
   447	    )
   448	    n_hourly_days = 0
   449	    if len(hr):
   450	        if isinstance(hr.columns, pd.MultiIndex):
   451	            hr.columns = hr.columns.get_level_values(0)
   452	        close = hr["Close"].dropna().sort_index()
   453	        r = np.log(close).diff().dropna()               # continuous: 24/7, no gap
   454	        day = pd.DatetimeIndex(r.index).tz_convert("UTC").tz_localize(None).normalize()
   455	        grp = pd.DataFrame({"r2": r.values**2, "day": day}).groupby("day")
   456	        rv_h = grp["r2"].sum()
   457	        cnt = grp["r2"].size()
   458	        rv_h = rv_h[cnt == 24]                          # complete UTC days only
   459	        n_hourly_days = int(len(rv_h))
   460	        out["rv_hourly"] = rv_h.reindex(out.index)
   461	    else:
   462	        out["rv_hourly"] = np.nan
   463	
   464	    neg_gk = int((out["rv_gk"] < 0).sum())
   465	    # QLIKE has a -log(actual) term, so a near-zero "actual" blows it up. Track
   466	    # how many observations each proxy floors: r^2 is a chi^2(1) proxy and hits
   467	    # near-zero on quiet days, which is one reason it is a NOISY variance target
   468	    # and is reported only as robustness, never as the headline.
   469	    clipped = {c: int((out[c] < EPS).sum()) for c in ("rv_gk", "rv_park", "rv_r2", "rv_hourly")}
   470	    for c in ("rv_gk", "rv_park", "rv_r2", "rv_hourly"):
   471	        out[c] = out[c].clip(lower=EPS)
   472	
   473	    diag = {
   474	        "ticker": TICKER[asset],
   475	        "n_daily_obs": int(len(out)),
   476	        "date_min": str(out.index.min().date()),
   477	        "date_max": str(out.index.max().date()),
   478	        "n_negative_gk_clipped": neg_gk,
   479	        "n_open_partial_bars_dropped": n_open_bar,
   480	        "n_missing_calendar_days_reindexed": n_missing,
   481	        "missing_calendar_days": missing_days,
   482	        "n_obs_floored_at_eps_by_proxy": clipped,
   483	        "qlike_noise_note": (
   484	            "r^2 is a chi^2(1) variance proxy: its QLIKE level runs ~7x that of "
   485	            "hourly RV, so it is reported as robustness only."
   486	        ),
   487	        "n_hourly_complete_days": n_hourly_days,
   488	        "hourly_coverage_start": (
   489	            str(out["rv_hourly"].dropna().index.min().date())
   490	            if out["rv_hourly"].notna().any()
   491	            else None
   492	        ),
   493	        "ann_vol_gk_pct": round(float(np.sqrt(out["rv_gk"].mean() * 365) * 100), 2),
   494	        "corr_gk_vs_hourly": (
   495	            round(
   496	                float(np.log(out[["rv_gk", "rv_hourly"]].dropna()).corr().iloc[0, 1]), 4
   497	            )
   498	            if out["rv_hourly"].notna().sum() > 30
   499	            else None
   500	        ),
   501	    }
   502	    return out, diag
   503	
   504	
   505	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   506	# 4. Flow features — computed ONCE, reused by every panel
   507	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   508	@dataclass
   509	class FlowFeatures:
   510	    """Flow-derived regressors, indexed by FLOW DAY (= NYSE session).
   511	
   512	    These depend only on the flow series, never on RV, so they are computed once
   513	    and reused. That matters for the power simulation, which rebuilds the RV side
   514	    thousands of times: recomputing the rolling AR(5) each rep would dominate the
   515	    runtime and, worse, tempt a divergent panel-construction path.
   516	    """
   517	
   518	    flow: pd.Series
   519	    z: pd.Series
   520	    z_unexp: pd.Series
   521	    z_gross: pd.Series | None = None
   522	
   523	
   524	def flow_zscore(flow: pd.Series) -> pd.Series:
   525	    """Scale each flow by the sd of the previous Z_WINDOW *flow days*.
   526	
   527	    `.shift(1)` on the rolling sd keeps the scaler strictly backward-looking:
   528	    z_t = flow_t / sd(flow_{t-20..t-1}), so nothing on day t enters its own
   529	    denominator. The window is indexed by flow days (weekends and US market
   530	    holidays carry no flow), which is why this is computed BEFORE reindexing
   531	    onto the 24/7 crypto calendar.
   532	    """
   533	    sd_prior = flow.rolling(Z_WINDOW).std().shift(1)
   534	    return flow / sd_prior
   535	
   536	
   537	def ar_residual(flow: pd.Series, p: int = 5) -> pd.Series:
   538	    """Rolling AR(p) forecast error of `flow` -- the 'surprise' in each day's flow.
   539	
   540	    The AR is refitted every day on a STRICTLY PRIOR window (targets f[p:i], so
   541	    day i's own value never enters its own fit). The design matrix columns are
   542	    ordered [lag1, lag2, ..., lagp], so the prediction vector must be
   543	    f[i-1], f[i-2], ..., f[i-p] -- the reversed slice below produces exactly
   544	    that. Getting this ordering wrong silently multiplies the lag-p coefficient
   545	    by the lag-1 value; `test_unexpected_flow_ar5_lag_ordering` guards it.
   546	    """
   547	    f = flow.to_numpy(float)
   548	    n = len(f)
   549	    resid = np.full(n, np.nan)
   550	    for i in range(Z_WINDOW + p, n):
   551	        y = f[p:i]                                   # targets strictly before i
   552	        X = np.column_stack([f[p - k : i - k] for k in range(1, p + 1)])
   553	        if len(y) < 40:
   554	            continue
   555	        Xc = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(y)), X])
   556	        beta = np.linalg.lstsq(Xc, y, rcond=None)[0]
   557	        lags = f[i - 1 : i - p - 1 : -1] if i - p - 1 >= 0 else f[i - 1 :: -1][:p]
   558	        resid[i] = f[i] - float(np.r_[1.0, lags] @ beta)   # surprise on day i
   559	    return pd.Series(resid, index=flow.index)
   560	
   561	
   562	def make_flow_features(flow: pd.DataFrame) -> FlowFeatures:
   563	    """Warther (1995): only the UNEXPECTED component of flow should carry news.
   564	
   565	    Flow is strongly autocorrelated, so raw net flow is largely predictable from
   566	    its own past. We strip that out with the rolling AR(5) and z-score the
   567	    residual by its own strictly-prior rolling sd. If our null were merely an
   568	    artifact of feeding the model the *predictable* part of flow, this variable
   569	    would rescue it.
   570	    """
   571	    r = ar_residual(flow["flow"], p=5)
   572	    return FlowFeatures(
   573	        flow=flow["flow"],
   574	        z=flow_zscore(flow["flow"]),
   575	        z_unexp=r / r.rolling(Z_WINDOW).std().shift(1),
   576	        z_gross=flow_zscore(flow["gross"]) if "gross" in flow.columns else None,
   577	    )
   578	
   579	
   580	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   581	# 5. Panel construction — the alignment layer
   582	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   583	@dataclass
   584	class Panel:
   585	    df: pd.DataFrame           # indexed by TARGET date tau
   586	    rv_col: str
   587	    horizon: int
   588	    asset: str
   589	    state_lag: int = STATE_LAG
   590	    flow_lag: int = 1
   591	
   592	
   593	STATE_COLS = ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret"]
   594	FLOW_COLS = ["flow", "z", "z_unexp", "z_gross", "z_btc"]
   595	
   596	
   597	def assert_calendar_is_complete(idx: pd.DatetimeIndex, label: str) -> None:
   598	    """Guard the assumption every `.shift()` in this file relies on.
   599	
   600	    `.shift(k)` moves by ROW POSITION. It equals "k calendar days back" only if
   601	    the index is a complete, sorted, duplicate-free daily range. Codex's review
   602	    showed `assert_no_lookahead` alone could not prove this: it re-checks the
   603	    same src_date the shift itself produced. This checks the PRECONDITION, before
   604	    any rolling or shifting happens.
   605	    """
   606	    if not idx.is_monotonic_increasing:
   607	        raise AssertionError(f"[{label}] calendar index is not sorted")
   608	    if idx.has_duplicates:
   609	        dupes = idx[idx.duplicated()][:5]
   610	        raise AssertionError(f"[{label}] duplicate calendar dates: {list(dupes)}")
   611	    full = pd.date_range(idx.min(), idx.max(), freq="D")
   612	    if len(full) != len(idx) or not full.equals(pd.DatetimeIndex(idx)):
   613	        missing = full.difference(idx)[:5]
   614	        raise AssertionError(
   615	            f"[{label}] calendar has {len(full) - len(idx)} hole(s); "
   616	            f"`.shift()` would move by row position, not by one day. "
   617	            f"First missing: {list(missing)}"
   618	        )
   619	
   620	
   621	def build_panel(
   622	    rv: pd.DataFrame,
   623	    ff: FlowFeatures,
   624	    rv_col: str,
   625	    horizon: int,
   626	    asset: str,
   627	    flow_lag: int = 1,
   628	    state_lag: int = STATE_LAG,
   629	    btc_z: pd.Series | None = None,
   630	) -> Panel:
   631	    """Assemble the design matrix, indexed by the *target* date tau.
   632	
   633	    C4 fix. v1 had a single `pub_lag` that shifted EVERYTHING, so the
   634	    conservative "flow is only published at the end of day t+1" run also pushed
   635	    the HAR and return controls back to t-1 -- but RV_{t+1} and ret_{t+1} are
   636	    obviously known by then. That handicapped the baseline and made the
   637	    robustness run answer a question nobody asked.
   638	
   639	    Now:
   640	      state_lag : RV/return controls. ALWAYS 1 -- yesterday's realized
   641	                  volatility is known today, whatever the flow vendor does.
   642	      flow_lag  : 1 (baseline: flow_t usable from the end of day t)
   643	                  2 (conservative: flow_t only usable from the end of day t+1)
   644	    """
   645	    assert_calendar_is_complete(pd.DatetimeIndex(rv.index), f"{asset} rv")
   646	    cal = rv.copy()
   647	
   648	    # --- HAR features, computed on calendar days, as of the END of each day ---
   649	    lr = np.log(cal[rv_col])
   650	    cal["har_d"] = lr
   651	    cal["har_w"] = lr.rolling(HAR_W).mean()
   652	    cal["har_m"] = lr.rolling(HAR_M).mean()
   653	    cal["abs_ret"] = cal["ret"].abs()
   654	
   655	    # --- flow shocks, reindexed from flow-day space onto the 24/7 calendar ---
   656	    cal["flow"] = ff.flow.reindex(cal.index)     # NaN on non-flow calendar days
   657	    cal["z"] = ff.z.reindex(cal.index)
   658	    cal["z_unexp"] = ff.z_unexp.reindex(cal.index)
   659	    if ff.z_gross is not None:
   660	        cal["z_gross"] = ff.z_gross.reindex(cal.index)
   661	    if btc_z is not None:
   662	        cal["z_btc"] = btc_z.reindex(cal.index)
   663	
   664	    # --- the two visible lags ------------------------------------------------
   665	    state = [c for c in STATE_COLS if c in cal.columns]
   666	    flow_c = [c for c in FLOW_COLS if c in cal.columns]
   667	    day = pd.Series(cal.index, index=cal.index)
   668	
   669	    X = cal[state].shift(state_lag)
   670	    X[flow_c] = cal[flow_c].shift(flow_lag)
   671	    X["state_src_date"] = day.shift(state_lag)
   672	    X["flow_src_date"] = day.shift(flow_lag)
   673	
   674	    # --- target: average RV over the next `horizon` calendar days ------------
   675	    # tau is the FIRST day of the target window; the window is [tau, tau+h-1].
   676	    fwd = cal[rv_col].rolling(horizon).mean().shift(-(horizon - 1))
   677	    X["y"] = fwd
   678	    X["y_end_date"] = day.shift(-(horizon - 1))
   679	
   680	    # Derived flow-shock regressors
   681	    X["abs_z"] = X["z"].abs()
   682	    X["z_neg"] = X["abs_z"] * (X["z"] < 0)     # extra loading on redemptions
   683	    X["z_signed"] = X["z"]                     # directional (not magnitude)
   684	    X["z_sq"] = X["z"] ** 2                    # convex in shock size
   685	    X["abs_z_unexp"] = X["z_unexp"].abs()      # Warther: unexpected component
   686	    if "z_gross" in X.columns:
   687	        X["abs_z_gross"] = X["z_gross"].abs()  # gross churn, blind to netting
   688	    if btc_z is not None:
   689	        X["abs_z_btc"] = X["z_btc"].abs()
   690	
   691	    X["dow_src"] = X["flow_src_date"].dt.dayofweek   # 4 = Friday flow day
   692	    panel = X.dropna(subset=["y", "har_m", "z", "y_end_date"])
   693	    return Panel(panel, rv_col, horizon, asset, state_lag=state_lag, flow_lag=flow_lag)
   694	
   695	
   696	def assert_no_lookahead(p: Panel) -> None:
   697	    """Re-derive the timing guarantee from the data itself.
   698	
   699	    Checks, per Codex's review:
   700	      (1) state gap == state_lag exactly. `<` would be lookahead; `>` would mean
   701	          `.shift()` moved by row position across a calendar hole, silently
   702	          turning a t+1 target into a t+2 target. An inequality check would miss
   703	          the second, which is exactly how the 2026-07-13 Yahoo gap slipped past.
   704	      (2) flow gap == flow_lag exactly, verified SEPARATELY. v1 only had one
   705	          src_date, so it could not have caught a state/flow lag mixup.
   706	      (3) the target window closes exactly h-1 days after it opens -- not merely
   707	          "at or after". A calendar hole inside the rolling target window would
   708	          stretch a 5-day window across 6 calendar days and the old `>=` check
   709	          would have waved it through.
   710	    """
   711	    d = p.df
   712	    for src, lag, name in (
   713	        ("state_src_date", p.state_lag, "state"),
   714	        ("flow_src_date", p.flow_lag, "flow"),
   715	    ):
   716	        gap = (d.index - d[src]).dt.days
   717	        if (gap != lag).any():
   718	            bad = d.loc[gap != lag]
   719	            raise AssertionError(
   720	                f"[{p.asset} h={p.horizon}] {len(bad)} rows have a {name} "
   721	                f"source->target gap != {lag} day(s) "
   722	                f"(min={gap.min()}, max={gap.max()}). First offender: "
   723	                f"target={bad.index[0].date()} src={bad[src].iloc[0].date()}"
   724	            )
   725	    span = (d["y_end_date"] - d.index).dt.days
   726	    if (span != p.horizon - 1).any():
   727	        bad = d.loc[span != p.horizon - 1]
   728	        raise AssertionError(
   729	            f"[{p.asset} h={p.horizon}] {len(bad)} target windows do not span "
   730	            f"exactly {p.horizon} calendar days (min={span.min()}, max={span.max()})"
   731	        )
   732	
   733

   735	# 6. Estimation
   736	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   737	SPECS: dict[str, list[str]] = {
   738	    "HAR":        ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m"],
   739	    "HAR+ctrl":   ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret"],
   740	    "H1_absflow": ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret", "abs_z"],
   741	    "H2_asym":    ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret", "abs_z", "z_neg"],
   742	    "H4_own":     ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret", "abs_z"],
   743	    "H4_plus_btc": [
   744	        "har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret", "abs_z", "abs_z_btc",
   745	    ],
   746	}
   747	
   748	
   749	def _ols(X: np.ndarray, y: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
   750	    return np.linalg.lstsq(np.column_stack([np.ones(len(X)), X]), y, rcond=None)[0]
   751	
   752	
   753	def _hac_se(X: np.ndarray, y: np.ndarray, beta: np.ndarray, lag: int) -> np.ndarray:
   754	    """Newey-West standard errors (needed: overlapping targets when h>1)."""
   755	    Xc = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(X)), X])
   756	    u = y - Xc @ beta
   757	    XtX_inv = np.linalg.pinv(Xc.T @ Xc)
   758	    S = (Xc * u[:, None]).T @ (Xc * u[:, None])
   759	    for L in range(1, lag + 1):
   760	        w = 1 - L / (lag + 1)
   761	        A = (Xc[L:] * u[L:, None]).T @ (Xc[:-L] * u[:-L, None])
   762	        S += w * (A + A.T)
   763	    V = XtX_inv @ S @ XtX_inv
   764	    return np.sqrt(np.maximum(np.diag(V), 0))
   765	
   766	
   767	def canonical_bandwidth(h: int, n: int) -> int:
   768	    """The repo's canonical HAC bandwidth (see .claude/rules/experiments.md).
   769	
   770	    `h-1` alone degenerates to ZERO at h=1, i.e. no HAC at all, which is the
   771	    K1655 trap. Every long-run variance in this file uses max(h-1, this).
   772	    """
   773	    return max(1, min(int(np.ceil(h ** (1 / 3) * n ** (1 / 3))), n // 4))
   774	
   775	
   776	def in_sample(p: Panel, spec: str) -> dict:
   777	    cols = SPECS[spec]
   778	    d = p.df.dropna(subset=cols)
   779	    X = d[cols].to_numpy(float)
   780	    y = np.log(d["y"].to_numpy(float))
   781	    beta = _ols(X, y)
   782	    lag = max(p.horizon - 1, canonical_bandwidth(p.horizon, len(d)))
   783	    se = _hac_se(X, y, beta, lag)
   784	    names = ["const"] + cols
   785	    yhat = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(X)), X]) @ beta
   786	    ss = 1 - np.sum((y - yhat) ** 2) / np.sum((y - y.mean()) ** 2)
   787	    return {
   788	        "spec": spec,
   789	        "n": int(len(d)),
   790	        "r2": round(float(ss), 4),
   791	        "hac_lag": int(lag),
   792	        "coef": {
   793	            nm: {"beta": round(float(b), 5), "t": round(float(b / s) if s > 0 else 0.0, 3)}
   794	            for nm, b, s in zip(names, beta, se)
   795	        },
   796	    }
   797	
   798	
   799	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   800	# 7. Paired fixed-window OOS — the estimator GW requires (C1)
   801	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   802	@dataclass
   803	class PairedOOS:
   804	    actual: np.ndarray
   805	    pred_base: np.ndarray
   806	    pred_aug: np.ndarray
   807	    origins: pd.DatetimeIndex
   808	    audit: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
   809	
   810	
   811	def paired_oos(
   812	    p: Panel,
   813	    base: str,
   814	    alt: str,
   815	    train_window: int | None = GW_TRAIN_WINDOW,
   816	    smearing: str = "own",
   817	) -> PairedOOS:
   818	    """Both models, one estimation window, identical training dates.
   819	
   820	    `train_window=None` reproduces v1's EXPANDING window. It is retained for one
   821	    purpose only: to show, in the results file, what the design change did to the
   822	    statistic. Expanding-window losses never feed a verdict here -- see
   823	    `giacomini_white` for why the scheme is the whole ballgame.
   824	
   825	    Why fixed and not expanding (this is the whole point of the C1 fix):
   826	    Giacomini-White (2006) tests equal predictive ability of FORECASTING METHODS
   827	    -- estimation error included -- and its asymptotics need the estimator to
   828	    have bounded memory. An expanding window does not. v1 ran an expanding
   829	    window and then applied a test whose null assumed otherwise.
   830	
   831	    Both specs are fit on the AUGMENTED model's complete-case mask, so they see
   832	    the exact same rows, the exact same targets, and the exact same embargo. That
   833	    common sample is part of the nested comparison, not a cosmetic detail.
   834	
   835	    Embargo: with overlapping h-day targets, "every row before i" would let the
   836	    model train on a label window that overlaps the forecast day. A training row
   837	    is eligible at origin i only once its whole label window has closed:
   838	    `y_end_date < origin_i`.
   839	
   840	    Smearing (log -> variance level). The augmented spec has more parameters ->
   841	    lower training residual variance -> a smaller exp(s^2/2) multiplier -> lower
   842	    variance forecasts. QLIKE is asymmetric, so this channel could in principle
   843	    MANUFACTURE the null we are reporting.
   844	      "own"    -- each spec uses its own s^2  (primary)
   845	      "none"   -- no smearing at all, forecast = exp(mu)
   846	      "shared" -- both specs use the BASELINE's s^2, neutralising the channel
   847	    The dof correction (N - k) is what makes E[s^2] spec-invariant under the
   848	    null: with RSS/N a richer spec gets a mechanically smaller s^2 even when its
   849	    extra regressor is pure noise.
   850	    """
   851	    if not set(SPECS[base]) < set(SPECS[alt]):
   852	        raise ValueError(
   853	            f"GW requires strict nesting: {base} must be a proper subset of {alt}"
   854	        )
   855	    if smearing not in {"own", "none", "shared"}:
   856	        raise ValueError(f"unknown smearing mode: {smearing}")
   857	
   858	    burn_in = GW_TRAIN_WINDOW if train_window is None else train_window
   859	    cols = list(dict.fromkeys([*SPECS[alt], "y", "y_end_date"]))
   860	    d = p.df.dropna(subset=cols)
   861	    if len(d) < burn_in + GW_MIN_LOSSES:
   862	        return PairedOOS(np.array([]), np.array([]), np.array([]), pd.DatetimeIndex([]))
   863	
   864	    Xb = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(d)), d[SPECS[base]].to_numpy(float)])
   865	    Xa = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(d)), d[SPECS[alt]].to_numpy(float)])
   866	    yraw = d["y"].to_numpy(float)
   867	    yfit = np.log(np.clip(yraw, VAR_FLOOR, None))
   868	    origins = d.index
   869	    y_end = d["y_end_date"].to_numpy()
   870	
   871	    if not pd.Series(y_end).is_monotonic_increasing:
   872	        raise AssertionError(f"[{p.asset}] y_end_date is not monotonic; searchsorted invalid")
   873	
   874	    n_clip = 0
   875	
   876	    def fit(x_all, start, end, i, s2_override=None):
   877	        nonlocal n_clip
   878	        xtr, ytr = x_all[start:end], yfit[start:end]
   879	        # Normal equations, not SVD. The power simulation re-runs this loop
   880	        # ~16k times, and an SVD per origin makes that a half-hour job. There is
   881	        # exactly ONE estimation path in this file -- the simulation must not be
   882	        # allowed to quietly use a different estimator than the headline result.
   883	        # `test_normal_equations_match_lstsq` pins this to the SVD solution.
   884	        gram = xtr.T @ xtr
   885	        try:
   886	            beta = np.linalg.solve(gram, xtr.T @ ytr)
   887	        except np.linalg.LinAlgError:
   888	            beta = np.linalg.lstsq(xtr, ytr, rcond=None)[0]
   889	        resid = ytr - xtr @ beta
   890	        # dof-corrected: E[s2] is then spec-invariant under the null
   891	        s2 = float(resid @ resid / max(len(ytr) - x_all.shape[1], 1))
   892	        mu = float(x_all[i] @ beta)
   893	        # Trap runaway extrapolation BEFORE exponentiating: a regressor far
   894	        # outside its training range can otherwise produce an astronomical
   895	        # variance forecast and let two origins dominate the whole QLIKE mean.
   896	        # Bounds come from training values only and are applied identically to
   897	        # both specs, so neither is advantaged. `n_clipped` reports how often it
   898	        # binds -- if that is 0, this is a pure no-op safety net.
   899	        lo, hi = float(ytr.min()) - 1.0, float(ytr.max()) + 1.0
   900	        if not (lo <= mu <= hi):
   901	            n_clip += 1
   902	            mu = min(max(mu, lo), hi)
   903	        use = s2 if s2_override is None else s2_override
   904	        if smearing == "none":
   905	            return math.exp(mu), s2
   906	        return max(math.exp(mu + 0.5 * use), VAR_FLOOR), s2
   907	
   908	    act, fb, fa, dates, sizes, gaps = [], [], [], [], [], []
   909	    for i in range(burn_in, len(d)):
   910	        # Forward-label embargo: a training row is usable only once its whole
   911	        # label window has closed strictly before this origin.
   912	        end = int(np.searchsorted(y_end, origins[i].to_datetime64(), side="left"))
   913	        end = min(end, i)
   914	        start = 0 if train_window is None else end - train_window
   915	        if start < 0 or end - start < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
   916	            continue
   917	        pb, s2b = fit(Xb, start, end, i)
   918	        # "shared" forces the BASELINE's s^2 onto the augmented spec too
   919	        pa, _ = fit(Xa, start, end, i, s2_override=s2b if smearing == "shared" else None)
   920	        act.append(float(yraw[i]))
   921	        fb.append(pb)
   922	        fa.append(pa)
   923	        dates.append(origins[i])
   924	        sizes.append(end - start)
   925	        gaps.append((origins[i] - pd.Timestamp(y_end[end - 1])).days)
   926	
   927	    audit = {
   928	        "scheme": "expanding" if train_window is None else "fixed_rolling",
   929	        "train_window": None if train_window is None else int(train_window),
   930	        "n_origins": len(act),
   931	        "fixed_window_held": bool(
   932	            train_window is not None
   933	            and sizes
   934	            and min(sizes) == max(sizes) == train_window
   935	        ),
   936	        "same_training_dates_for_both_models": True,
   937	        "min_origin_minus_last_train_label_end_days": int(min(gaps)) if gaps else None,
   938	        "embargo_ok": bool(gaps and min(gaps) >= 1),
   939	        "smearing": smearing,
   940	        "n_forecasts_clipped_to_training_range": n_clip,
   941	    }
   942	    return PairedOOS(
   943	        np.array(act), np.array(fb), np.array(fa), pd.DatetimeIndex(dates), audit
   944	    )
   945	
   946	
   947	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   948	# 8. Inference
   949	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   950	def _bartlett_lrv(x: np.ndarray, max_lag: int) -> float:
   951	    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
   952	    x = x[np.isfinite(x)]
   953	    n = len(x)
   954	    if n < 10:
   955	        raise ValueError("long-run variance needs >=10 finite observations")
   956	    c = x - float(np.mean(x))
   957	    lrv = float(c @ c / n)
   958	    for lag in range(1, min(max_lag, n - 1) + 1):
   959	        w = 1.0 - lag / (max_lag + 1.0)
   960	        lrv += 2.0 * w * float(c[lag:] @ c[:-lag] / n)
   961	    if not np.isfinite(lrv) or lrv <= 0:
   962	        raise ValueError(f"non-positive long-run variance: {lrv}")
   963	    return lrv
   964	
   965	
   966	def giacomini_white(loss_aug: np.ndarray, loss_base: np.ndarray, h: int) -> dict:
   967	    """GW (2006) equal-unconditional-predictive-ability test under general loss.
   968	
   969	    READ THIS BEFORE CONCLUDING WE JUST RENAMED DM.
   970	    The arithmetic here -- mean loss difference over a Bartlett HAC standard
   971	    error -- is algebraically the same as a HAC Diebold-Mariano t. On the same
   972	    loss stream the two statistics COINCIDE, and the results file shows them
   973	    coinciding. That is not a bug and it is not a relabelling.
   974	
   975	    What makes this a legal test under nesting is the ESTIMATION SCHEME, not the
   976	    formula. GW tests equal predictive ability of forecasting METHODS -- the
   977	    fitted-parameter noise is part of the object being compared, not a nuisance
   978	    to be purged. For that limiting experiment to hold, the estimator must have
   979	    BOUNDED MEMORY, i.e. a fixed-length rolling window. Feed it fixed-window
   980	    forecasts and the statistic is asymptotically standard normal even when the
   981	    models are nested. Feed it EXPANDING-window forecasts -- what v1 did -- and
   982	    the nested null is degenerate, the statistic is biased toward the smaller
   983	    model, and no reference distribution rescues it.
   984	
   985	    So: same number, different scheme, different null, different validity. The
   986	    expanding-window value is reported alongside as a diagnostic precisely so a
   987	    reader can see what the scheme change did.
   988	
   989	    Moment: E[L_aug - L_base] = 0.  Negative z favours the flow model.
   990	    """
   991	    aug = np.asarray(loss_aug, float)
   992	    base = np.asarray(loss_base, float)
   993	    if aug.shape != base.shape:
   994	        raise ValueError("loss arrays must have identical shapes")
   995	    fin = np.isfinite(aug) & np.isfinite(base)
   996	    diff = aug[fin] - base[fin]
   997	    n = len(diff)
   998	    if n < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
   999	        raise ValueError(f"GW requires >={GW_MIN_LOSSES} losses; got {n}")
  1000	    bw = max(h - 1, canonical_bandwidth(h, n))
  1001	    lrv = _bartlett_lrv(diff, bw)
  1002	    se = math.sqrt(lrv / n)
  1003	    z = float(np.mean(diff)) / se
  1004	    return {
  1005	        "test": "Giacomini-White (2006) equal unconditional predictive ability",
  1006	        "loss": "Patton QLIKE",
  1007	        "estimand": "E[QLIKE_aug - QLIKE_base]",
  1008	        "forecast_scheme": "paired fixed rolling estimation window",
  1009	        "null": "equal expected QLIKE loss for the two forecasting METHODS",
  1010	        "direction": "negative z favours the flow model",
  1011	        "n": int(n),
  1012	        "mean_loss_diff_aug_minus_base": float(np.mean(diff)),
  1013	        "z_stat": float(z),
  1014	        "p_value_two_sided": float(2.0 * stats.norm.sf(abs(z))),
  1015	        "p_value_one_sided_flow_better": float(stats.norm.cdf(z)),
  1016	        "hac_lag_used": int(bw),
  1017	        "standard_error": float(se),
  1018	    }
  1019	
  1020	
  1021	def material_gain_exclusion(
  1022	    loss_aug: np.ndarray,
  1023	    loss_base: np.ndarray,
  1024	    h: int,
  1025	    margin: float = MATERIAL_GAIN_MARGIN,
  1026	) -> dict:
  1027	    """Can a pre-specified relative QLIKE gain be RULED OUT? (C2b)
  1028	
  1029	    This is the only test in the file that can license a bounded null, and it is
  1030	    the one v1 never ran.
  1031	
  1032	    H0: the flow method improves expected QLIKE by at least `margin`,
  1033	        E[L_aug] <= (1 - margin) * E[L_base].
  1034	    Rejecting H0 (positive z, small p) means a gain that large is NOT there.
  1035	
  1036	    Note what this does and does not buy. It reverses the burden of proof, so a
  1037	    rejection is a genuine upper bound on the effect -- but the bound lives in
  1038	    QLIKE-LOSS space ("no >=1% relative QLIKE improvement"), NOT in RV-uplift
  1039	    space. v1's "we can rule out a >=16% RV rise per 1-sd shock" came from
  1040	    reading a power curve backwards, which is not an inference at all. Failing to
  1041	    reject equal accuracy never proves equality; only this does, and only for
  1042	    what it actually measures.
  1043	    """
  1044	    if not 0 < margin < 1:
  1045	        raise ValueError("margin must lie strictly in (0, 1)")
  1046	    aug = np.asarray(loss_aug, float)
  1047	    base = np.asarray(loss_base, float)
  1048	    fin = np.isfinite(aug) & np.isfinite(base)
  1049	    moment = aug[fin] - (1.0 - margin) * base[fin]
  1050	    n = len(moment)
  1051	    if n < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1052	        raise ValueError(f"exclusion test requires >={GW_MIN_LOSSES} losses; got {n}")
  1053	    bw = max(h - 1, canonical_bandwidth(h, n))
  1054	    lrv = _bartlett_lrv(moment, bw)
  1055	    se = math.sqrt(lrv / n)
  1056	    z = float(np.mean(moment)) / se
  1057	    return {
  1058	        "test": "one-sided material-gain exclusion (fixed-window GW moment)",
  1059	        "margin_relative_qlike": float(margin),
  1060	        "null": f"flow method improves expected QLIKE by at least {100 * margin:.1f}%",
  1061	        "alternative": f"any QLIKE gain is smaller than {100 * margin:.1f}%",
  1062	        "n": int(n),
  1063	        "mean_margin_moment": float(np.mean(moment)),
  1064	        "z_stat": float(z),
  1065	        "p_value_one_sided": float(stats.norm.sf(z)),
  1066	        "hac_lag_used": int(bw),
  1067	        "scope": (
  1068	            "rules out a material QLIKE gain; does NOT prove exact equality, and "
  1069	            "says nothing about RV-uplift magnitude"
  1070	        ),
  1071	    }
  1072	
  1073	
  1074	def qlike_gain_upper_bound(
  1075	    loss_aug: np.ndarray, loss_base: np.ndarray, h: int, alpha: float = 0.05
  1076	) -> float | None:
  1077	    """One-sided (1-alpha) UPPER CONFIDENCE BOUND on the relative QLIKE gain, in %.
  1078	
  1079	    This inverts `material_gain_exclusion`: it returns the smallest margin whose
  1080	    one-sided test rejects. Gains larger than the bound are excluded by the data;
  1081	    gains smaller than it are not.
  1082	
  1083	    Why this and not the power curve. Both answer "how big an effect could this
  1084	    design have seen?", but only ONE of them is an inference about the effect. A
  1085	    power curve is a property of the design under an assumed truth; a confidence
  1086	    bound is a statement about the truth given the data. v1 reached for the power
  1087	    curve and read it backwards. This is the object it should have reported.
  1088	
  1089	    Returns None when even a 90% gain cannot be excluded, i.e. the design says
  1090	    essentially nothing about the effect size.
  1091	    """
  1092	    z_crit = float(stats.norm.ppf(1 - alpha))
  1093	
  1094	    def z_of(m: float) -> float:
  1095	        return material_gain_exclusion(loss_aug, loss_base, h, margin=m)["z_stat"]
  1096	
  1097	    lo, hi = 1e-4, 0.90
  1098	    if z_of(hi) < z_crit:
  1099	        return None
  1100	    if z_of(lo) >= z_crit:
  1101	        return lo * 100.0
  1102	    for _ in range(50):                       # z(m) is increasing in m
  1103	        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
  1104	        if z_of(mid) >= z_crit:
  1105	            hi = mid
  1106	        else:
  1107	            lo = mid
  1108	    return hi * 100.0
  1109	
  1110	
  1111	def block_bootstrap_ci(
  1112	    loss_aug: np.ndarray, loss_base: np.ndarray, h: int, seed: int, reps: int = 1999
  1113	) -> dict:
  1114	    """Paired circular moving-block CI for the relative QLIKE improvement."""
  1115	    aug = np.asarray(loss_aug, float)
  1116	    base = np.asarray(loss_base, float)
  1117	    fin = np.isfinite(aug) & np.isfinite(base)
  1118	    aug, base = aug[fin], base[fin]
  1119	    n = len(aug)
  1120	    if n < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1121	        raise ValueError(f"bootstrap requires >={GW_MIN_LOSSES} losses; got {n}")
  1122	    bl = max(int(h), int(math.ceil(n ** (1 / 3))))
  1123	    nb = int(math.ceil(n / bl))
  1124	    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
  1125	    off = np.arange(bl)
  1126	    draws = np.empty(reps)
  1127	    for b in range(reps):
  1128	        starts = rng.integers(0, n, size=nb)
  1129	        idx = ((starts[:, None] + off[None, :]) % n).ravel()[:n]
  1130	        bm = float(np.mean(base[idx]))
  1131	        draws[b] = 100.0 * (bm - float(np.mean(aug[idx]))) / bm
  1132	    pb = float(np.mean(base))
  1133	    return {
  1134	        "method": "paired circular moving-block bootstrap of fixed-window QLIKE losses",
  1135	        "seed": int(seed),
  1136	        "reps": int(reps),
  1137	        "block_length": int(bl),
  1138	        "point_improvement_pct": 100.0 * (pb - float(np.mean(aug))) / pb,
  1139	        "ci95_improvement_pct": [float(x) for x in np.quantile(draws, [0.025, 0.975])],
  1140	        "scope": "uncertainty diagnostic; forecasts are not re-estimated inside the bootstrap",
  1141	    }
  1142	
  1143	
  1144	def raw_dm_diagnostic(loss_aug: np.ndarray, loss_base: np.ndarray, h: int) -> dict:
  1145	    """Ordinary Diebold-Mariano on the QLIKE loss difference. DESCRIPTIVE ONLY.
  1146	
  1147	    Kept because it is what the literature reports and because its gap from the
  1148	    Giacomini-White statistic is itself informative -- but under the nested null
  1149	    it is biased toward the smaller model, so it is tagged `feeds_gate=False`
  1150	    everywhere and never reaches a verdict.
  1151	
  1152	    C5 -- naming. `volpred.stats.model_evaluation.dm_test` is a HAC-DM statistic
  1153	    combined with the Harvey-Liu-Zhu (2016) |t| > 3 heuristic threshold. It does
  1154	    NOT apply the Harvey-Leybourne-Newbold (1997) finite-sample correction
  1155	    factor, so v1 was wrong to call it "HLN modified DM". We report it under its
  1156	    true name. The one-sided p-value is taken from the SAME Student-t(n-1)
  1157	    distribution the helper uses for its two-sided p -- v1 switched to a normal
  1158	    CDF here, so its two p-values disagreed with each other.
  1159	    """
  1160	    t, p_two = dm_test(loss_aug, loss_base, h=h)
  1161	    n = int(np.isfinite(np.asarray(loss_aug) - np.asarray(loss_base)).sum())
  1162	    return {
  1163	        "statistic": "HAC-DM (canonical bandwidth) + Harvey-Liu-Zhu |t|>3 heuristic",
  1164	        "not_hln": (
  1165	            "This is NOT Harvey-Leybourne-Newbold modified DM: no finite-sample "
  1166	            "correction factor is applied. v1 mislabelled it."
  1167	        ),
  1168	        "role": "diagnostic-only; biased toward the smaller model under nesting",
  1169	        "feeds_gate": False,
  1170	        "t_stat": float(t),
  1171	        "p_two_sided_student_t": float(p_two),
  1172	        "p_one_sided_flow_better_student_t": float(stats.t.cdf(t, df=max(n - 1, 1))),
  1173	        "df": n - 1,
  1174	    }
  1175	
  1176	
  1177	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1178	# 9. The cell — one nested comparison, fully inferred
  1179	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1180	def evaluate_cell(
  1181	    p: Panel,
  1182	    base: str,
  1183	    alt: str,
  1184	    family: str,
  1185	    train_window: int = GW_TRAIN_WINDOW,
