     1	"""
     2	Comprehensive Model Evaluation Framework — Patton (2011) + Economic Significance.
     3	
     4	Implements CLAUDE.md Rule 6b: every model comparison must include at least
     5	the first 3 layers, and report ALL targets (not just favorable ones).
     6	
     7	Usage:
     8	    from volpred.stats.model_evaluation import evaluate_models
     9	
    10	    results = evaluate_models(
    11	        forecasts={"GJR": gjr_forecasts, "AMEM": amem_forecasts, "HAR": har_forecasts},
    12	        realized_r2=actual_squared_returns,
    13	        returns=raw_returns,  # for VaR/ES evaluation
    14	        alpha_var=0.01,       # VaR confidence level
    15	    )
    16	    # results contains all 6 layers of evaluation
    17	
    18	Layers:
    19	  1. Native target QLIKE (each model on its own target)
    20	  2. QLIKE on r² (Patton 2011 proxy-robust) — primary ranking
    21	  3. Spearman rank correlation (distribution-free)
    22	  4. DM tests + Harvey t>3.0 (pairwise)
    23	  5. MCS (Model Confidence Set) — multi-model control
    24	  6. VaR/ES economic significance — backtesting + calibration
    25	
    26	References:
    27	  - Patton (2011) J. Econometrics 160 — proxy-robust loss functions
    28	  - Hansen, Lunde & Nason (2011) Econometrica 79 — Model Confidence Set
    29	  - Kupiec (1995) — unconditional VaR coverage test
    30	  - Christoffersen (1998) — conditional VaR independence test
    31	  - Harvey et al. (2016) — multiple testing threshold t>3.0
    32	"""
    33	
    34	from __future__ import annotations
    35	
    36	import sys
    37	
    38	import numpy as np
    39	from scipy import stats
    40	from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
    41	
    42	
    43	def _warn_model_evaluation(message: str, exc: Exception) -> None:
    44	    print(f"[model_evaluation] WARN {message}: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
    45	
    46	
    47	# ─── Layer 1 & 2: QLIKE Loss ────────────────────────────────────
    48	
    49	def qlike(actual: np.ndarray, predicted: np.ndarray) -> float:
    50	    """QLIKE loss: mean(a/f - log(a/f) - 1). Lower is better.
    51	    Patton (2011): proxy-robust when actual is conditionally unbiased for sigma².
    52	    """
    53	    a = np.asarray(actual, dtype=np.float64)
    54	    f = np.asarray(predicted, dtype=np.float64)
    55	    valid = (a > 0) & (f > 0) & np.isfinite(a) & np.isfinite(f)
    56	    if valid.sum() < 10:
    57	        return np.nan
    58	    a, f = a[valid], f[valid]
    59	    ratio = a / f
    60	    return float(np.mean(ratio - np.log(ratio) - 1))
    61	
    62	
    63	def qlike_pointwise(actual: np.ndarray, predicted: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    64	    """Pointwise QLIKE losses for DM test."""
    65	    a = np.maximum(np.asarray(actual, dtype=np.float64), 1e-16)
    66	    f = np.maximum(np.asarray(predicted, dtype=np.float64), 1e-16)
    67	    ratio = a / f
    68	    return ratio - np.log(ratio) - 1
    69	
    70	
    71	def mse(actual: np.ndarray, predicted: np.ndarray) -> float:
    72	    """Mean Squared Error."""
    73	    return float(np.mean((actual - predicted) ** 2))
    74	
    75	
    76	# ─── Layer 3: Spearman Rank Correlation ──────────────────────────
    77	
    78	def spearman_corr(actual: np.ndarray, predicted: np.ndarray) -> Tuple[float, float]:
    79	    """Spearman rank correlation. Returns (rho, p_value)."""
    80	    valid = np.isfinite(actual) & np.isfinite(predicted)
    81	    if valid.sum() < 10:
    82	        return (np.nan, np.nan)
    83	    rho, p = stats.spearmanr(actual[valid], predicted[valid])
    84	    return (float(rho), float(p))
    85	
    86	
    87	# ─── Layer 4: Diebold-Mariano Test ──────────────────────────────
    88	
    89	def dm_test(loss1: np.ndarray, loss2: np.ndarray, h: int = 1) -> Tuple[float, float]:
    90	    """Diebold-Mariano test with Newey-West HAC.
    91	    Negative t → model 1 is better. Harvey (2016): |t| > 3.0 for significance.
    92	    """
    93	    d = np.asarray(loss1, dtype=np.float64) - np.asarray(loss2, dtype=np.float64)
    94	    valid = np.isfinite(d)
    95	    d = d[valid]
    96	    n = len(d)
    97	    if n < 10:
    98	        return (0.0, 1.0)
    99	
   100	    d_mean = np.mean(d)
   101	    max_lag = max(1, min(int(np.ceil(h ** (1/3) * n ** (1/3))), n // 4))
   102	    gamma0 = np.mean((d - d_mean) ** 2)
   103	    var_d = gamma0
   104	    for lag in range(1, max_lag + 1):
   105	        weight = 1 - lag / (max_lag + 1)
   106	        gamma_l = np.mean((d[lag:] - d_mean) * (d[:-lag] - d_mean))
   107	        var_d += 2 * weight * gamma_l
   108	
   109	    if var_d <= 0:
   110	        return (0.0, 1.0)
   111	    se = np.sqrt(var_d / n)
   112	    if se < 1e-15:
   113	        return (0.0, 1.0)
   114	
   115	    t_stat = d_mean / se
   116	    p_val = 2 * (1 - stats.t.cdf(abs(t_stat), df=n - 1))
   117	    return (float(t_stat), float(p_val))
   118	
   119	
   120	def clark_west_test(
   121	    actual: np.ndarray,
   122	    forecast_small: np.ndarray,
   123	    forecast_large: np.ndarray,
   124	    h: int = 1,
   125	    *,
   126	    max_lag: Optional[int] = None,
   127	    aggregate_axis: Optional[int] = None,
   128	) -> Dict:
   129	    """Clark-West (2007) MSPE-adjusted test for nested forecasts.
   130	
   131	    The adjusted loss differential is
   132	    ``e_small² - (e_large² - (forecast_small - forecast_large)²)``.
   133	    A positive statistic supports incremental predictive content in the larger
   134	    model. The formal test is one-sided; a two-sided p-value is also returned
   135	    for transparent comparison with older review artifacts.
   136	
   137	    ``aggregate_axis`` supports dependent panels. Cell-level adjusted losses
   138	    are averaged along that axis before HAC inference, so asset losses can be
   139	    aggregated by date instead of treating asset-days as independent.
   140	    """
   141	    if h < 1:
   142	        raise ValueError("h must be at least 1")
   143	
   144	    y = np.asarray(actual, dtype=np.float64)
   145	    f_small = np.asarray(forecast_small, dtype=np.float64)
   146	    f_large = np.asarray(forecast_large, dtype=np.float64)
   147	    if y.shape != f_small.shape or y.shape != f_large.shape:
   148	        raise ValueError("actual and both forecast arrays must have identical shapes")
   149	
   150	    valid = np.isfinite(y) & np.isfinite(f_small) & np.isfinite(f_large)
   151	    adjusted = np.full(y.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)
   152	    adjusted[valid] = (
   153	        (y[valid] - f_small[valid]) ** 2
   154	        - (y[valid] - f_large[valid]) ** 2
   155	        + (f_small[valid] - f_large[valid]) ** 2
   156	    )
   157	
   158	    if aggregate_axis is not None:
   159	        if adjusted.ndim == 0:
   160	            raise ValueError("aggregate_axis requires an array with at least one dimension")
   161	        counts = np.sum(np.isfinite(adjusted), axis=aggregate_axis)
   162	        sums = np.nansum(adjusted, axis=aggregate_axis)
   163	        adjusted = np.divide(
   164	            sums,
   165	            counts,
   166	            out=np.full(np.shape(sums), np.nan, dtype=np.float64),
   167	            where=counts > 0,
   168	        )
   169	
   170	    d = np.asarray(adjusted, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
   171	    d = d[np.isfinite(d)]
   172	    n = len(d)
   173	    if n < 10:
   174	        return {
   175	            "test": "Clark-West (2007) nested MSPE-adjusted",
   176	            "status": "insufficient_observations",
   177	            "n_obs": int(n),
   178	            "t_stat": 0.0,
   179	            "p_value_one_sided": 1.0,
   180	            "p_value_two_sided": 1.0,
  1180	def evaluate_cell(
  1181	    p: Panel,
  1182	    base: str,
  1183	    alt: str,
  1184	    family: str,
  1185	    train_window: int = GW_TRAIN_WINDOW,
  1186	    smearing: str = "own",
  1187	    register_gate: bool = True,
  1188	) -> dict | None:
  1189	    """Run one nested comparison end to end and register its tests."""
  1190	    po = paired_oos(p, base, alt, train_window, smearing)
  1191	    if len(po.actual) < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1192	        return None
  1193	
  1194	    lb = qlike_pointwise(po.actual, po.pred_base)
  1195	    la = qlike_pointwise(po.actual, po.pred_aug)
  1196	    h = p.horizon
  1197	    qb, qa = float(np.mean(lb)), float(np.mean(la))
  1198	    improve = (qb - qa) / qb * 100.0
  1199	
  1200	    gw = giacomini_white(la, lb, h)
  1201	    excl = material_gain_exclusion(la, lb, h)
  1202	    excl["qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct"] = qlike_gain_upper_bound(la, lb, h, alpha=0.05)
  1203	    excl["upper_bound_note"] = (
  1204	        "One-sided 95% upper confidence bound on the RELATIVE QLIKE gain, obtained "
  1205	        "by inverting the exclusion test. Gains above it are excluded by the data; "
  1206	        "gains below it are not. None = even a 90% gain cannot be excluded."
  1207	    )
  1208	    boot = block_bootstrap_ci(la, lb, h, seed=SEED + h)
  1209	    dm = raw_dm_diagnostic(la, lb, h)
  1210	
  1211	    # v1's design, re-run purely so the results file can show what changed. An
  1212	    # expanding window breaks GW's bounded-memory condition, so a HAC t-test on
  1213	    # these losses is NOT a valid nested test -- whatever it happens to say.
  1214	    exp_po = paired_oos(p, base, alt, train_window=None, smearing=smearing)
  1215	    expanding = None
  1216	    if len(exp_po.actual) >= GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1217	        exp_lb = qlike_pointwise(exp_po.actual, exp_po.pred_base)
  1218	        exp_la = qlike_pointwise(exp_po.actual, exp_po.pred_aug)
  1219	        exp_dm = raw_dm_diagnostic(exp_la, exp_lb, h)
  1220	        expanding = {
  1221	            "scheme": "expanding window (v1's design)",
  1222	            "valid_for_nested_inference": False,
  1223	            "why_invalid": (
  1224	                "GW's limiting experiment needs bounded estimator memory. An "
  1225	                "expanding window has none, so the nested null is degenerate and "
  1226	                "the statistic is biased toward the smaller model."
  1227	            ),
  1228	            "feeds_gate": False,
  1229	            "n_oos": int(len(exp_po.actual)),
  1230	            "qlike_improvement_pct": float(
  1231	                (np.mean(exp_lb) - np.mean(exp_la)) / np.mean(exp_lb) * 100.0
  1232	            ),
  1233	            "hac_t_stat": exp_dm["t_stat"],
  1234	        }
  1235	
  1236	    # Clark-West: a DIFFERENT estimand. CW adjusts the MSPE of variance-level
  1237	    # forecasts; it is not a QLIKE general-loss test and is not relabelled as one.
  1238	    cw = clark_west_test(po.actual, po.pred_base, po.pred_aug, h=h)
  1239	
  1240	    cell = f"{p.asset}_h{h}_{alt}" + ("" if smearing == "own" else f"_{smearing}")
  1241	    LOSS_CACHE[cell] = (la, lb, h)
  1242	    register(
  1243	        TestRecord(
  1244	            family=family, cell=cell, asset=p.asset, horizon=h, base=base, alt=alt,
  1245	            inference="giacomini_white_qlike_fixed_window",
  1246	            estimand="E[QLIKE_aug - QLIKE_base]",
  1247	            scheme="paired fixed rolling window",
  1248	            stat=gw["z_stat"], p_one_sided=gw["p_value_one_sided_flow_better"],
  1249	            feeds_gate=register_gate, qlike_improve_pct=improve, n=gw["n"],
  1250	        )
  1251	    )
  1252	    register(
  1253	        TestRecord(
  1254	            family=family, cell=cell, asset=p.asset, horizon=h, base=base, alt=alt,
  1255	            inference="raw_dm_qlike",
  1256	            estimand="E[QLIKE_aug - QLIKE_base] (nested-biased)",
  1257	            scheme="paired fixed rolling window",
  1258	            stat=dm["t_stat"], p_one_sided=dm["p_one_sided_flow_better_student_t"],
  1259	            feeds_gate=False, qlike_improve_pct=improve, n=gw["n"],
  1260	        )
  1261	    )
  1262	    register(
  1263	        TestRecord(
  1264	            family=family, cell=cell, asset=p.asset, horizon=h, base=base, alt=alt,
  1265	            inference="clark_west_mspe",
  1266	            estimand="MSPE of variance-level forecasts (NOT QLIKE)",
  1267	            scheme="paired fixed rolling window",
  1268	            stat=float(cw["t_stat"]),
  1269	            p_one_sided=float(cw.get("p_value_one_sided", np.nan)),
  1270	            feeds_gate=False, qlike_improve_pct=improve, n=gw["n"],
  1271	        )
  1272	    )
  1273	
  1274	    return {
  1275	        "asset": p.asset,
  1276	        "horizon": h,
  1277	        "rv_proxy": p.rv_col,
  1278	        "base": base,
  1279	        "alt": alt,
  1280	        "cell": cell,
  1281	        "family": family,
  1282	        "state_lag": p.state_lag,
  1283	        "flow_lag": p.flow_lag,
  1284	        "smearing": smearing,
  1285	        "n_oos": int(len(po.actual)),
  1286	        "oos_start": str(po.origins.min().date()),
  1287	        "oos_end": str(po.origins.max().date()),
  1288	        "qlike_base": qb,
  1289	        "qlike_alt": qa,
  1290	        "qlike_improvement_pct": improve,
  1291	        "oos_audit": po.audit,
  1292	        "primary_inference_gw_qlike": gw,
  1293	        "material_gain_exclusion": excl,
  1294	        "bootstrap_ci": boot,
  1295	        "raw_dm_diagnostic": dm,
  1296	        "expanding_window_diagnostic_v1_design": expanding,
  1297	        "statistic_coincidence_note": (
  1298	            "raw_dm_diagnostic.t_stat equals primary_inference_gw_qlike.z_stat by "
  1299	            "construction: on ONE loss stream the HAC t and the GW z are the same "
  1300	            "arithmetic. What separates them is the estimation scheme feeding the "
  1301	            "losses and the null being tested. Compare against "
  1302	            "expanding_window_diagnostic_v1_design to see what the scheme change did."
  1303	        ),
  1304	        "clark_west_mspe_separate_estimand": {
  1305	            "t_stat": float(cw["t_stat"]),
  1306	            "p_one_sided": float(cw.get("p_value_one_sided", np.nan)),
  1307	            "estimand": "MSPE (variance level), not QLIKE",
  1308	            "feeds_gate": False,
  1309	            "note": (
  1310	                "Clark-West corrects the MSPE of the nested comparison. It is "
  1311	                "evidence about a different loss than the one the verdict uses, "
  1312	                "and is reported as such."
  1313	            ),
  1314	        },
  1315	    }

  1321	def fit_calendar_har(rv: pd.DataFrame, rv_col: str) -> dict:
  1322	    """Fit the HAR+ctrl recursion on CALENDAR days. This is the simulation DGP.
  1323	
  1324	    Distinct from `in_sample`, which fits on PANEL rows (flow days only). The
  1325	    simulation has to step the volatility process forward on every calendar day,

  1318	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1319	# 10. Power simulation (C2a) — a statement about the DESIGN, not the effect
  1320	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1321	def fit_calendar_har(rv: pd.DataFrame, rv_col: str) -> dict:
  1322	    """Fit the HAR+ctrl recursion on CALENDAR days. This is the simulation DGP.
  1323	
  1324	    Distinct from `in_sample`, which fits on PANEL rows (flow days only). The
  1325	    simulation has to step the volatility process forward on every calendar day,
  1326	    including the weekends when no flow prints, so it needs a calendar-day law of
  1327	    motion.
  1328	    """
  1329	    lr = np.log(rv[rv_col])
  1330	    frame = pd.DataFrame(
  1331	        {
  1332	            "y": lr,
  1333	            "har_d": lr.shift(1),
  1334	            "har_w": lr.rolling(HAR_W).mean().shift(1),
  1335	            "har_m": lr.rolling(HAR_M).mean().shift(1),
  1336	            "ret": rv["ret"].shift(1),
  1337	            "abs_ret": rv["ret"].abs().shift(1),
  1338	        }
  1339	    ).dropna()
  1340	    cols = ["har_d", "har_w", "har_m", "ret", "abs_ret"]
  1341	    X = frame[cols].to_numpy(float)
  1342	    y = frame["y"].to_numpy(float)
  1343	    beta = _ols(X, y)
  1344	    resid = y - np.column_stack([np.ones(len(X)), X]) @ beta
  1345	    persistence = float(beta[1] + beta[2] + beta[3])
  1346	    if persistence >= 1.0:
  1347	        raise AssertionError(
  1348	            f"calendar HAR is non-stationary (phi_d+phi_w+phi_m = {persistence:.4f}); "
  1349	            "the simulated paths would explode"
  1350	        )
  1351	    return {
  1352	        "beta": beta,
  1353	        "resid": resid,
  1354	        "n": int(len(frame)),
  1355	        "persistence": persistence,
  1356	        "resid_sd": float(np.std(resid)),
  1357	    }
  1358	
  1359	
  1360	def _block_resample(resid: np.ndarray, n_out: int, rng, block: int) -> np.ndarray:
  1361	    """Circular moving-block resample: keeps the innovation autocorrelation."""
  1362	    nb = int(math.ceil(n_out / block))
  1363	    starts = rng.integers(0, len(resid), size=nb)
  1364	    idx = ((starts[:, None] + np.arange(block)[None, :]) % len(resid)).ravel()[:n_out]
  1365	    return resid[idx]
  1366	
  1367	
  1368	def simulate_lrv(
  1369	    lr_seed: np.ndarray,
  1370	    ret_lag: np.ndarray,
  1371	    aret_lag: np.ndarray,
  1372	    shock: np.ndarray,
  1373	    dgp: dict,
  1374	    beta_flow: float,
  1375	    rng,
  1376	    bounds: tuple[float, float],
  1377	) -> tuple[np.ndarray, int]:
  1378	    """One simulated calendar log-RV path carrying a KNOWN flow effect.
  1379	
  1380	    The effect is injected into the LAW OF MOTION, so it propagates into
  1381	    tomorrow's HAR features exactly as a genuine effect would. That matters: the
  1382	    HAR baseline partially absorbs a real effect through its own lags, which
  1383	    makes the effect HARDER to detect. Injecting into the target only -- without
  1384	    propagation -- would overstate power, which is the optimistic direction and
  1385	    therefore the dangerous one.
  1386	    """
  1387	    b = dgp["beta"]
  1388	    n = len(shock)
  1389	    lrv = np.empty(n)
  1390	    lrv[:HAR_M] = lr_seed[:HAR_M]
  1391	    e = _block_resample(dgp["resid"], n, rng, block=max(2, int(math.ceil(n ** (1 / 3)))))
  1392	    lo, hi = bounds
  1393	    n_clip = 0
  1394	    sw = float(lrv[HAR_M - HAR_W : HAR_M].sum())
  1395	    sm = float(lrv[:HAR_M].sum())
  1396	    for d in range(HAR_M, n):
  1397	        v = (
  1398	            b[0]
  1399	            + b[1] * lrv[d - 1]
  1400	            + b[2] * (sw / HAR_W)
  1401	            + b[3] * (sm / HAR_M)
  1402	            + b[4] * ret_lag[d]
  1403	            + b[5] * aret_lag[d]
  1404	            + beta_flow * shock[d]
  1405	            + e[d]
  1406	        )
  1407	        if not (lo <= v <= hi):
  1408	            n_clip += 1
  1409	            v = min(max(v, lo), hi)
  1410	        lrv[d] = v
  1411	        sw += lrv[d] - lrv[d - HAR_W]
  1412	        sm += lrv[d] - lrv[d - HAR_M]
  1413	    return lrv, n_clip
  1414	
  1415	
  1416	def power_simulation(
  1417	    rv: pd.DataFrame,
  1418	    ff: FlowFeatures,
  1419	    asset: str,
  1420	    horizon: int = 1,
  1421	    reps: int = POWER_REPS,
  1422	    betas: tuple[float, ...] = POWER_BETAS,
  1423	) -> dict:
  1424	    """Simulated power of the pre-specified GW gate, with size calibration.
  1425	
  1426	    v1's "MDE" was not a power analysis at all: it injected each beta ONCE into
  1427	    the single realised noise path and took the first crossing. No repeated
  1428	    sampling, no size calibration, no confidence interval -- and the resulting
  1429	    curve was not even monotone in beta, which is a tell that it was reading
  1430	    noise. It then INVERTED the logic and claimed the null "excludes effects
  1431	    >= the MDE". Power cannot do that. Only the exclusion test can, and it is
  1432	    reported separately.
  1433	
  1434	    What this function establishes: for an effect of size beta, how often would
  1435	    the pre-specified gate fire? Nothing more.
  1436	    """
  1437	    dgp = fit_calendar_har(rv, "rv_gk")
  1438	    idx = rv.index
  1439	    lr_real = np.log(rv["rv_gk"])
  1440	    lr_seed = lr_real.ffill().bfill().to_numpy()
  1441	    missing = rv["rv_gk"].isna().to_numpy()
  1442	
  1443	    z_cal = ff.z.reindex(idx)
  1444	    shock = z_cal.abs().shift(1).fillna(0.0).to_numpy()     # |z_{d-1}|, as in H1
  1445	    ret_lag = rv["ret"].shift(1).fillna(0.0).to_numpy()
  1446	    aret_lag = rv["ret"].abs().shift(1).fillna(0.0).to_numpy()
  1447	
  1448	    # Keep simulated log-variance inside a generous envelope of what the asset has
  1449	    # actually done. Without it a draw from the tail of the innovation block can
  1450	    # walk a persistent HAR into absurd territory and one path dominates the
  1451	    # rejection rate. `clip_rate` reports how often it binds.
  1452	    obs = lr_real.dropna()
  1453	    bounds = (float(obs.min()) - 3.0, float(obs.max()) + 3.0)
  1454	
  1455	    rows = []
  1456	    for b in betas:
  1457	        rng = np.random.default_rng(SEED + int(round(b * 10_000)))
  1458	        zs, clips, fails = [], 0, 0
  1459	        for _ in range(reps):
  1460	            lrv, nc = simulate_lrv(
  1461	                lr_seed, ret_lag, aret_lag, shock, dgp, b, rng, bounds
  1462	            )
  1463	            clips += nc
  1464	            sim = rv[["ret"]].copy()
  1465	            sim["rv_gk"] = np.exp(lrv)
  1466	            sim.loc[missing, "rv_gk"] = np.nan     # preserve the real missingness
  1467	            try:
  1468	                p = build_panel(sim, ff, "rv_gk", horizon, asset)
  1469	                po = paired_oos(p, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow")
  1470	                if len(po.actual) < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1471	                    fails += 1
  1472	                    continue
  1473	                gw = giacomini_white(
  1474	                    qlike_pointwise(po.actual, po.pred_aug),
  1475	                    qlike_pointwise(po.actual, po.pred_base),
  1476	                    horizon,
  1477	                )
  1478	                zs.append(gw["z_stat"])
  1479	            except (ValueError, AssertionError):
  1480	                fails += 1
  1481	        z = np.asarray(zs)
  1482	        n_ok = len(z)
  1483	        rej_5 = float(np.mean(z < POWER_GATE_Z)) if n_ok else float("nan")
  1484	        rej_h = float(np.mean(z < -3.0)) if n_ok else float("nan")
  1485	        rows.append(
  1486	            {
  1487	                "beta": b,
  1488	                "rv_uplift_per_1sd_shock_pct": round((math.exp(b) - 1) * 100, 2),
  1489	                "reps_completed": n_ok,
  1490	                "reps_failed": fails,
  1491	                "power_gw_one_sided_5pct": rej_5,
  1492	                "power_gw_se": (
  1493	                    float(math.sqrt(max(rej_5 * (1 - rej_5), 0) / n_ok)) if n_ok else None
  1494	                ),
  1495	                "power_gw_harvey_z_lt_minus3": rej_h,
  1496	                "median_gw_z": float(np.median(z)) if n_ok else None,
  1497	                "clip_rate_per_path": round(clips / max(n_ok, 1) / len(idx), 6),
  1498	            }
  1499	        )
  1500	
  1501	    size = next((r for r in rows if r["beta"] == 0.0), None)
  1502	
  1503	    def _first_at(power: float) -> dict | None:
  1504	        return next((r for r in rows if r["power_gw_one_sided_5pct"] >= power), None)
  1505	
  1506	    p80, p90 = _first_at(0.80), _first_at(0.90)
  1507	    return {
  1508	        "asset": asset,
  1509	        "horizon": horizon,
  1510	        "design": (
  1511	            "Semi-parametric DGP: calendar-day HAR+ctrl law of motion fitted on the "
  1512	            "real series, innovations drawn by circular moving-block bootstrap, real "
  1513	            "flow shocks and real returns retained, effect injected into the law of "
  1514	            "motion so it propagates through the HAR lags."
  1515	        ),
  1516	        "reps_per_beta": reps,
  1517	        "seed": SEED,
  1518	        "gate": f"GW z < {POWER_GATE_Z} (one-sided 5%), pre-specified",
  1519	        "dgp_persistence_phi_sum": round(dgp["persistence"], 4),
  1520	        "dgp_resid_sd": round(dgp["resid_sd"], 4),
  1521	        "false_positive_rate_at_beta_0": size["power_gw_one_sided_5pct"] if size else None,
  1522	        "false_positive_note": (
  1523	            "This is the rejection rate of the flow-favouring gate when the true "
  1524	            "effect is ZERO, and it must be read carefully -- it is NOT 'size' in "
  1525	            "the textbook sense. Under GW's method-level null with a fixed window, "
  1526	            "an irrelevant extra regressor makes the augmented METHOD genuinely "
  1527	            "worse: it pays an estimation cost and buys nothing, so "
  1528	            "E[L_aug - L_base] > 0 strictly. The one-sided flow-favouring test is "
  1529	            "therefore CONSERVATIVE at beta = 0 by construction, and a rate well "
  1530	            "below the nominal 5% is the expected, correct behaviour -- not a bug "
  1531	            "and not a calibration failure. What it does establish is the thing we "
  1532	            "need: this gate does not manufacture flow signals out of noise. A rate "
  1533	            "materially ABOVE 5% would have been the alarming result."
  1534	        ),
  1535	        "curve": rows,
  1536	        "beta_at_80pct_power": p80["beta"] if p80 else None,
  1537	        "rv_uplift_at_80pct_power_pct": (
  1538	            p80["rv_uplift_per_1sd_shock_pct"] if p80 else None
  1539	        ),
  1540	        "beta_at_90pct_power": p90["beta"] if p90 else None,
  1541	        "rv_uplift_at_90pct_power_pct": (
  1542	            p90["rv_uplift_per_1sd_shock_pct"] if p90 else None
  1543	        ),
  1544	        "max_beta_tested": max(betas),
  1545	        "max_uplift_tested_pct": round((math.exp(max(betas)) - 1) * 100, 2),
  1546	        "scope_warning": (
  1547	            "POWER IS NOT AN EXCLUSION. This says how often the gate fires against "
  1548	            "an effect of a given size. It does NOT say the true effect is smaller "
  1549	            "than the 80%-power point -- that inversion is exactly the error v1 "
  1550	            "made when it claimed to 'rule out >= +16% RV per 1-sd shock'. The only "
  1551	            "upper bound this study can defend is the material-gain exclusion test, "
  1552	            "and it lives in QLIKE-loss space. "
  1553	            "Also note this is PER-CELL power at the nominal 5% gate: the primary "
  1554	            "family additionally applies a Holm correction, which is stricter, so "
  1555	            "the family-wise design has LESS power than the curve below."
  1556	        ),
  1557	    }
  1558

  1560	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

  1565	        "family": rec.family,
  1566	        "cell": rec.cell,

  1568	        "horizon": rec.horizon,
  1569	        "base": rec.base,

  1572	        "estimand": rec.estimand,

  1577	        "qlike_improve_pct": (

  1581	    }
  1582	    if holm_p is not None:
  1583	        d["holm_adjusted_p"] = holm_p
  1584	    return d

  1587	def main() -> None:
  1588	    res: dict = {
  1589	        "experiment_id": "k1709",

  1600	            "RV are both known; target RV_{t+1..t+h} lies entirely ahead. State "

  1606	                "forecasting methods. Under the nested null the augmented method's "
  1607	                "extra estimated coefficients add pure forecast noise, so an "
  1608	                "ordinary loss-difference statistic is biased toward the smaller "
  1609	                "model and does not have its usual equal-accuracy reading."
  1610	            ),
  1611	            "claim_bearing_test": (
  1612	                "Giacomini-White (2006) equal unconditional predictive ability on "
  1613	                "Patton QLIKE, from a PAIRED FIXED ROLLING WINDOW (both specs share "
  1614	                "the augmented complete-case mask, the training dates, the embargo "

  1616	            ),

  1620	                "the ONLY test here that can license an upper bound on the effect."
  1621	            ),
  1622	            "diagnostic_only": (
  1623	                "Raw Diebold-Mariano (HAC, canonical bandwidth) and Clark-West are "
  1624	                "reported but tagged feeds_gate=false. Clark-West scores a DIFFERENT "
  1625	                "estimand (variance-level MSPE), so it is never relabelled as a "

  1633	            ),
  1634	        },
  1635	        "v1_defects_fixed": {
  1636	            "C1": "nested comparison no longer inferred with raw DM / mislabelled CW",
  1637	            "C2": "MDE replaced by a real power simulation + a real exclusion test",
  1638	            "C3": "US market holidays no longer counted as genuine zero-flow days",
  1639	            "C4": "state_lag and flow_lag are now separate",

  1659

  1661	    endo = {}
  1662	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
  1663	        j = pd.DataFrame(
  1664	            {

  1670	        endo[a] = {
  1671	            "corr_flow_vs_same_day_return": round(float(j["flow"].corr(j["ret"])), 4),
  1672	            "corr_flow_vs_same_day_logrv": round(

  1674	            ),

  1676	                float(j["flow"].abs().corr(np.log(j["rv"]))), 4
  1677	            ),
  1678	            "n": int(len(j)),
  1679	        }
  1680	    res["endogeneity_diagnostic"] = endo
  1681	    res["endogeneity_note"] = (
  1682	        "Flow is contemporaneously correlated with same-day return and volatility, "
  1683	        "which is exactly why a contemporaneous OLS of RV on flow would be "
  1684	        "uninterpretable. All predictive claims below are strictly out-of-sample "
  1685	        "and conditional on a HAR-RV baseline."
  1686	    )
  1687	
  1688	    btc_z_cal = feats["BTC"].z
  1689	
  1690	    panels: dict[tuple, Panel] = {}
  1691	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
  1692	        for h in HORIZONS:
  1693	            bz = btc_z_cal if a == "ETH" else None
  1694	            p = build_panel(rvs[a], feats[a], "rv_gk", h, a, flow_lag=1, btc_z=bz)
  1695	            assert_no_lookahead(p)
  1696	            panels[(a, h)] = p
  1697	            print(
  1698	                f"  panel {a} h={h}: n={len(p.df)} "
  1699	                f"{p.df.index.min().date()}..{p.df.index.max().date()}"
  1700	            )
  1701	
  1702	    # --- in-sample (descriptive) ---------------------------------------------
  1703	    insample = {}
  1704	    for (a, h), p in panels.items():
  1705	        for spec in ("HAR", "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", "H2_asym"):
  1706	            insample[f"{a}_h{h}_{spec}"] = in_sample(p, spec)
  1707	    res["in_sample"] = insample
  1708	    res["in_sample_note"] = (
  1709	        "Descriptive. The verdict rests on out-of-sample predictive ability, not "
  1710	        "on an in-sample coefficient."
  1711	    )
  1712	
  1713	    # --- PRIMARY family: H1 / H2 on both assets and horizons ------------------
  1714	    cells = []
  1715	    for (a, h), p in panels.items():
  1716	        for alt in ("H1_absflow", "H2_asym"):
  1717	            c = evaluate_cell(p, "HAR+ctrl", alt, family="primary")
  1718	            if c:
  1719	                cells.append(c)
  1720	
  1721	    # --- PRIMARY family: BTC flow -> ETH RV spillover -------------------------
  1722	    for h in HORIZONS:
  1723	        p = panels[("ETH", h)]
  1724	        pf = Panel(
  1725	            p.df.dropna(subset=["abs_z_btc"]), p.rv_col, h, "ETH",
  1726	            state_lag=p.state_lag, flow_lag=p.flow_lag,
  1727	        )
  1728	        c = evaluate_cell(pf, "H4_own", "H4_plus_btc", family="primary")
  1729	        if c:
  1730	            c["description"] = "BTC flow shock -> ETH RV, controlling ETH's own flow"
  1731	            cells.append(c)
  1732	    res["primary_cells"] = cells
  1733	
  1734	    # --- ROBUSTNESS: RV proxy ------------------------------------------------
  1735	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
  1736	        for col in ("rv_park", "rv_r2", "rv_hourly"):
  1737	            if rvs[a][col].notna().sum() < 300:
  1738	                continue
  1739	            p = build_panel(rvs[a], feats[a], col, 1, a, flow_lag=1)
  1740	            assert_no_lookahead(p)
  1741	            c = evaluate_cell(p, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", family="rv_proxy")
  1742	            if c:
  1743	                cells.append(c)
  1744	
  1745	    # --- ROBUSTNESS: conservative publication lag (C4 — flow only) ------------
  1746	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
  1747	        for h in HORIZONS:
  1748	            p = build_panel(rvs[a], feats[a], "rv_gk", h, a, flow_lag=2, state_lag=1)
  1749	            assert_no_lookahead(p)
  1750	            c = evaluate_cell(p, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", family="flow_lag2")
  1751	            if c:
  1752	                c["description"] = (
  1753	                    "flow_lag=2 (flow usable only at the end of t+1) while "
  1754	                    "state_lag stays 1: RV_{t+1} and ret_{t+1} ARE known by then. "
  1755	                    "v1 wrongly lagged the state controls too, handicapping its own "
  1756	                    "baseline."
  1757	                )
  1758	                cells.append(c)
  1759	
  1760	    # --- ROBUSTNESS: smearing (is the null an artifact of the log->level map?) -
  1761	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
  1762	        for h in HORIZONS:
  1763	            for sm in ("none", "shared"):
  1764	                c = evaluate_cell(
  1765	                    panels[(a, h)], "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow",
  1766	                    family=f"smearing_{sm}", smearing=sm,
  1767	                )
  1768	                if c:
  1769	                    cells.append(c)
  1770	
  1771	    # --- ROBUSTNESS: flow transforms (Warther 1995) ---------------------------
  1772	    CTRL = SPECS["HAR+ctrl"]
  1773	    TRANSFORMS = {
  1774	        "signed_z": "z_signed",
  1775	        "squared_z": "z_sq",
  1776	        "gross_churn_z": "abs_z_gross",
  1777	        "unexpected_z": "abs_z_unexp",
  1778	    }
  1779	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
  1780	        p = panels[(a, 1)]
  1781	        for label, col in TRANSFORMS.items():
  1782	            if col not in p.df.columns:
  1783	                continue
  1784	            SPECS[f"T_{label}"] = CTRL + [col]
  1785	            pt = Panel(
  1786	                p.df.dropna(subset=[col]), p.rv_col, 1, a,
  1787	                state_lag=p.state_lag, flow_lag=p.flow_lag,
  1788	            )
  1789	            c = evaluate_cell(pt, "HAR+ctrl", f"T_{label}", family="flow_transform")
  1790	            if c:
  1791	                c["transform"] = label
  1792	                c["in_sample_t"] = in_sample(pt, f"T_{label}")["coef"][col]["t"]
  1793	                cells.append(c)
  1794	
  1795	    # --- ROBUSTNESS: shock-threshold dummies ---------------------------------
  1796	    for (a, h), p in panels.items():
  1797	        for thr in (1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5):
  1798	            d = p.df.copy()
  1799	            d["abs_z"] = (d["abs_z"] >= thr).astype(float)   # shock DUMMY
  1800	            pt = Panel(d, p.rv_col, h, a, state_lag=p.state_lag, flow_lag=p.flow_lag)
  1801	            c = evaluate_cell(pt, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", family="threshold")
  1802	            if c:
  1803	                c["threshold"] = thr
  1804	                c["n_shock_days"] = int((p.df["abs_z"] >= thr).sum())
  1805	                cells.append(c)
  1806	
  1807	    # --- ROBUSTNESS: shorter ETH burn-in --------------------------------------
  1808	    for h in HORIZONS:
  1809	        c = evaluate_cell(
  1810	            panels[("ETH", h)], "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow",
  1811	            family="eth_window", train_window=200,
  1812	        )
  1813	        if c:
  1814	            cells.append(c)
  1815	
  1816	    res["all_cells"] = cells
  1817	
  1818	    # --- H3: Friday flow -> weekend RV (IN-SAMPLE, descriptive) ---------------
  1819	    h3 = {}
  1820	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
  1821	        p = build_panel(rvs[a], feats[a], "rv_gk", 2, a, flow_lag=1)   # Sat+Sun mean
  1822	        assert_no_lookahead(p)
  1823	        fri = p.df[p.df["dow_src"] == 4].copy()
  1824	        cols = SPECS["H1_absflow"]
  1825	        fri = fri.dropna(subset=cols)
  1826	        Xf = fri[cols].to_numpy(float)
  1827	        yf_ = np.log(fri["y"].to_numpy(float))
  1828	        b = _ols(Xf, yf_)
  1829	        se = _hac_se(Xf, yf_, b, max(1, canonical_bandwidth(1, len(fri))))
  1830	        names = ["const"] + cols
  1831	        i_z = names.index("abs_z")
  1832	        t_z = float(b[i_z] / se[i_z]) if se[i_z] > 0 else 0.0
  1833	        h3[a] = {
  1834	            "description": "Friday ETF flow -> mean GK RV over Sat+Sun (weekend gap)",
  1835	            "inference": "in-sample HAC t on the abs_z coefficient",
  1836	            "feeds_gate": False,
  1837	            "note": (
  1838	                "In-sample only. The study's claim is about out-of-sample predictive "
  1839	                "content, so this is descriptive and does not enter any verdict."
  1840	            ),
  1841	            "n_fridays": int(len(fri)),
  1842	            "coef": {
  1843	                nm: {
  1844	                    "beta": round(float(bb), 5),
  1845	                    "t": round(float(bb / ss) if ss > 0 else 0.0, 3),
  1846	                }
  1847	                for nm, bb, ss in zip(names, b, se)
  1848	            },
  1849	            "abs_z_t": round(t_z, 3),
  1850	            "abs_z_p_two_sided": float(
  1851	                2 * stats.t.sf(abs(t_z), df=max(len(fri) - len(names), 1))
  1852	            ),
  1853	        }
  1854	    res["h3_weekend_in_sample"] = h3
  1855	
  1856	    # --- Multiple testing, derived from the registry (C6) ----------------------
  1857	    gw_all = [
  1858	        r for r in REGISTRY if r.inference == "giacomini_white_qlike_fixed_window"
  1859	    ]
  1860	    gw_primary = [r for r in gw_all if r.family == "primary"]
  1861	    if not gw_primary:
  1862	        raise AssertionError("primary family is empty — nothing to adjudicate")
  1863	
  1864	    holm_primary = holm([r.p_one_sided for r in gw_primary])
  1865	    holm_full = holm([r.p_one_sided for r in gw_all])
  1866	
  1867	    by_cell = {c["cell"]: c for c in cells}
  1868	    excl_p = [
  1869	        by_cell[r.cell]["material_gain_exclusion"]["p_value_one_sided"]
  1870	        for r in gw_primary
  1871	    ]
  1872	    excl_z = [by_cell[r.cell]["material_gain_exclusion"]["z_stat"] for r in gw_primary]
  1873	    holm_excl = holm(excl_p)
  1874	
  1875	    primary_rows = []
  1876	    for r, hp, hz, hx in zip(gw_primary, holm_primary, excl_z, holm_excl):
  1877	        row = _serialize(r, holm_p=hp)
  1878	        row["passes_flow_gate"] = bool(
  1879	            r.qlike_improve_pct is not None
  1880	            and r.qlike_improve_pct > 0
  1881	            and r.stat < POWER_GATE_Z
  1882	            and hp < 0.05
  1883	        )
  1884	        row["excludes_material_gain"] = bool(hz > 0 and hx < 0.05)
  1885	        row["material_gain_exclusion_holm_p"] = hx
  1886	        ex = by_cell[r.cell]["material_gain_exclusion"]
  1887	        row["qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct"] = ex["qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct"]
  1888	        primary_rows.append(row)
  1889	
  1890	    # Simultaneous (Bonferroni) upper bounds: each cell's one-sided bound computed
  1891	    # at alpha/m so the whole family holds jointly at 95%. Conservative on purpose.
  1892	    m = len(gw_primary)
  1893	    simultaneous = [
  1894	        qlike_gain_upper_bound_from_cell(r.cell, alpha=0.05 / m) for r in gw_primary
  1895	    ]
  1896	    for row, b in zip(primary_rows, simultaneous):
  1897	        row["qlike_gain_upper_bound_simultaneous"] = b
  1898	    finite = [b for b in simultaneous if b is not None]
  1899	    # The family-wide statement is only as strong as its WEAKEST cell: a bound that
  1900	    # holds for 9 cells and fails for the 10th does not hold for the family.
  1901	    family_bound = max(finite) if len(finite) == m else None
  1902	
  1903	    n_pass = int(sum(r["passes_flow_gate"] for r in primary_rows))
  1904	    n_excl = int(sum(r["excludes_material_gain"] for r in primary_rows))
  1905	    all_excl = bool(primary_rows) and all(
  1906	        r["excludes_material_gain"] for r in primary_rows
  1907	    )
  1908	
  1909	    res["multiple_testing"] = {
  1910	        "registry_note": (
  1911	            "The families below are DERIVED from the in-code test registry, not "
  1912	            "hand-listed. v1 hand-listed 'EVERY DM test' and missed 8 of them. Holm "
  1913	            "runs on RAW p-values; rounding happens only at serialization."
  1914	        ),
  1915	        "n_tests_registered_total": len(REGISTRY),
  1916	        "n_gate_eligible_gw_tests": len(gw_all),
  1917	        "n_diagnostic_only_tests": len([r for r in REGISTRY if not r.feeds_gate]),
  1918	        "primary_family": primary_rows,
  1919	        "full_family_holm": [
  1920	            _serialize(r, holm_p=hp) for r, hp in zip(gw_all, holm_full)
  1921	        ],
  1922	        "n_full_family_holm_significant_at_05": int(
  1923	            sum(hp < 0.05 and r.stat < 0 for r, hp in zip(gw_all, holm_full))
  1924	        ),
  1925	    }
  1926	
  1927	    # --- Power (design sensitivity, NOT an exclusion) -------------------------
  1928	    print(
  1929	        f"\nrunning power simulation ({POWER_REPS} reps x "
  1930	        f"{len(POWER_BETAS)} betas x 2 assets)..."
  1931	    )
  1932	    res["power_simulation"] = {
  1933	        a: power_simulation(rvs[a], feats[a], a, horizon=1) for a in ("BTC", "ETH")
  1934	    }
  1935	
  1936	    # --- VERDICT --------------------------------------------------------------
  1937	    if n_pass > 0:
  1938	        verdict = "POSITIVE_INCREMENTAL_PREDICTIVE_CONTENT"
  1939	    elif all_excl:
  1940	        verdict = "BOUNDED_NULL_NO_MATERIAL_QLIKE_GAIN"
  1941	    else:
  1942	        verdict = "INCONCLUSIVE_NO_EXACT_NULL_CLAIM"
  1943	    res["verdict"] = verdict
  1944	
  1945	    margin_pct = 100 * MATERIAL_GAIN_MARGIN
  1946	    if verdict == "BOUNDED_NULL_NO_MATERIAL_QLIKE_GAIN":
  1947	        claim = (
  1948	            f"BOUNDED NULL. No cell shows incremental predictive content, and all "
  1949	            f"{n_excl}/{len(primary_rows)} primary cells REJECT the hypothesis that "
  1950	            f"adding ETF flow improves expected QLIKE by at least {margin_pct:.0f}% "
  1951	            f"(Holm-adjusted). Defensible claim: 'spot BTC/ETH ETF net flow buys no "
  1952	            f"material ({margin_pct:.0f}%+) QLIKE improvement over a HAR-RV baseline, "
  1953	            f"out of sample.' The bound lives in QLIKE-LOSS space. It is NOT a bound "
  1954	            f"on RV-uplift magnitude and NOT a proof of exact zero."
  1955	        )
  1956	    elif verdict == "INCONCLUSIVE_NO_EXACT_NULL_CLAIM":
  1957	        bound_txt = (
  1958	            f"The inverted one-sided 95% upper confidence bound on the relative "
  1959	            f"QLIKE gain is {family_bound:.1f}% simultaneously across all "
  1960	            f"{len(primary_rows)} cells (Bonferroni): gains LARGER than that are "
  1961	            f"excluded by the data; anything smaller is not."
  1962	            if family_bound is not None
  1963	            else (
  1964	                "The design cannot even exclude a 90% relative QLIKE gain in every "
  1965	                "cell, so NO upper bound can be stated. That is how little this "
  1966	                "sample can say about the effect size."
  1967	            )
  1968	        )
  1969	        claim = (
  1970	            f"INCONCLUSIVE. No cell shows incremental predictive content, but only "
  1971	            f"{n_excl}/{len(primary_rows)} primary cells can rule out the "
  1972	            f"pre-specified {margin_pct:.0f}% QLIKE gain, so the bounded null is NOT "
  1973	            f"established. Failure to reject equal accuracy is not evidence of "
  1974	            f"equality. The honest headline is: 'no robust incremental predictive "
  1975	            f"evidence was found for spot BTC/ETH ETF flow over a HAR-RV baseline' "
  1976	            f"-- a negative finding, not a proven zero. {bound_txt}"
  1977	        )
  1978	    else:
  1979	        claim = (
  1980	            f"POSITIVE. {n_pass}/{len(primary_rows)} primary cells clear the "
  1981	            f"pre-specified flow gate."
  1982	        )
  1983	
  1984	    res["verdict_basis"] = {
  1985	        "test": (
  1986	            "Giacomini-White (2006) equal unconditional predictive ability "
  1987	            "(one-sided, flow-favouring), Holm-adjusted across the primary family"
  1988	        ),
  1989	        "loss": "Patton QLIKE on the variance level",
  1990	        "estimation_scheme": (
  1991	            f"paired fixed rolling window of {GW_TRAIN_WINDOW} flow days; both specs "
  1992	            "share the augmented complete-case mask, the training dates and the "
  1993	            "forward-label embargo (y_end_date < forecast origin)"
  1994	        ),
  1995	        "gate": f"qlike_improve > 0 AND GW z < {POWER_GATE_Z} AND Holm p < 0.05",
  1996	        "cells_in_primary_family": len(primary_rows),
  1997	        "cells_passing_flow_gate": n_pass,
  1998	        "cells_excluding_material_gain": n_excl,
  1999	        "material_gain_margin_pct": margin_pct,
  2000	        "qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct_per_cell": {
  2001	            row["cell"]: row["qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct"] for row in primary_rows
  2002	        },
  2003	        "qlike_gain_upper_bound_family_simultaneous_pct": family_bound,
  2004	        "upper_bound_method": (
  2005	            "Inverted one-sided exclusion test (Bonferroni alpha/m across the "
  2006	            f"{m} primary cells). This -- not the power curve -- is the object that "
  2007	            "can bound an effect. It lives in QLIKE-LOSS space, not RV-uplift space."
  2008	        ),
  2009	        "claim_strength": claim,
  2010	        "withdrawn_v1_claim": (
  2011	            "v1 claimed it could 'rule out an RV uplift of >= +16.2% per 1-sd flow "
  2012	            "shock'. That number came from reading a single-path power curve "
  2013	            "backwards. Power is not an exclusion. The claim is WITHDRAWN and is not "
  2014	            "replaced by an RV-space bound of any size."
  2015	        ),
  2016	        "four_way_alignment": (
  2017	            "test = Giacomini-White | loss = Patton QLIKE | scheme = paired fixed "
  2018	            "rolling window | claim = bounded in QLIKE-loss space only. These four "
  2019	            "match by construction; v1's did not."
  2020	        ),
  2021	    }
  2022	
  2023	    make_plots(flows, rvs, panels, res)
  2024	
  2025	    tmp = OUT / "k1709_results.json.tmp"
  2026	    with open(tmp, "w") as fh:
  2027	        json.dump(res, fh, indent=2, default=str)
  2028	    with open(tmp) as fh:
  2029	        json.load(fh)                      # parse before replacing
  2030	    os.replace(tmp, OUT / "k1709_results.json")
  2031	
  2032	    print("\n" + "=" * 78)
  2033	    print("PRIMARY FAMILY (paired fixed-window Giacomini-White on QLIKE):")
  2034	    for r in primary_rows:
  2035	        print(
  2036	            f"  {r['cell']:26s} QLIKE {r['qlike_improve_pct']:+7.3f}%  "
  2037	            f"GW z={r['stat']:+6.2f}  Holm p={r['holm_adjusted_p']:.3f}  "
  2038	            f"excl({margin_pct:.0f}%)={'Y' if r['excludes_material_gain'] else 'n'}"
  2039	        )
  2040	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
  2041	        pw = res["power_simulation"][a]
  2042	        b80 = pw["rv_uplift_at_80pct_power_pct"]
  2043	        print(
  2044	            f"  power[{a}]: false-positive rate at beta=0 = "
  2045	            f"{pw['false_positive_rate_at_beta_0']:.3f}  |  80% power at RV uplift = "
  2046	            + (f"+{b80}%" if b80 is not None else
  2047	               f"NOT REACHED even at +{pw['max_uplift_tested_pct']}%")
  2048	        )
  2049	    print(f"\nVERDICT: {verdict}")
  2050	    print(claim)
  2051	
  2052	
  2053	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  2054	# 12. Figures
  2055	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

     3	The v1 suite passed 26/26 while the experiment was making two CRITICAL inference
     4	errors, so "the tests are green" was never the issue -- they tested the plumbing
     5	and not the claim. This suite adds the checks the 2026-07-14 independent review
     6	said were missing:
     7	
     8	  * the calendar precondition every `.shift()` silently assumes
     9	    (`test_deleting_a_sunday_is_rejected`)
    10	  * that nothing already computed can be moved by data arriving later
    11	    (`test_future_data_cannot_move_a_past_forecast`)
    12	  * that US market holidays are not counted as genuine zero-flow days
    13	    (`test_session_filter_*`)
    14	  * that the flow lag and the state lag move independently
    15	    (`test_state_lag_and_flow_lag_are_independent`)
    16	  * that raw Diebold-Mariano cannot reach a verdict
    17	    (`test_raw_dm_never_feeds_a_gate`,
    18	     `test_repo_nested_dm_auditor_marks_this_file_safe`)
    19	  * that power is never reported as an exclusion
    20	    (`test_power_is_not_reported_as_an_exclusion`)
    21	
    22	Run:  uv run --extra dev python -m pytest experiments/k1709/test_k1709.py -q
    23	"""
    24	
    25	from __future__ import annotations

    68	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    69	# Synthetic world — a complete 24/7 RV calendar, flows only on NYSE sessions
    70	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    71	def _make_world(
    72	    n_days: int = 900,
    73	    start: str = "2023-01-02",
    74	    seed: int = SEED,
    75	    flow_beta: float = 0.0,
    76	) -> tuple[pd.DataFrame, pd.DataFrame]:
    77	    """`flow_beta` injects a KNOWN |z| effect into log RV, so the same helper
    78	    drives both the placebo tests (beta = 0) and the power tests (beta large)."""
    79	    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    80	    cal = pd.date_range(start, periods=n_days, freq="D")
    81	    sessions = us_equity_sessions(cal.min(), cal.max())
    82	    fdays = cal.intersection(sessions)
    83	
    84	    flow = pd.Series(rng.normal(0, 300, len(fdays)), index=fdays)
    85	    shock = flow_zscore(flow).abs().reindex(cal).shift(1).fillna(0.0).to_numpy()
    86	
    87	    lrv = np.empty(n_days)
    88	    lrv[:HAR_M] = -9.0 + rng.normal(0, 0.3, HAR_M)
    89	    e = rng.normal(0, 0.45, n_days)
    90	    for d in range(HAR_M, n_days):
    91	        lrv[d] = (
    92	            -9.0 * 0.15
    93	            + 0.40 * lrv[d - 1]
    94	            + 0.30 * lrv[d - 5 : d].mean()
    95	            + 0.15 * lrv[d - HAR_M : d].mean()
    96	            + flow_beta * shock[d]
    97	            + e[d]
    98	        )
    99	    rv = pd.DataFrame(
   100	        {"rv_gk": np.exp(lrv), "ret": rng.normal(0, 0.03, n_days)}, index=cal
   101	    )
   102	    flow_df = pd.DataFrame({"flow": flow, "gross": flow.abs() + 100.0}, index=fdays)
   103	    return rv, flow_df
   104	
   105	
   106	@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
   107	def world():
   108	    return _make_world()
   109	
   110	
   111	@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
   112	def panel(world):
   113	    rv, flow = world
   114	    p = build_panel(rv, make_flow_features(flow), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN", flow_lag=1)
   115	    assert_no_lookahead(p)
   116	    return p
   117

   119	@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
   120	def _clean_registry():
   121	    """The registry is module-level state; a leaked record would corrupt the
   122	    next test's family count."""
   123	    REGISTRY.clear()
   124	    yield
   125	    REGISTRY.clear()

   128	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   129	# 1. Farside parsing — missing is not zero
   130	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   131	@pytest.mark.parametrize(
   132	    "raw,expected",
   133	    [
   134	        ("(95.1)", -95.1),
   135	        ("(27,332)", -27332.0),
   136	        ("1,234.5", 1234.5),
   137	        ("0.0", 0.0),
   138	        ("$12.3", 12.3),
   139	    ],
   140	)
   141	def test_parse_money_values(raw, expected):
   142	    assert _parse_money(raw) == pytest.approx(expected)
   143	
   144	
   145	@pytest.mark.parametrize("raw", ["-", "–", "—", "", "nan"])
   146	def test_parse_money_missing_is_nan_not_zero(raw):
   147	    assert np.isnan(_parse_money(raw))
   148	
   149	
   150	def test_zero_and_missing_are_distinct():
   151	    assert _parse_money("0.0") == 0.0
   152	    assert np.isnan(_parse_money("-"))

   155	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   156	# 2. C3 — US market holidays are MISSING, not zero-flow days
   157	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   158	def test_session_calendar_excludes_nyse_holidays():
   159	    sess = us_equity_sessions(pd.Timestamp("2024-01-01"), pd.Timestamp("2024-12-31"))
   160	    for holiday in (
   161	        "2024-01-15",  # MLK
   162	        "2024-02-19",  # Presidents Day
   163	        "2024-03-29",  # Good Friday
   164	        "2024-05-27",  # Memorial Day
   165	        "2024-06-19",  # Juneteenth
   166	        "2024-11-28",  # Thanksgiving
   167	        "2024-12-25",  # Christmas
   168	    ):
   169	        assert pd.Timestamp(holiday) not in sess, holiday
   170	
   171	
   172	def test_session_calendar_is_nyse_not_the_federal_holiday_list():
   173	    """Columbus Day and Veterans Day are FEDERAL holidays -- the NYSE is OPEN.
   174	
   175	    A federal-holiday calendar would silently delete two real flow days a year.
   176	    This is the test that tells the two calendars apart.
   177	    """
   178	    sess = us_equity_sessions(pd.Timestamp("2024-01-01"), pd.Timestamp("2024-12-31"))
   179	    assert pd.Timestamp("2024-10-14") in sess     # Columbus Day
   180	    assert pd.Timestamp("2024-11-11") in sess     # Veterans Day
   181	
   182	
   183	def test_session_filter_drops_holiday_rows_that_look_like_genuine_zeros():
   184	    """The exact trap: an all-dash holiday row sums to 0.0 under skipna, so
   185	    Farside's Total and the recomputed sum agree and the parser cross-check is
   186	    blind to it."""
   187	    idx = pd.DatetimeIndex(["2024-01-12", "2024-01-15", "2024-01-16"])  # Fri/MLK/Tue
   188	    df = pd.DataFrame(
   189	        {
   190	            "flow": [120.0, 0.0, -80.0],          # the middle 0.0 is fake
   191	            "gross": [500.0, 0.0, 400.0],
   192	            "all_dash": [False, True, False],
   193	        },
   194	        index=idx,
   195	    )
   196	    kept, diag = filter_to_sessions(df)
   197	
   198	    assert pd.Timestamp("2024-01-15") not in kept.index
   199	    assert len(kept) == 2
   200	    assert diag["n_nonsession_rows_dropped"] == 1
   201	    assert diag["n_nonsession_rows_with_nonzero_total"] == 0
   202	    assert diag["nonsession_rows_dropped"][0]["all_fund_columns_dash"] is True
   203	
   204	
   205	def test_session_filter_keeps_a_genuine_zero_on_an_open_market_day():
   206	    """Missing is not zero -- but zero is not missing either. A real zero-flow
   207	    day on an open market must survive."""
   208	    idx = pd.DatetimeIndex(["2024-01-12", "2024-01-16"])
   209	    df = pd.DataFrame(
   210	        {"flow": [0.0, -80.0], "gross": [0.0, 400.0], "all_dash": [False, False]},
   211	        index=idx,
   212	    )
   213	    kept, diag = filter_to_sessions(df)
   214	    assert len(kept) == 2
   215	    assert kept.loc[pd.Timestamp("2024-01-12"), "flow"] == 0.0
   216	    assert diag["n_nonsession_rows_dropped"] == 0
   217	
   218	
   219	def test_holiday_zeros_would_have_inflated_every_shock():
   220	    """Show the damage rather than asserting the rows exist. Fake zeros shrink the
   221	    20-day rolling sd, which INFLATES every |z| that follows -- that is the
   222	    channel through which C3 moved the test statistics."""
   223	    rng = np.random.default_rng(7)
   224	    fdays = us_equity_sessions(pd.Timestamp("2024-01-02"), pd.Timestamp("2024-12-31"))
   225	    clean = pd.Series(rng.normal(0, 300, len(fdays)), index=fdays)
   226	    holidays = pd.DatetimeIndex(["2024-01-15", "2024-02-19", "2024-03-29"])
   227	    polluted = pd.concat([clean, pd.Series(0.0, index=holidays)]).sort_index()
   228	
   229	    assert flow_zscore(polluted).abs().mean() > flow_zscore(clean).abs().mean()
   230

   232	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   233	# 3. Lookahead — the calendar precondition and the future-mutation invariant
   234	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   235	def test_deleting_a_sunday_is_rejected(world):
   236	    """Codex's test. `.shift(1)` moves by ROW POSITION, not by a day.
   237	
   238	    Punch one Sunday out and every later row silently becomes a t+2 forecast
   239	    wearing a t+1 label. v1's `assert_no_lookahead` re-derived its gap from the
   240	    very src_date the shift produced, so it could be fooled. The precondition
   241	    check cannot be.
   242	    """
   243	    rv, flow = world
   244	    sunday = next(d for d in rv.index if d.dayofweek == 6 and d > rv.index[100])
   245	    holed = rv.drop(index=sunday)
   246	
   247	    with pytest.raises(AssertionError, match="hole"):
   248	        build_panel(holed, make_flow_features(flow), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN")
   249	
   250	
   251	def test_assert_calendar_rejects_duplicates_and_disorder():
   252	    with pytest.raises(AssertionError, match="duplicate"):
   253	        assert_calendar_is_complete(
   254	            pd.DatetimeIndex(["2024-01-01", "2024-01-02", "2024-01-02"]), "dup"
   255	        )
   256	    with pytest.raises(AssertionError, match="sorted"):
   257	        assert_calendar_is_complete(
   258	            pd.DatetimeIndex(["2024-01-03", "2024-01-01", "2024-01-02"]), "unsorted"
   259	        )
   260	
   261	
   262	def test_future_data_cannot_move_a_past_forecast(world):
   263	    """Codex's test, and the strongest lookahead check there is: mutate the future
   264	    and demand that nothing already computed moves.
   265	
   266	    A src_date assertion can be satisfied by a leak that never touches src_date --
   267	    Codex demonstrated this by copying `y` straight into `z` and watching v1's
   268	    assertion still pass. This test cannot be fooled that way, because it never
   269	    asks the code where the data came from. It asks whether the answer changed.
   270	    """
   271	    rv, flow = world
   272	    p0 = build_panel(rv, make_flow_features(flow), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN")
   273	    base = paired_oos(p0, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow")
   274	    assert len(base.actual) > GW_MIN_LOSSES
   275	
   276	    cut = base.origins[len(base.origins) // 2]
   277	
   278	    rv_mut = rv.copy()
   279	    rv_mut.loc[rv_mut.index >= cut, "rv_gk"] *= 100.0      # blow up the future
   280	    flow_mut = flow.copy()
   281	    flow_mut.loc[flow_mut.index >= cut, "flow"] *= -50.0
   282	
   283	    p1 = build_panel(rv_mut, make_flow_features(flow_mut), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN")
   284	    mut = paired_oos(p1, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow")
   285	
   286	    keep0, keep1 = base.origins < cut, mut.origins < cut
   287	    assert keep0.sum() > 50
   288	    assert (base.origins[keep0] == mut.origins[keep1]).all()
   289	
   290	    np.testing.assert_allclose(base.pred_base[keep0], mut.pred_base[keep1], atol=1e-12)
   291	    np.testing.assert_allclose(base.pred_aug[keep0], mut.pred_aug[keep1], atol=1e-12)
   292	
   293	
   294	def test_flow_zscore_is_invariant_to_future_values(world):
   295	    _, flow = world
   296	    z0 = flow_zscore(flow["flow"])
   297	    mutated = flow["flow"].copy()
   298	    mutated.iloc[-30:] *= 99.0
   299	    pd.testing.assert_series_equal(z0.iloc[:-30], flow_zscore(mutated).iloc[:-30])
   300	
   301	
   302	def test_flow_zscore_denominator_excludes_own_day():
   303	    f = pd.Series(np.arange(1.0, 61.0), index=pd.date_range("2024-01-01", periods=60))
   304	    z = flow_zscore(f)
   305	    i = 40
   306	    assert z.iloc[i] == pytest.approx(f.iloc[i] / f.iloc[i - 20 : i].std())
   307	
   308	
   309	def test_unexpected_flow_ar5_lag_ordering():
   310	    """The AR design matrix is [lag1..lag5], so the prediction vector must be
   311	    f[i-1]..f[i-5]. Reversing it silently multiplies the lag-5 coefficient by the
   312	    lag-1 value. On a pure AR(1) the residual must collapse to ~0."""
   313	    n = 300
   314	    rng = np.random.default_rng(3)
   315	    f = np.zeros(n)
   316	    for i in range(1, n):
   317	        f[i] = 0.8 * f[i - 1] + rng.normal(0, 0.01)
   318	    s = pd.Series(f, index=pd.date_range("2024-01-01", periods=n))
   319	    assert ar_residual(s, p=5).dropna().abs().mean() < 0.05
   320	
   321	
   322	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   323	# 4. C4 — the state lag and the flow lag move independently
   324	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   325	def test_state_lag_and_flow_lag_are_independent(world):
   326	    """v1's `pub_lag=2` run also pushed HAR and the return controls back a day.
   327	
   328	    But if the flow is only usable at the end of t+1, RV_{t+1} and ret_{t+1} are
   329	    plainly already known. Lagging them too handicapped v1's own baseline and made
   330	    the robustness run answer a question nobody asked.
   331	    """
   332	    rv, flow = world
   333	    ff = make_flow_features(flow)
   334	    p = build_panel(rv, ff, "rv_gk", 1, "SYN", flow_lag=2, state_lag=1)
   335	    assert_no_lookahead(p)
   336	
   337	    assert (p.df.index - p.df["state_src_date"]).dt.days.eq(1).all()
   338	    assert (p.df.index - p.df["flow_src_date"]).dt.days.eq(2).all()
   339	
   340	    tau = p.df.index[80]
   341	    lr = np.log(rv["rv_gk"])
   342	    assert p.df.loc[tau, "har_d"] == pytest.approx(lr.loc[tau - pd.Timedelta(days=1)])
   343	    assert p.df.loc[tau, "z"] == pytest.approx(
   344	        ff.z.reindex(rv.index).loc[tau - pd.Timedelta(days=2)]
   345	    )
   346	
   347	
   348	def test_baseline_lags_are_one_and_one(panel):
   349	    assert (panel.df.index - panel.df["state_src_date"]).dt.days.eq(1).all()
   350	    assert (panel.df.index - panel.df["flow_src_date"]).dt.days.eq(1).all()
   351	
   352	
   353	def test_assert_no_lookahead_catches_a_lag_mixup(world):
   354	    rv, flow = world
   355	    p = build_panel(rv, make_flow_features(flow), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN")
   356	    bad = p.df.copy()
   357	    bad.iloc[5, bad.columns.get_loc("flow_src_date")] -= pd.Timedelta(days=1)
   358	    with pytest.raises(AssertionError, match="flow source->target gap"):
   359	        assert_no_lookahead(Panel(bad, p.rv_col, p.horizon, p.asset, 1, 1))
   360	
   361	
   362	def test_h5_target_is_the_mean_of_the_next_five_days(world):
   363	    rv, flow = world
   364	    p = build_panel(rv, make_flow_features(flow), "rv_gk", 5, "SYN")
   365	    assert_no_lookahead(p)
   366	    tau = p.df.index[60]
   367	    window = rv["rv_gk"].loc[tau : tau + pd.Timedelta(days=4)]
   368	    assert len(window) == 5
   369	    assert p.df.loc[tau, "y"] == pytest.approx(window.mean())
   370	    assert p.df.loc[tau, "y_end_date"] == tau + pd.Timedelta(days=4)
   371	
   372	
   373	def test_h5_training_labels_close_before_the_forecast_origin(world):
   374	    """The forward-label embargo: with overlapping 5-day targets, "every row before
   375	    i" would train on a label window overlapping the forecast day."""
   376	    rv, flow = world
   377	    p = build_panel(rv, make_flow_features(flow), "rv_gk", 5, "SYN")
   378	    po = paired_oos(p, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow")
   379	    assert po.audit["embargo_ok"]
   380	    assert po.audit["min_origin_minus_last_train_label_end_days"] >= 1
   381

   386	def test_repo_nested_dm_auditor_marks_this_file_safe():
   387	    """The project's own mechanical gate, mirrored here so it fails loudly and
   388	    locally. `scripts/tests/test_nested_dm_misuse_ratchet.py` FAILED on v1.
   389	    """
   390	    from audit_nested_dm_misuse import scan_file
   390	    from audit_nested_dm_misuse import scan_file
   391	
   392	    root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
   393	    finding = scan_file(Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "k1709.py", root)
   394	    assert finding is not None
   395	    assert finding.test_role == "diagnostic_with_cw_primary"
   396	    assert finding.safe_role_evidence

   399	def test_raw_dm_never_feeds_a_gate(panel):
   400	    evaluate_cell(panel, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", family="unit")
   401	    by_inf = {r.inference: r for r in REGISTRY}
   402	    assert by_inf["giacomini_white_qlike_fixed_window"].feeds_gate is True
   403	    assert by_inf["raw_dm_qlike"].feeds_gate is False
   404	    assert by_inf["clark_west_mspe"].feeds_gate is False

   407	def test_clark_west_is_not_relabelled_as_a_qlike_test(panel):
   408	    """v1's CRITICAL #1. The CW helper scores variance-level squared error, but v1
   409	    presented it as the nested-correct test for a QLIKE loss and let it carry the
   410	    NULL on its own."""
   411	    c = evaluate_cell(panel, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", family="unit")
   412	    cw = c["clark_west_mspe_separate_estimand"]
   413	    assert "MSPE" in cw["estimand"]
   414	    assert cw["feeds_gate"] is False
   415	    assert c["primary_inference_gw_qlike"]["loss"] == "Patton QLIKE"
   416	
   417	
   418	def test_gw_requires_strict_nesting(panel):
   419	    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="strict nesting"):
   420	        paired_oos(panel, "H1_absflow", "HAR+ctrl")      # backwards
   421	    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="strict nesting"):
   422	        paired_oos(panel, "HAR+ctrl", "HAR+ctrl")        # not proper
   423	
   424	
   425	def test_gw_uses_a_fixed_window_with_shared_training_dates(panel):
   426	    """The scheme is what makes GW legal under nesting -- not the formula."""
   427	    po = paired_oos(panel, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow")
   428	    assert po.audit["scheme"] == "fixed_rolling"
   429	    assert po.audit["fixed_window_held"] is True
   430	    assert po.audit["train_window"] == GW_TRAIN_WINDOW
   431	    assert po.audit["same_training_dates_for_both_models"] is True
   432	
   433	
   434	def test_expanding_window_is_reported_but_flagged_invalid(panel):
   435	    c = evaluate_cell(panel, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", family="unit")
   436	    exp = c["expanding_window_diagnostic_v1_design"]
   437	    assert exp["valid_for_nested_inference"] is False
   438	    assert exp["feeds_gate"] is False
   439	    assert exp["scheme"].startswith("expanding")
   440	
   441	
   442	def test_gw_direction_negative_z_favours_the_flow_model():
   443	    """Sign convention. Getting it backwards would report "flow works" exactly
   444	    when the data says the opposite."""
   445	    rng = np.random.default_rng(11)
   446	    loss_base = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.2, 400))
   447	    gw = giacomini_white(loss_base - 0.10, loss_base, h=1)   # augmented is BETTER
   448	    assert gw["z_stat"] < 0
   449	    assert gw["p_value_one_sided_flow_better"] < 0.01
   450	
   451	
   452	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   453	# 6. C2 — a real exclusion test, and power that is actually power
   454	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   455	def test_material_gain_exclusion_rejects_when_there_is_no_gain():
   456	    rng = np.random.default_rng(5)
   457	    loss_base = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.15, 400))
   458	    loss_aug = loss_base + rng.normal(0, 1e-4, 400)          # no gain
   459	    ex = material_gain_exclusion(loss_aug, loss_base, h=1, margin=0.01)
   460	    assert ex["z_stat"] > 0
   461	    assert ex["p_value_one_sided"] < 0.01
   462	
   463	
   464	def test_material_gain_exclusion_does_not_reject_when_the_gain_is_real():
   465	    """The other direction. Without this the exclusion test could be a rubber
   466	    stamp that always says "no material gain", and the bounded null would be
   467	    vacuous."""
   468	    rng = np.random.default_rng(6)
   469	    loss_base = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.15, 400))
   470	    ex = material_gain_exclusion(loss_base * 0.95, loss_base, h=1, margin=0.01)
   471	    assert ex["p_value_one_sided"] > 0.05                    # a real 5% gain
   472	
   473	
   474	def test_exclusion_margin_is_the_prespecified_one():
   475	    assert MATERIAL_GAIN_MARGIN == 0.01
   476	
   477	
   478	def test_inverted_upper_bound_brackets_the_true_gain():
   479	    """The bound is the ONLY object here that can legitimately bound the effect.
   480	
   481	    Plant a known 5% gain: the one-sided 95% upper bound must sit ABOVE it (a bound
   482	    that excluded the truth would be wrong) and must still be finite (a bound that
   483	    excluded nothing would be useless).
   484	    """
   485	    rng = np.random.default_rng(21)
   486	    loss_base = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.10, 800))
   487	    bound = qlike_gain_upper_bound(loss_base * 0.95, loss_base, h=1, alpha=0.05)
   488	    assert bound is not None
   489	    assert bound > 5.0            # does not exclude the true 5% gain
   490	    assert bound < 20.0           # but still says something
   491	
   492	
   493	def test_inverted_upper_bound_is_tight_when_there_is_no_gain():
   494	    rng = np.random.default_rng(22)
   495	    loss_base = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.10, 800))
   496	    bound = qlike_gain_upper_bound(
   497	        loss_base + rng.normal(0, 1e-4, 800), loss_base, h=1, alpha=0.05
   498	    )
   499	    assert bound is not None
   500	    assert bound < 3.0            # near-zero gain -> a tight bound
   501	
   502	
   503	def test_inverted_upper_bound_returns_none_when_nothing_can_be_excluded():
   504	    """A design that cannot exclude even a 90% gain must SAY so, not emit a number.
   505	
   506	    Here the augmented model really is ~95% better, so a 90% gain is genuinely NOT
   507	    excludable and the honest answer is "no bound". Emitting a number instead would
   508	    be the same class of error as v1's: manufacturing a bound the data cannot carry.
   509	    """
   510	    rng = np.random.default_rng(23)
   511	    loss_base = np.abs(rng.normal(1.0, 0.2, 300))
   512	    assert qlike_gain_upper_bound(loss_base * 0.05, loss_base, h=1, alpha=0.05) is None
   513	
   514	
   515	def test_power_rises_with_the_effect_and_does_not_fire_on_noise():
   516	    """v1's "MDE" injected each beta ONCE into the single realised noise path and
   517	    took the first crossing: no repeated sampling, no size calibration, and a
   518	    curve that was not even monotone (its BTC CW fell from 1.713 at beta=.15 to
   519	    1.623 at beta=.8).
   520	
   521	    A real power curve must (a) not fire when the effect is zero and (b) rise with
   522	    the effect. Note (a) is asserted as an UPPER bound only: under GW's
   523	    method-level null a useless extra regressor makes the augmented method
   524	    genuinely worse, so the flow-favouring one-sided gate is conservative at
   525	    beta=0 by construction. A rate far BELOW 5% is correct behaviour; a rate above
   526	    it would be the alarm.
   527	    """
   528	    rv, flow = _make_world(n_days=800)
   529	    pw = power_simulation(
   530	        rv, make_flow_features(flow), "SYN", horizon=1, reps=60,
   531	        betas=(0.0, 0.30, 0.80),
   532	    )
   533	    curve = {r["beta"]: r["power_gw_one_sided_5pct"] for r in pw["curve"]}
   534	    assert curve[0.0] <= 0.10                # never manufactures a flow signal
   535	    assert curve[0.80] > curve[0.0]
   536	    assert curve[0.80] > 0.50
   537	    assert pw["false_positive_rate_at_beta_0"] == curve[0.0]
   538	    assert "CONSERVATIVE at beta = 0" in pw["false_positive_note"]
   539	
   540	
   541	def test_a_planted_effect_is_recovered_end_to_end():
   542	    """A null is only credible if the pipeline could have found a signal. Plant one
   543	    in the DGP and require the pre-specified gate to fire on the real estimator."""
   544	    rv, flow = _make_world(n_days=900, flow_beta=0.9)
   545	    p = build_panel(rv, make_flow_features(flow), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN")
   546	    assert_no_lookahead(p)
   547	    c = evaluate_cell(p, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", family="unit")
   548	    assert c["qlike_improvement_pct"] > 0
   549	    assert c["primary_inference_gw_qlike"]["z_stat"] < -1.645
   550	
   551	
   552	def test_placebo_scrambled_flow_is_not_detected(world):
   553	    """Destroy the flow/RV correspondence and the gate must go quiet."""
   554	    rv, flow = world
   555	    rng = np.random.default_rng(99)
   556	    scrambled = flow.copy()
   557	    scrambled["flow"] = rng.permutation(scrambled["flow"].to_numpy())
   558	    p = build_panel(rv, make_flow_features(scrambled), "rv_gk", 1, "SYN")
   559	    c = evaluate_cell(p, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", family="unit")
   560	    assert c["primary_inference_gw_qlike"]["z_stat"] > -1.645
   561	
   562	
   563	def test_power_is_not_reported_as_an_exclusion():
   564	    """v1's logic error, pinned so it cannot come back: power says how often the
   565	    gate fires against an effect of a given size. It does NOT bound the effect."""
   566	    rv, flow = _make_world(n_days=700)
   567	    pw = power_simulation(
   568	        rv, make_flow_features(flow), "SYN", horizon=1, reps=20, betas=(0.0, 0.5)
   569	    )
   570	    assert "POWER IS NOT AN EXCLUSION" in pw["scope_warning"]

   573	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

   576	def test_holm_step_down_is_correct():

   578	    assert adj[0] == pytest.approx(0.03)      # 3 * 0.01
   579	    assert adj == sorted(adj)                 # step-down enforces monotonicity
   580	    assert all(0.0 <= a <= 1.0 for a in adj)

   583	def test_holm_on_raw_p_can_differ_from_holm_on_rounded_p():

   585	    verdict, so the raw values must survive all the way to serialization."""
   586	    raw = [0.0166667, 0.4, 0.9]
   587	    rounded = [round(p, 4) for p in raw]

   606	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   607	# 8. Estimator integrity
   608	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   609	def test_normal_equations_match_lstsq(panel):
   610	    """The OOS loop solves the normal equations for speed (the power simulation
   611	    runs it ~16k times). It must be numerically identical to the SVD solution, or
   612	    the simulation and the headline would be using different estimators."""
   613	    d = panel.df.dropna(subset=SPECS["H1_absflow"])
   614	    X = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(d)), d[SPECS["H1_absflow"]].to_numpy(float)])
   615	    y = np.log(d["y"].to_numpy(float))
   616	    for start in (0, 100, 200):
   617	        xtr = X[start : start + GW_TRAIN_WINDOW]
   618	        ytr = y[start : start + GW_TRAIN_WINDOW]
   619	        np.testing.assert_allclose(
   620	            np.linalg.solve(xtr.T @ xtr, xtr.T @ ytr),
   621	            np.linalg.lstsq(xtr, ytr, rcond=None)[0],
   622	            rtol=1e-7,
   623	            atol=1e-9,
   624	        )
   625	
   626	
   627	def test_ols_helper_agrees_with_the_oos_loop(panel):
   628	    d = panel.df.dropna(subset=SPECS["HAR+ctrl"])
   629	    X = d[SPECS["HAR+ctrl"]].to_numpy(float)
   630	    y = np.log(d["y"].to_numpy(float))
   631	    Xc = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(X)), X])
   632	    np.testing.assert_allclose(
   633	        _ols(X, y), np.linalg.solve(Xc.T @ Xc, Xc.T @ y), rtol=1e-7, atol=1e-9
   634	    )
   635	
   636	
   637	def test_smearing_modes_produce_different_forecasts(panel):
   638	    """If none/own/shared all returned the same numbers, the smearing robustness
   639	    family would be theatre."""
   640	    own = paired_oos(panel, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", smearing="own")
   641	    none = paired_oos(panel, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", smearing="none")
   642	    shared = paired_oos(panel, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow", smearing="shared")
   643	
   644	    assert not np.allclose(own.pred_aug, none.pred_aug)
   645	    assert not np.allclose(own.pred_aug, shared.pred_aug)
   646	    # 'shared' only overrides the AUGMENTED spec's multiplier
   647	    np.testing.assert_allclose(own.pred_base, shared.pred_base, rtol=1e-12)
   648	
   649	
   650	def test_forecasts_are_positive_variances(panel):
   651	    po = paired_oos(panel, "HAR+ctrl", "H1_absflow")
   652	    assert (po.pred_base > 0).all()
   653	    assert (po.pred_aug > 0).all()
   654	    assert (po.actual > 0).all()

   507	        yhat = float(x_all[i] @ beta)

   510	            yhat = min(max(yhat, lo), hi)
