   553	# ── Raw DM diagnostics (never used by the nested-model verdict) ───────────────
   554	
   555	
   556	def _nw_dm(d: np.ndarray, max_lag: int) -> float:
   557	    """Newey-West DM t-stat at an explicit bandwidth.
   558	
   559	    Used ONLY for lag-sensitivity reporting.  `dm_parity_check` asserts it
   560	    reproduces the canonical dm_test at the canonical bandwidth, so the canonical
   561	    helper remains the single source of truth for every headline number.
   562	    """
   563	    d = d[np.isfinite(d)]
   564	    n = len(d)
   565	    if n < 10:
   566	        return float("nan")
   567	    dm_ = float(np.mean(d))
   568	    g0 = float(np.mean((d - dm_) ** 2))
   569	    var = g0
   570	    for lag in range(1, max_lag + 1):
   571	        w = 1 - lag / (max_lag + 1)
   572	        g = float(np.mean((d[lag:] - dm_) * (d[:-lag] - dm_)))
   573	        var += 2 * w * g
   574	    if var <= 0:
   575	        return float("nan")
   576	    return dm_ / math.sqrt(var / n)
   577	
   578	
   579	def canonical_bandwidth(h: int, n: int) -> int:
   580	    return max(1, min(int(np.ceil(h ** (1 / 3) * n ** (1 / 3))), n // 4))
   581	
   582	
   583	def _bartlett_long_run_variance(x: np.ndarray, max_lag: int) -> float:
   584	    """Bartlett-kernel long-run variance of a scalar moment sequence."""
   585	    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
   586	    x = x[np.isfinite(x)]
   587	    n = len(x)
   588	    if n < 10:
   589	        raise ValueError("Giacomini-White test requires at least 10 finite losses")
   590	    centred = x - float(np.mean(x))
   591	    lrv = float(np.dot(centred, centred) / n)
   592	    for lag in range(1, min(max_lag, n - 1) + 1):
   593	        weight = 1.0 - lag / (max_lag + 1.0)
   594	        gamma = float(np.dot(centred[lag:], centred[:-lag]) / n)
   595	        lrv += 2.0 * weight * gamma
   596	    if not np.isfinite(lrv) or lrv <= 0:
   597	        raise ValueError(f"Non-positive Giacomini-White long-run variance: {lrv}")
   598	    return lrv
   599	
   600	
   601	def giacomini_white_unconditional(
   602	    loss_aug: np.ndarray,
   603	    loss_base: np.ndarray,
   604	    h: int,
   605	) -> dict:
   606	    """GW equal-unconditional-predictive-ability test under general loss.
   607	
   608	    The caller must supply losses from forecasting methods estimated with a
   609	    fixed rolling window.  Giacomini and White (2006) then retain estimation
   610	    uncertainty in the limiting experiment, including for nested methods.  The
   611	    moment is ``E[L_aug - L_base] = 0``; negative z favours the augmented method.
   612	    """
   613	    aug = np.asarray(loss_aug, dtype=np.float64)
   614	    base = np.asarray(loss_base, dtype=np.float64)
   615	    if aug.shape != base.shape:
   616	        raise ValueError("Giacomini-White loss arrays must have identical shapes")
   617	    finite = np.isfinite(aug) & np.isfinite(base)
   618	    diff = aug[finite] - base[finite]
   619	    n = len(diff)
   620	    if n < 60:
   621	        raise ValueError(f"Giacomini-White test requires >=60 losses; got {n}")
   622	    bandwidth = max(h - 1, canonical_bandwidth(h, n))
   623	    lrv = _bartlett_long_run_variance(diff, bandwidth)
   624	    mean_diff = float(np.mean(diff))
   625	    se = math.sqrt(lrv / n)
   626	    z_stat = mean_diff / se
   627	    return {
   628	        "test": "Giacomini-White (2006) equal unconditional predictive ability",
   629	        "loss": "Patton QLIKE",
   630	        "forecast_scheme": "fixed rolling estimation window",
   631	        "null": "equal expected QLIKE loss for the two forecasting methods",
   632	        "direction": "negative z favours HAR plus rdisp",
   633	        "n": int(n),
   634	        "mean_loss_diff_aug_minus_base": mean_diff,
   635	        "z_stat": float(z_stat),
   636	        "p_value_two_sided": float(2.0 * stats.norm.sf(abs(z_stat))),
   637	        "p_value_one_sided_aug_better": float(stats.norm.cdf(z_stat)),
   638	        "hac_lag_used": int(bandwidth),
   639	        "long_run_variance": float(lrv),
   640	        "standard_error": float(se),
   641	    }
   642	
   643	
   644	def gw_material_gain_exclusion(
   645	    loss_aug: np.ndarray,
   646	    loss_base: np.ndarray,
   647	    h: int,
   648	    margin: float = MATERIAL_GAIN_MARGIN,
   649	) -> dict:
   650	    """Test whether a pre-specified relative QLIKE gain can be ruled out.
   651	
   652	    H0 says the augmented method improves expected QLIKE by at least ``margin``:
   653	    ``E[L_aug] <= (1-margin) E[L_base]``.  Positive z rejects that claim.  This
   654	    reverses the burden of proof needed for a bounded null; it does not assert
   655	    exact equality or two-sided equivalence.
   656	    """
   657	    if not 0 < margin < 1:
   658	        raise ValueError("Material-gain margin must lie strictly between zero and one")
   659	    aug = np.asarray(loss_aug, dtype=np.float64)
   660	    base = np.asarray(loss_base, dtype=np.float64)
   661	    if aug.shape != base.shape:
   662	        raise ValueError("Material-gain loss arrays must have identical shapes")
   663	    finite = np.isfinite(aug) & np.isfinite(base)
   664	    moment = aug[finite] - (1.0 - margin) * base[finite]
   665	    n = len(moment)
   666	    if n < 60:
   667	        raise ValueError(f"Material-gain exclusion requires >=60 losses; got {n}")
   668	    bandwidth = max(h - 1, canonical_bandwidth(h, n))
   669	    lrv = _bartlett_long_run_variance(moment, bandwidth)
   670	    mean_moment = float(np.mean(moment))
   671	    se = math.sqrt(lrv / n)
   672	    z_stat = mean_moment / se
   673	    return {
   674	        "test": "GW bounded-window one-sided material-gain exclusion",
   675	        "margin_relative_qlike": float(margin),
   676	        "null": f"augmented method improves expected QLIKE by at least {100 * margin:.1f}%",
   677	        "alternative": f"expected QLIKE gain is smaller than {100 * margin:.1f}%",
   678	        "n": int(n),
   679	        "mean_margin_moment": mean_moment,
   680	        "z_stat": float(z_stat),
   681	        "p_value_one_sided": float(stats.norm.sf(z_stat)),
   682	        "hac_lag_used": int(bandwidth),
   683	        "long_run_variance": float(lrv),
   684	        "standard_error": float(se),
   685	        "scope": "rules out a material gain; does not prove exact equality",
   686	    }
   687	
   688	
   689	def moving_block_skill_ci(
   690	    loss_aug: np.ndarray,
   691	    loss_base: np.ndarray,
   692	    h: int,
   693	    seed: int,
   694	    reps: int = 1999,
   695	) -> dict:
   696	    """Paired moving-block CI for relative QLIKE improvement.
   697	
   698	    This is a finite-sample uncertainty diagnostic on the already-generated GW
   699	    loss stream, not a replacement for the fixed-window GW test and not a model
   700	    re-estimation bootstrap.
   701	    """
   702	    aug = np.asarray(loss_aug, dtype=np.float64)
   703	    base = np.asarray(loss_base, dtype=np.float64)
   704	    finite = np.isfinite(aug) & np.isfinite(base)
   705	    aug, base = aug[finite], base[finite]
   706	    n = len(aug)
   707	    if n < 60:
   708	        raise ValueError(f"Block bootstrap requires >=60 paired losses; got {n}")
   709	    if reps < 1000:
   710	        raise ValueError("Research rule requires at least 1000 bootstrap replications")
   711	    block_length = max(int(h), int(math.ceil(n ** (1.0 / 3.0))))
   712	    n_blocks = int(math.ceil(n / block_length))
   713	    generator = np.random.default_rng(seed)
   714	    draws = np.empty(reps, dtype=np.float64)
   715	    offsets = np.arange(block_length)
   716	    for b in range(reps):
   717	        starts = generator.integers(0, n, size=n_blocks)
   718	        idx = ((starts[:, None] + offsets[None, :]) % n).ravel()[:n]
   719	        base_mean = float(np.mean(base[idx]))
   720	        if base_mean <= 0 or not np.isfinite(base_mean):
   721	            raise ValueError("Mean baseline QLIKE must be positive and finite")
   722	        draws[b] = 100.0 * (base_mean - float(np.mean(aug[idx]))) / base_mean
   723	    point_base = float(np.mean(base))
   724	    point = 100.0 * (point_base - float(np.mean(aug))) / point_base
   725	    return {
   726	        "method": "paired circular moving-block bootstrap of fixed-window QLIKE losses",
   727	        "seed": int(seed),
   728	        "reps": int(reps),
   729	        "block_length": int(block_length),
   730	        "point_improvement_pct": float(point),
   731	        "ci95_improvement_pct": [float(x) for x in np.quantile(draws, [0.025, 0.975])],
   732	        "scope": "uncertainty diagnostic; forecasts are not re-estimated inside bootstrap",
   733	    }
   734	
   735	
   736	def acf(x: np.ndarray, nlags: int = 10) -> list[float]:
   737	    x = x[np.isfinite(x)] - np.mean(x[np.isfinite(x)])
   738	    denom = float(np.sum(x**2))
   739	    return [float(np.sum(x[k:] * x[:-k]) / denom) if denom > 0 else float("nan") for k in range(1, nlags + 1)]
   740	
   741	
   742	def compare(loss_aug: np.ndarray, loss_base: np.ndarray, h: int) -> dict:
   743	    """Canonical DM + the diagnostics the repo's DM/HAC lesson demands."""
   744	    t_stat, p_val = dm_test(loss_aug, loss_base, h=h)   # negative t => augmented better
   745	    d = loss_aug - loss_base
   746	    n = int(np.isfinite(d).sum())
   747	    bw = canonical_bandwidth(h, n)
   748	
   749	    a = acf(d, 10)
   750	    band = 1.96 / math.sqrt(n) if n else float("nan")
   751	    sens = {
   752	        "lag_0_iid": _nw_dm(d, 0) if n >= 10 else float("nan"),
   753	        f"lag_{bw}_canonical": _nw_dm(d, bw),
   754	        f"lag_{2 * bw}": _nw_dm(d, 2 * bw),
   755	        f"lag_{4 * bw}": _nw_dm(d, 4 * bw),
   756	    }
   757	    return {
   758	        "dm_t": float(t_stat),
   759	        "dm_p": float(p_val),
   760	        "n": n,
   761	        "hac_lag_used": bw,
   762	        "loss_diff_acf_1_10": a,
   763	        "acf_white_noise_band": band,
   764	        "acf1_outside_band": bool(abs(a[0]) > band) if n else None,
   765	        "dm_t_by_lag": sens,
   766	        "harvey_pass": bool(abs(t_stat) > 3.0),
   767	    }
   768	
   769	
   770	def dm_parity_check() -> dict:
   771	    """Local NW variant must reproduce the canonical helper at canonical bandwidth."""
   772	    g = np.random.default_rng(SEED)
   773	    worst = 0.0
   774	    for h in HORIZONS:
   775	        e = g.standard_normal(1200)
   776	        d = 0.02 + e + 0.6 * np.roll(e, 1)   # deliberately autocorrelated
   777	        canon_t, _ = dm_test(d, np.zeros_like(d), h=h)
   778	        mine = _nw_dm(d, canonical_bandwidth(h, len(d)))
   779	        worst = max(worst, abs(canon_t - mine))
   780	    return {"max_abs_diff_vs_canonical": float(worst), "passed": bool(worst < 1e-8)}
   781	
   782	
   783	def common_all(*frames: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DatetimeIndex:
   784	    """Origins shared by every model, INCLUDING zero-variance days.
   785	
   786	    MSE and Clark-West are perfectly well defined when the realised variance is zero,
   787	    and those are the calmest (highest-dispersion) days -- exactly the regime the
   788	    hypothesis would most want scored.  Only QLIKE has to drop them.
   789	    """
   790	    idx = frames[0].index
   791	    for f in frames[1:]:
   792	        idx = idx.intersection(f.index)
   793	    return idx
   794	
   795	
   796	def common_valid(*frames: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DatetimeIndex:
   797	    """Origins shared by every model AND on which QLIKE is defined.
   798	
   799	    QLIKE contains -log(actual/predicted), which diverges when the realised variance
   800	    is exactly zero.  The repo's canonical qlike() already drops those observations;
   801	    qlike_pointwise() (used for the DM test) does not, so we drop them here instead of
   802	    letting an unchanged close contribute a loss of ~+30 to a mean of ~0.5.  The same
   803	    origins are removed for every model, so no specification is advantaged.
   804	    """
   805	    idx = frames[0].index
   806	    for f in frames[1:]:
   807	        idx = idx.intersection(f.index)
   808	    actual = frames[0].loc[idx, "actual"].to_numpy()
   809	    return idx[actual > 0]
   810	
   811	
   812	def bh_fdr(pvals: list[float]) -> list[float]:
   813	    p = np.asarray(pvals, dtype=float)
   814	    m = len(p)
   815	    order = np.argsort(p)
   816	    q = np.empty(m)
   817	    prev = 1.0
   818	    for rank in range(m - 1, -1, -1):
   819	        i = order[rank]
   820	        prev = min(prev, p[i] * m / (rank + 1))
   821	        q[i] = min(prev, 1.0)
   822	    return [float(v) for v in q]
   823	
   824	
   825	def holm_fwer(pvals: list[float]) -> list[float]:
   826	    """Holm step-down adjusted p-values in the caller's original order."""
   827	    p = np.asarray(pvals, dtype=float)
   828	    m = len(p)
   829	    order = np.argsort(p)
   830	    adjusted = np.empty(m)
   831	    running = 0.0
   832	    for rank, i in enumerate(order):
   833	        running = max(running, float((m - rank) * p[i]))
   834	        adjusted[i] = min(running, 1.0)
   835	    return [float(v) for v in adjusted]
   836	
   837	
   838	def require_complete_primary_family(family: list[dict]) -> None:
   839	    """Fail closed unless every pre-registered asset-horizon cell exists once."""
   840	    expected = {(asset, int(h)) for asset in ASSETS for h in HORIZONS}
   841	    observed = [(str(cell.get("asset")), int(cell.get("h", -1))) for cell in family]
   842	    observed_set = set(observed)
   843	    duplicates = sorted({key for key in observed_set if observed.count(key) > 1})
   844	    missing = sorted(expected - observed_set)
   845	    unexpected = sorted(observed_set - expected)
   846	    if len(family) != len(expected) or duplicates or missing or unexpected:
   847	        raise ValueError(
   848	            "Incomplete pre-registered K1701 primary family: "
   849	            f"expected={len(expected)} got={len(family)} "
   850	            f"missing={missing} unexpected={unexpected} duplicates={duplicates}"
   851	        )
   852	
   853	
   854	def hac_ols(y: np.ndarray, X: np.ndarray, lag: int) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
   855	    """OLS with Newey-West standard errors (for in-sample diagnostics only)."""
   856	    n, k = X.shape
   857	    beta = _ols(X, y)
   858	    e = y - X @ beta
   859	    XtX_inv = np.linalg.pinv(X.T @ X)
   860	    S = (X * e[:, None]).T @ (X * e[:, None])
   861	    for L in range(1, lag + 1):
   862	        w = 1 - L / (lag + 1)
   863	        Xe_t = X[L:] * e[L:, None]
   864	        Xe_l = X[:-L] * e[:-L, None]
   865	        G = Xe_t.T @ Xe_l
   866	        S += w * (G + G.T)
   867	    V = XtX_inv @ S @ XtX_inv
   868	    se = np.sqrt(np.maximum(np.diag(V), 0))
   869	    return beta, se
   870	
   871	
   872	# ── experiment ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   873	
   874	
   875	def run_asset(name: str, results: dict) -> dict:
   876	    cfg = ASSETS[name]
   877	    print(f"\n=== {name}: {cfg['label']} ===")
   878	    A = load_asset(name)
   879	    ret, mem, idx_ret = A["ret"], A["mem"], A["idx_ret"]
   880	
   881	    universe = sorted(set(mem["ticker"]) & set(ret.columns))
   882	    mask = membership_matrix(mem, ret.index, universe)
   883	    disp = build_dispersion(ret, idx_ret, mask, universe)
   884	
   885	    vix = None
   886	    if cfg["vix"] and cfg["vix"] in A["px"].columns:
   887	        vix = A["px"][cfg["vix"]]
   888	
   889	    df = build_panel(idx_ret, disp, vix)
   890	    df = df.loc[df.index >= pd.Timestamp(SAMPLE_START)]
   891	
   892	    valid = df["rdisp"].notna()
   893	    print(f"  usable dispersion days: {int(valid.sum())} "
   894	          f"({df.index[valid][0].date()} .. {df.index[valid][-1].date()})")
   895	    print(f"  members/day: median {df['n_members'].median():.0f}, "
   896	          f"min {df['n_members'].min():.0f}, max {df['n_members'].max():.0f}")
   897	
   898	    out: dict = {
   899	        "label": cfg["label"],
   900	        "index_proxy": cfg["index"],
   901	        "data_cleaning": A["cleaning"],
   902	        "sample": {
   903	            "start": str(df.index[valid][0].date()),
   904	            "end": str(df.index[valid][-1].date()),
   905	            "n_days_with_dispersion": int(valid.sum()),
   906	            "members_per_day": {
   907	                "median": float(df["n_members"].median()),
   908	                "min": float(df["n_members"].min()),
   909	                "max": float(df["n_members"].max()),
   910	            },
   911	        },
   912	        "descriptive": {},
   913	        "oos_vol": {},
   914	        "oos_tail": {},
   915	        "in_sample_hac": {},
   916	        # every data-cleaning decision is recorded, because a cross-sectional vol
   917	        # average is unusually sensitive to a single unadjusted corporate action
   918	        "data_quality": {
   919	            "index_cleaning": A["cleaning"],
   920	            "constituent_mask_threshold_abs_log_return": A["mask_threshold"],
   921	            "n_constituent_obs_masked": A["n_masked"],
   922	            "masked_observations": A["masked_obs"],
   923	            "rv_winsor_floor": float(df.attrs.get("rv_floor", VAR_FLOOR)),
   924	            "rv_winsor_floor_pct_of_warmup": RV_FLOOR_PCT,
   925	            "n_zero_variance_targets_dropped_from_qlike": {
   926	                f"h{h}": int((df[f"y_h{h}"].dropna() <= 0).sum()) for h in HORIZONS
   927	            },
   928	        },
   929	    }
   930	
   931	    # descriptive statistics
   932	    for s in SIGNALS + ["idx_vol", "avg_vol"]:
   933	        v = df[s].dropna()
   934	        out["descriptive"][s] = {
   935	            "mean": float(v.mean()), "std": float(v.std()),
   936	            "p5": float(v.quantile(0.05)), "median": float(v.median()),
   937	            "p95": float(v.quantile(0.95)),
   938	            "acf1": float(v.autocorr(1)),
   939	        }
   940	    if "l_vix" in df:
  1440	    for name in ("SPX", "TW50"):
  1441	        k = keep[name]
  1442	        ret, mem, universe = k["ret"], k["A"]["mem"], k["universe"]
  1443	        today = pd.Timestamp(results["data_provenance"]["fetched_at_utc"][:10])
  1444	        cur = sorted(set(mem[(mem["start"] <= today) & (mem["end"] >= today)]["ticker"]) & set(universe))
  1445	        naive_mask = np.zeros((len(ret.index), len(universe)), dtype=bool)
  1446	        pos = {t: i for i, t in enumerate(universe)}
  1447	        for t in cur:
  1448	            naive_mask[:, pos[t]] = True
  1449	        naive = build_dispersion(ret, k["A"]["idx_ret"], naive_mask, universe)
  1450	        pit = k["disp"]
  1451	        j = pit[["rdisp"]].join(naive[["rdisp"]], rsuffix="_naive").dropna()
  1452	        j = j.loc[j.index >= pd.Timestamp(SAMPLE_START)]
  1453	        early = j.loc[j.index < pd.Timestamp("2015-01-01")]
  1454	        r2[name] = {
  1455	            "corr_pit_vs_naive": float(j["rdisp"].corr(j["rdisp_naive"])),
  1456	            "mean_rdisp_pit": float(j["rdisp"].mean()),
  1457	            "mean_rdisp_naive": float(j["rdisp_naive"].mean()),
  1458	            "mean_gap_naive_minus_pit": float((j["rdisp_naive"] - j["rdisp"]).mean()),
  1459	            "mean_gap_pre2015": float((early["rdisp_naive"] - early["rdisp"]).mean()) if len(early) else None,
  1460	            "note": "naive = today's constituent list applied to all history (survivorship + look-ahead membership)",
  1461	        }
  1462	    rob["R2_membership_bias"] = r2
  1463	
  1464	    # R3 — same framework on the survivorship-free sector-ETF basket (links to K415/K982)
  1465	    k = keep["SPX"]
  1466	    ret = k["A"]["ret"]
  1467	    sec = [t for t in SECTOR_ETFS if t in ret.columns]
  1468	    sec_mask = np.zeros((len(ret.index), len(sec)), dtype=bool)
  1469	    sec_mask[:, :] = True
  1470	    sec_ret = ret[sec]
  1471	    # only 9 sector ETFs exist, so the constituent-count floor has to come down
  1472	    sdisp = build_dispersion(sec_ret, k["A"]["idx_ret"], sec_mask, sec, min_members=5)
  1473	    sdf = build_panel(k["A"]["idx_ret"], sdisp, None)
  1474	    sdf = sdf.loc[sdf.index >= pd.Timestamp(SAMPLE_START)]
  1475	    r3 = {"basket": sec, "note": "sector-ETF cross-section (survivorship-free) inside the identical framework", "cells": {}}
  1476	    for h in HORIZONS:
  1477	        b = expanding_oos(sdf, ["l_rv_d", "l_rv_w", "l_rv_m"], f"y_h{h}", h, log_target=True)
  1478	        a = expanding_oos(sdf, ["l_rv_d", "l_rv_w", "l_rv_m", "l_rdisp"], f"y_h{h}", h, log_target=True)
  1479	        if b.empty or a.empty:
  1480	            continue
  1481	        c = common_valid(b, a)
  1482	        l0 = qlike_pointwise(b.loc[c, "actual"].to_numpy(), b.loc[c, "pred"].to_numpy())
  1483	        la = qlike_pointwise(a.loc[c, "actual"].to_numpy(), a.loc[c, "pred"].to_numpy())
  1484	        r3["cells"][f"h{h}"] = {
  1485	            "qlike_improve_pct": float(100 * (np.mean(l0) - np.mean(la)) / np.mean(l0)),
  1486	            "raw_dm_diagnostic": {**compare(la, l0, h), "feeds_gate": False},
  1487	        }
  1488	    rob["R3_sector_etf_basket"] = r3
  1489	
  1490	    # R4 — vol-window sensitivity
  1491	    r4 = {}
  1492	    for w in (10, 66):
  1493	        k = keep["SPX"]
  1494	        d_w = build_dispersion(k["ret"], k["A"]["idx_ret"], k["mask"], k["universe"], window=w)
  1495	        dfw = build_panel(k["A"]["idx_ret"], d_w, None)
  1496	        dfw = dfw.loc[dfw.index >= pd.Timestamp(SAMPLE_START)]
  1497	        cells = {}
  1498	        for h in HORIZONS:
  1499	            b = expanding_oos(dfw, ["l_rv_d", "l_rv_w", "l_rv_m"], f"y_h{h}", h, log_target=True)
  1500	            a = expanding_oos(dfw, ["l_rv_d", "l_rv_w", "l_rv_m", "l_rdisp"], f"y_h{h}", h, log_target=True)
  1501	            if b.empty or a.empty:
  1502	                continue
  1503	            c = common_valid(b, a)
  1504	            l0 = qlike_pointwise(b.loc[c, "actual"].to_numpy(), b.loc[c, "pred"].to_numpy())
  1505	            la = qlike_pointwise(a.loc[c, "actual"].to_numpy(), a.loc[c, "pred"].to_numpy())
  1506	            cells[f"h{h}"] = {
  1507	                "qlike_improve_pct": float(100 * (np.mean(l0) - np.mean(la)) / np.mean(l0)),
  1508	                "raw_dm_diagnostic": {**compare(la, l0, h), "feeds_gate": False},
  1509	            }
  1510	        r4[f"window_{w}d"] = cells
  1511	    rob["R4_vol_window_sensitivity"] = r4
  1512	
  1513	    # R5 — lookahead audit (truncation test + embargo gaps recorded during the OOS run)
  1514	    rob["R5_lookahead_audit"] = lookahead_audit(keep, EMBARGO_AUDIT)
  1515	
  1516	    # R6 — did the CLEANING manufacture the null?  The constituent mask is the only
  1517	    # cleaning knob with a real bias vector: masking a return NaNs it out of that stock's
  1518	    # vol AND out of the equal-weight portfolio, which depresses avg_vol and rho in the
  1519	    # crash regime -- the exact regime a dispersion effect would live in.  So re-run the
  1520	    # whole level-controlled ladder at a tighter and a looser threshold and check the
  1521	    # verdict is invariant.  If the null only exists at one threshold, it was manufactured.
  1522	    r6: dict = {"default_threshold": CONSTITUENT_RET_MASK, "thresholds": {}}
  1523	    for thr in MASK_SENSITIVITY:
  1524	        per_thr = {"n_masked": {}, "cells": {}}
  1525	        for name in ASSETS:
  1526	            A = load_asset(name, mask_threshold=thr)
  1527	            per_thr["n_masked"][name] = A["n_masked"]
  1528	            universe = sorted(set(A["mem"]["ticker"]) & set(A["ret"].columns))
  1529	            mask = membership_matrix(A["mem"], A["ret"].index, universe)
  1530	            disp = build_dispersion(A["ret"], A["idx_ret"], mask, universe)
  1531	            d = build_panel(A["idx_ret"], disp, None)
  1532	            d = d.loc[d.index >= pd.Timestamp(SAMPLE_START)]
  1533	            bf = ["l_rv_d", "l_rv_w", "l_rv_m"]
  1534	            for h in HORIZONS:
  1535	                lvl = expanding_oos(d, bf + ["l_avg_vol"], f"y_h{h}", h, log_target=True)
  1536	                if lvl.empty:
  1537	                    continue
  1538	                for sig in ("rho", "csvd_rel"):
  1539	                    m = expanding_oos(d, bf + ["l_avg_vol", sig], f"y_h{h}", h, log_target=True)
  1540	                    if m.empty:
  1541	                        continue
  1542	                    cq, ca = common_valid(m, lvl), common_all(m, lvl)
  1543	                    lb = qlike_pointwise(lvl.loc[cq, "actual"].to_numpy(), lvl.loc[cq, "pred"].to_numpy())
  1544	                    la = qlike_pointwise(lvl.loc[cq, "actual"].to_numpy(), m.loc[cq, "pred"].to_numpy())
  1545	                    cw = clark_west_test(
  1546	                        lvl.loc[ca, "actual"].to_numpy(),
  1547	                        lvl.loc[ca, "pred"].to_numpy(),
  1548	                        m.loc[ca, "pred"].to_numpy(),
  1549	                        h=h,
  1550	                    )
  1551	                    actual_mse = lvl.loc[ca, "actual"].to_numpy()
  1552	                    pred_base_mse = lvl.loc[ca, "pred"].to_numpy()
  1553	                    pred_aug_mse = m.loc[ca, "pred"].to_numpy()
  1554	                    mse_base = float(np.mean((actual_mse - pred_base_mse) ** 2))
  1555	                    mse_aug = float(np.mean((actual_mse - pred_aug_mse) ** 2))
  1556	                    per_thr["cells"][f"{name}_h{h}_{sig}"] = {
  1557	                        "qlike_improve_pct": float(100 * (np.mean(lb) - np.mean(la)) / np.mean(lb)),
  1558	                        "mse_improve_pct": float(100 * (mse_base - mse_aug) / mse_base),
  1559	                        "raw_dm_diagnostic": {**compare(la, lb, h), "feeds_gate": False},
  1560	                        "cw_t": cw.get("t_stat"),
  1561	                        "cw_p_one_sided": cw.get("p_value_one_sided"),
  1562	                    }
  1563	        cells = per_thr["cells"]
  1564	        if cells:
  1565	            qs = bh_fdr([c["cw_p_one_sided"] for c in cells.values()])
  1566	            for c, q in zip(cells.values(), qs):
  1567	                c["cw_bh_q"] = q
  1568	            per_thr["summary"] = {
  1569	                "cells": len(cells),
  1570	                "cw_nominally_significant": int(sum(c["cw_p_one_sided"] < 0.05 for c in cells.values())),
  1571	                "cw_significant_after_bh": int(sum(c["cw_bh_q"] < 0.05 for c in cells.values())),
  1572	                "mse_positive_and_cw_bh": int(
  1573	                    sum(c["mse_improve_pct"] > 0 and c["cw_t"] > 3 and c["cw_bh_q"] < 0.05
  1574	                        for c in cells.values())
  1575	                ),
  1576	            }
  1577	        r6["thresholds"][f"mask_{thr}"] = per_thr
  1578	    rob["R6_cleaning_did_not_manufacture_the_null"] = r6
  1579	    return rob
  1580	
  1581	
  1582	def lookahead_audit(keep: dict, embargo_audit: dict) -> dict:
  1583	    """Mechanical proof that no signal can see the future.
  1584	
  1585	    The decisive test is TRUNCATION: rebuild every dispersion signal from a return
  1586	    panel that has been cut off at T0, and require the values at every t <= T0 to
  1587	    be bit-identical to the full-sample values.  A signal that peeks at t+1 (or at
  1588	    any later row) cannot survive this, whereas eyeballing `.shift(1)` calls can
  1589	    miss a leak hiding inside a rolling window.
  1590	    """
  1591	    checks: dict = {}
  1592	    for name, k in keep.items():
  1593	        df, A = k["df"], k["A"]
  1594	
  1595	        # 1. targets are strictly forward: y_h1 at t must be exactly r_{t+1}^2
  1596	        y1 = df["y_h1"].dropna()
  1597	        rebuilt = (df["ret"] ** 2).shift(-1).reindex(y1.index)
  1598	        ok_target = bool(np.allclose(y1.to_numpy(), rebuilt.to_numpy(), rtol=0, atol=0, equal_nan=True))
  1599	
  1600	        # 2. truncation test on the signals
  1601	        T0 = pd.Timestamp("2018-06-29")
  1602	        ret_cut = A["ret"].loc[A["ret"].index <= T0]
  1603	        idx_cut = A["idx_ret"].loc[A["idx_ret"].index <= T0]
  1604	        mask_cut = k["mask"][: len(ret_cut)]
  1605	        disp_cut = build_dispersion(ret_cut, idx_cut, mask_cut, k["universe"])
  1606	        full = k["disp"].loc[k["disp"].index <= T0]
  1607	        common = disp_cut.index.intersection(full.index)
  1608	        diffs = {}
  1609	        for s in SIGNALS:
  1610	            a = full.loc[common, s].to_numpy()
  1611	            b = disp_cut.loc[common, s].to_numpy()
  1612	            both = np.isfinite(a) & np.isfinite(b)
  1613	            diffs[s] = float(np.max(np.abs(a[both] - b[both]))) if both.any() else float("nan")
  1614	        ok_trunc = all(d == 0.0 or (np.isfinite(d) and d < 1e-12) for d in diffs.values())
  1615	
  1616	        checks[name] = {
  1617	            "y_h1_equals_next_day_squared_return": ok_target,
  1618	            "truncation_test_T0": str(T0.date()),
  1619	            "truncation_max_abs_diff_by_signal": diffs,
  1620	            "truncation_identical": bool(ok_trunc),
  1621	            "n_days_compared": int(len(common)),
  1622	            "bad_ticks_masked": int(A["n_masked"]),
  1623	        }
  1624	
  1625	    checks["embargo"] = embargo_audit.get("embargo", [])
  1626	    checks["embargo_all_ok"] = bool(all(e["ok"] for e in checks["embargo"])) if checks["embargo"] else None
  1627	    checks["gw_fixed_window"] = embargo_audit.get("gw_fixed_window", [])
  1628	    checks["gw_fixed_window_all_ok"] = (
  1629	        bool(all(e["ok"] for e in checks["gw_fixed_window"]))
  1630	        if checks["gw_fixed_window"]
  1631	        else False
  1632	    )
  1633	    checks["all_passed"] = bool(
  1634	        all(
  1635	            v["y_h1_equals_next_day_squared_return"] and v["truncation_identical"]
  1636	            for v in checks.values()
  1637	            if isinstance(v, dict) and "truncation_identical" in v
  1638	        )
  1639	        and (checks["embargo_all_ok"] is not False)
  1640	        and checks["gw_fixed_window_all_ok"]
  1641	    )
  1642	    return checks
  1643	
  1644

  1177	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1178	# 9. The cell — one nested comparison, fully inferred
  1179	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1180	def evaluate_cell(
  1181	    p: Panel,
  1182	    base: str,
  1183	    alt: str,
  1184	    family: str,
  1185	    train_window: int = GW_TRAIN_WINDOW,
  1186	    smearing: str = "own",
  1187	    register_gate: bool = True,
  1188	) -> dict | None:
  1189	    """Run one nested comparison end to end and register its tests."""
  1190	    po = paired_oos(p, base, alt, train_window, smearing)
  1191	    if len(po.actual) < GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1192	        return None
  1193	
  1194	    lb = qlike_pointwise(po.actual, po.pred_base)
  1195	    la = qlike_pointwise(po.actual, po.pred_aug)
  1196	    h = p.horizon
  1197	    qb, qa = float(np.mean(lb)), float(np.mean(la))
  1198	    improve = (qb - qa) / qb * 100.0
  1199	
  1200	    gw = giacomini_white(la, lb, h)
  1201	    excl = material_gain_exclusion(la, lb, h)
  1202	    excl["qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct"] = qlike_gain_upper_bound(la, lb, h, alpha=0.05)
  1203	    excl["upper_bound_note"] = (
  1204	        "One-sided 95% upper confidence bound on the RELATIVE QLIKE gain, obtained "
  1205	        "by inverting the exclusion test. Gains above it are excluded by the data; "
  1206	        "gains below it are not. None = even a 90% gain cannot be excluded."
  1207	    )
  1208	    boot = block_bootstrap_ci(la, lb, h, seed=SEED + h)
  1209	    dm = raw_dm_diagnostic(la, lb, h)
  1210	
  1211	    # v1's design, re-run purely so the results file can show what changed. An
  1212	    # expanding window breaks GW's bounded-memory condition, so a HAC t-test on
  1213	    # these losses is NOT a valid nested test -- whatever it happens to say.
  1214	    exp_po = paired_oos(p, base, alt, train_window=None, smearing=smearing)
  1215	    expanding = None
  1216	    if len(exp_po.actual) >= GW_MIN_LOSSES:
  1217	        exp_lb = qlike_pointwise(exp_po.actual, exp_po.pred_base)
  1218	        exp_la = qlike_pointwise(exp_po.actual, exp_po.pred_aug)
  1219	        exp_dm = raw_dm_diagnostic(exp_la, exp_lb, h)
  1220	        expanding = {
  1221	            "scheme": "expanding window (v1's design)",
  1222	            "valid_for_nested_inference": False,
  1223	            "why_invalid": (
  1224	                "GW's limiting experiment needs bounded estimator memory. An "
  1225	                "expanding window has none, so the nested null is degenerate and "
  1226	                "the statistic is biased toward the smaller model."
  1227	            ),
  1228	            "feeds_gate": False,
  1229	            "n_oos": int(len(exp_po.actual)),
  1230	            "qlike_improvement_pct": float(
  1231	                (np.mean(exp_lb) - np.mean(exp_la)) / np.mean(exp_lb) * 100.0
  1232	            ),
  1233	            "hac_t_stat": exp_dm["t_stat"],
  1234	        }
  1235	
  1236	    # Clark-West: a DIFFERENT estimand. CW adjusts the MSPE of variance-level
  1237	    # forecasts; it is not a QLIKE general-loss test and is not relabelled as one.
  1238	    cw = clark_west_test(po.actual, po.pred_base, po.pred_aug, h=h)
  1239	
  1240	    cell = f"{p.asset}_h{h}_{alt}" + ("" if smearing == "own" else f"_{smearing}")
  1241	    LOSS_CACHE[cell] = (la, lb, h)
  1242	    register(
  1243	        TestRecord(
  1244	            family=family, cell=cell, asset=p.asset, horizon=h, base=base, alt=alt,
  1245	            inference="giacomini_white_qlike_fixed_window",
  1246	            estimand="E[QLIKE_aug - QLIKE_base]",
  1247	            scheme="paired fixed rolling window",
  1248	            stat=gw["z_stat"], p_one_sided=gw["p_value_one_sided_flow_better"],
  1249	            feeds_gate=register_gate, qlike_improve_pct=improve, n=gw["n"],
  1250	        )
  1251	    )
  1252	    register(
  1253	        TestRecord(
  1254	            family=family, cell=cell, asset=p.asset, horizon=h, base=base, alt=alt,
  1255	            inference="raw_dm_qlike",
  1256	            estimand="E[QLIKE_aug - QLIKE_base] (nested-biased)",
  1257	            scheme="paired fixed rolling window",
  1258	            stat=dm["t_stat"], p_one_sided=dm["p_one_sided_flow_better_student_t"],
  1259	            feeds_gate=False, qlike_improve_pct=improve, n=gw["n"],
  1260	        )
  1261	    )
  1262	    register(
  1263	        TestRecord(
  1264	            family=family, cell=cell, asset=p.asset, horizon=h, base=base, alt=alt,
  1265	            inference="clark_west_mspe",
  1266	            estimand="MSPE of variance-level forecasts (NOT QLIKE)",
  1267	            scheme="paired fixed rolling window",
  1268	            stat=float(cw["t_stat"]),
  1269	            p_one_sided=float(cw.get("p_value_one_sided", np.nan)),
  1270	            feeds_gate=False, qlike_improve_pct=improve, n=gw["n"],
  1271	        )
  1272	    )
  1273	
  1274	    return {
  1275	        "asset": p.asset,
  1276	        "horizon": h,
  1277	        "rv_proxy": p.rv_col,
  1278	        "base": base,
  1279	        "alt": alt,
  1280	        "cell": cell,
  1281	        "family": family,
  1282	        "state_lag": p.state_lag,
  1283	        "flow_lag": p.flow_lag,
  1284	        "smearing": smearing,
  1285	        "n_oos": int(len(po.actual)),
  1286	        "oos_start": str(po.origins.min().date()),
  1287	        "oos_end": str(po.origins.max().date()),
  1288	        "qlike_base": qb,
  1289	        "qlike_alt": qa,
  1290	        "qlike_improvement_pct": improve,
  1291	        "oos_audit": po.audit,
  1292	        "primary_inference_gw_qlike": gw,
  1293	        "material_gain_exclusion": excl,
  1294	        "bootstrap_ci": boot,
  1295	        "raw_dm_diagnostic": dm,
  1296	        "expanding_window_diagnostic_v1_design": expanding,
  1297	        "statistic_coincidence_note": (
  1298	            "raw_dm_diagnostic.t_stat equals primary_inference_gw_qlike.z_stat by "
  1299	            "construction: on ONE loss stream the HAC t and the GW z are the same "
  1300	            "arithmetic. What separates them is the estimation scheme feeding the "
  1301	            "losses and the null being tested. Compare against "
  1302	            "expanding_window_diagnostic_v1_design to see what the scheme change did."
  1303	        ),
  1304	        "clark_west_mspe_separate_estimand": {
  1305	            "t_stat": float(cw["t_stat"]),
  1306	            "p_one_sided": float(cw.get("p_value_one_sided", np.nan)),
  1307	            "estimand": "MSPE (variance level), not QLIKE",
  1308	            "feeds_gate": False,
  1309	            "note": (
  1310	                "Clark-West corrects the MSPE of the nested comparison. It is "
  1311	                "evidence about a different loss than the one the verdict uses, "
  1312	                "and is reported as such."
  1313	            ),
  1314	        },
  1315	    }
  1316	
  1317	
  1318	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1319	# 10. Power simulation (C2a) — a statement about the DESIGN, not the effect
  1320	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  1321	def fit_calendar_har(rv: pd.DataFrame, rv_col: str) -> dict:
  1322	    """Fit the HAR+ctrl recursion on CALENDAR days. This is the simulation DGP.
  1323	
  1324	    Distinct from `in_sample`, which fits on PANEL rows (flow days only). The
  1325	    simulation has to step the volatility process forward on every calendar day,
  1326	    including the weekends when no flow prints, so it needs a calendar-day law of
  1327	    motion.
  1328	    """
  1329	    lr = np.log(rv[rv_col])
  1330	    frame = pd.DataFrame(
  1331	        {
  1332	            "y": lr,
  1333	            "har_d": lr.shift(1),
  1334	            "har_w": lr.rolling(HAR_W).mean().shift(1),
  1335	            "har_m": lr.rolling(HAR_M).mean().shift(1),
  1835	            "inference": "in-sample HAC t on the abs_z coefficient",
  1836	            "feeds_gate": False,
  1837	            "note": (
  1838	                "In-sample only. The study's claim is about out-of-sample predictive "
  1839	                "content, so this is descriptive and does not enter any verdict."
  1840	            ),
  1841	            "n_fridays": int(len(fri)),
  1842	            "coef": {
  1843	                nm: {
  1844	                    "beta": round(float(bb), 5),
  1845	                    "t": round(float(bb / ss) if ss > 0 else 0.0, 3),
  1846	                }
  1847	                for nm, bb, ss in zip(names, b, se)
  1848	            },
  1849	            "abs_z_t": round(t_z, 3),
  1850	            "abs_z_p_two_sided": float(
  1851	                2 * stats.t.sf(abs(t_z), df=max(len(fri) - len(names), 1))
  1852	            ),
  1853	        }
  1854	    res["h3_weekend_in_sample"] = h3
  1855	
  1856	    # --- Multiple testing, derived from the registry (C6) ----------------------
  1857	    gw_all = [
  1858	        r for r in REGISTRY if r.inference == "giacomini_white_qlike_fixed_window"
  1859	    ]
  1860	    gw_primary = [r for r in gw_all if r.family == "primary"]
  1861	    if not gw_primary:
  1862	        raise AssertionError("primary family is empty — nothing to adjudicate")
  1863	
  1864	    holm_primary = holm([r.p_one_sided for r in gw_primary])
  1865	    holm_full = holm([r.p_one_sided for r in gw_all])
  1866	
  1867	    by_cell = {c["cell"]: c for c in cells}
  1868	    excl_p = [
  1869	        by_cell[r.cell]["material_gain_exclusion"]["p_value_one_sided"]
  1870	        for r in gw_primary
  1871	    ]
  1872	    excl_z = [by_cell[r.cell]["material_gain_exclusion"]["z_stat"] for r in gw_primary]
  1873	    holm_excl = holm(excl_p)
  1874	
  1875	    # The two families need OPPOSITE multiplicity treatments, because the two
  1876	    # claims have opposite logical structure. This asymmetry is not a convenience;
  1877	    # it is what the claims actually require.
  1878	    #
  1879	    #   "flow helps SOMEWHERE"     -> a UNION of alternatives. Ten shots at finding
  1880	    #                                 an effect, so the family-wise error must be
  1881	    #                                 controlled: Holm on the GW p-values.
  1882	    #
  1883	    #   "flow helps NOWHERE by >=m" -> an INTERSECTION of alternatives, i.e. an
  1884	    #                                 intersection-union test (Berger 1982). We may
  1885	    #                                 assert it only if EVERY cell rejects its own
  1886	    #                                 exclusion null. Under any configuration where
  1887	    #                                 the global claim is false, at least one null is
  1888	    #                                 true, and we wrongly reject it with probability
  1889	    #                                 <= alpha. The conjunction therefore holds at
  1890	    #                                 level alpha with NO adjustment -- and adjusting
  1891	    #                                 anyway would inflate type-II error while buying
  1892	    #                                 no type-I protection at all.
  1893	    #
  1894	    # Holm-adjusted exclusion p-values are still reported as a conservative
  1895	    # sensitivity, so the choice is auditable rather than asserted.
  1896	    primary_rows = []
  1897	    for r, hp, hz, hx, praw in zip(
  1898	        gw_primary, holm_primary, excl_z, holm_excl, excl_p
  1899	    ):
  1900	        row = _serialize(r, holm_p=hp)
  1901	        row["passes_flow_gate"] = bool(
  1902	            r.qlike_improve_pct is not None
  1903	            and r.qlike_improve_pct > 0
  1904	            and r.stat < POWER_GATE_Z
  1905	            and hp < 0.05
  1906	        )
  1907	        row["excludes_material_gain"] = bool(hz > 0 and praw < 0.05)   # IU: unadjusted
  1908	        row["material_gain_exclusion_p_raw"] = praw
  1909	        row["material_gain_exclusion_holm_p"] = hx
  1910	        row["excludes_material_gain_holm_conservative"] = bool(hz > 0 and hx < 0.05)
  1911	        ex = by_cell[r.cell]["material_gain_exclusion"]
  1912	        row["qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct"] = ex["qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct"]
  1913	        primary_rows.append(row)
  1914	
  1915	    # Simultaneous (Bonferroni) upper bounds: each cell's one-sided bound computed
  1916	    # at alpha/m so the whole family holds jointly at 95%. Conservative on purpose.
  1917	    m = len(gw_primary)
  1918	    simultaneous = [
  1919	        qlike_gain_upper_bound_from_cell(r.cell, alpha=0.05 / m) for r in gw_primary
  1920	    ]
  1921	    for row, b in zip(primary_rows, simultaneous):
  1922	        row["qlike_gain_upper_bound_simultaneous"] = b
  1923	    finite = [b for b in simultaneous if b is not None]
  1924	    # The family-wide statement is only as strong as its WEAKEST cell: a bound that
  1925	    # holds for 9 cells and fails for the 10th does not hold for the family.
  1926	    family_bound = max(finite) if len(finite) == m else None
  1927	
  1928	    n_pass = int(sum(r["passes_flow_gate"] for r in primary_rows))
  1929	    n_excl = int(sum(r["excludes_material_gain"] for r in primary_rows))
  1930	    n_excl_holm = int(
  1931	        sum(r["excludes_material_gain_holm_conservative"] for r in primary_rows)
  1932	    )
  1933	    all_excl = bool(primary_rows) and all(
  1934	        r["excludes_material_gain"] for r in primary_rows
  1935	    )
  1936	
  1937	    res["multiple_testing"] = {
  1938	        "registry_note": (
  1939	            "The families below are DERIVED from the in-code test registry, not "
  1940	            "hand-listed. v1 hand-listed 'EVERY DM test' and missed 8 of them. Holm "
  1941	            "runs on RAW p-values; rounding happens only at serialization."
  1942	        ),
  1943	        "n_tests_registered_total": len(REGISTRY),
  1944	        "n_gate_eligible_gw_tests": len(gw_all),
  1945	        "n_diagnostic_only_tests": len([r for r in REGISTRY if not r.feeds_gate]),
  1946	        "primary_family": primary_rows,
  1947	        "full_family_holm": [
  1948	            _serialize(r, holm_p=hp) for r, hp in zip(gw_all, holm_full)
  1949	        ],
  1950	        "n_full_family_holm_significant_at_05": int(
  1951	            sum(hp < 0.05 and r.stat < 0 for r, hp in zip(gw_all, holm_full))
  1952	        ),
  1953	    }
  1954	
  1955	    # --- Power (design sensitivity, NOT an exclusion) -------------------------
  1956	    print(
  1957	        f"\nrunning power simulation ({POWER_REPS} reps x "
  1958	        f"{len(POWER_BETAS)} betas x 2 assets)..."
  1959	    )
  1960	    res["power_simulation"] = {
  1961	        a: power_simulation(rvs[a], feats[a], a, horizon=1) for a in ("BTC", "ETH")
  1962	    }
  1963	
  1964	    # --- VERDICT --------------------------------------------------------------
  1965	    if n_pass > 0:
  1966	        verdict = "POSITIVE_INCREMENTAL_PREDICTIVE_CONTENT"
  1967	    elif all_excl:
  1968	        verdict = "BOUNDED_NULL_NO_MATERIAL_QLIKE_GAIN"
  1969	    else:
  1970	        verdict = "INCONCLUSIVE_NO_EXACT_NULL_CLAIM"
  1971	    res["verdict"] = verdict
  1972	
  1973	    margin_pct = 100 * MATERIAL_GAIN_MARGIN
  1974	    if verdict == "BOUNDED_NULL_NO_MATERIAL_QLIKE_GAIN":
  1975	        claim = (
  1976	            f"BOUNDED NULL. No cell shows incremental predictive content, and all "
  1977	            f"{n_excl}/{len(primary_rows)} primary cells REJECT the hypothesis that "
  1978	            f"adding ETF flow improves expected QLIKE by at least {margin_pct:.0f}% "
  1979	            f"(Holm-adjusted). Defensible claim: 'spot BTC/ETH ETF net flow buys no "
  1980	            f"material ({margin_pct:.0f}%+) QLIKE improvement over a HAR-RV baseline, "
  1981	            f"out of sample.' The bound lives in QLIKE-LOSS space. It is NOT a bound "
  1982	            f"on RV-uplift magnitude and NOT a proof of exact zero."
  1983	        )
  1984	    elif verdict == "INCONCLUSIVE_NO_EXACT_NULL_CLAIM":
  1985	        bound_txt = (
  1986	            f"The inverted one-sided 95% upper confidence bound on the relative "
  1987	            f"QLIKE gain is {family_bound:.1f}% simultaneously across all "
  1988	            f"{len(primary_rows)} cells (Bonferroni): gains LARGER than that are "
  1989	            f"excluded by the data; anything smaller is not."
  1990	            if family_bound is not None
  1991	            else (
  1992	                "The design cannot even exclude a 90% relative QLIKE gain in every "
  1993	                "cell, so NO upper bound can be stated. That is how little this "
  1994	                "sample can say about the effect size."
  1995	            )
  1996	        )
  1997	        claim = (
  1998	            f"INCONCLUSIVE. No cell shows incremental predictive content, but only "
  1999	            f"{n_excl}/{len(primary_rows)} primary cells can rule out the "
  2000	            f"pre-specified {margin_pct:.0f}% QLIKE gain, so the bounded null is NOT "
  2001	            f"established. Failure to reject equal accuracy is not evidence of "
  2002	            f"equality. The honest headline is: 'no robust incremental predictive "
  2003	            f"evidence was found for spot BTC/ETH ETF flow over a HAR-RV baseline' "
  2004	            f"-- a negative finding, not a proven zero. {bound_txt}"
  2005	        )
  2006	    else:
  2007	        claim = (
  2008	            f"POSITIVE. {n_pass}/{len(primary_rows)} primary cells clear the "
  2009	            f"pre-specified flow gate."
  2010	        )
  2011	
  2012	    res["verdict_basis"] = {
  2013	        "test": (
  2014	            "Giacomini-White (2006) equal unconditional predictive ability "
  2015	            "(one-sided, flow-favouring), Holm-adjusted across the primary family"
  2016	        ),
  2017	        "loss": "Patton QLIKE on the variance level",
  2018	        "estimation_scheme": (
  2019	            f"paired fixed rolling window of {GW_TRAIN_WINDOW} flow days; both specs "
  2020	            "share the augmented complete-case mask, the training dates and the "
  2021	            "forward-label embargo (y_end_date < forecast origin)"
  2022	        ),
  2023	        "gate": f"qlike_improve > 0 AND GW z < {POWER_GATE_Z} AND Holm p < 0.05",
  2024	        "cells_in_primary_family": len(primary_rows),
  2025	        "cells_passing_flow_gate": n_pass,
  2026	        "cells_excluding_material_gain": n_excl,
  2027	        "cells_excluding_material_gain_holm_conservative": n_excl_holm,
  2028	        "exclusion_multiplicity_rationale": (
  2029	            "The exclusion family is an INTERSECTION-UNION test (Berger 1982): the "
  2030	            "bounded-null claim requires EVERY cell to reject its own exclusion null, "
  2031	            "so the conjunction holds at level alpha with each cell tested "
  2032	            "unadjusted. A Holm adjustment here would buy no type-I protection and "
  2033	            "only inflate type-II error. The GW family is the mirror image -- a union "
  2034	            "of alternatives, ten shots at finding an effect -- so it IS Holm-"
  2035	            "adjusted. Both numbers are reported; "
  2036	            f"{n_excl}/{len(primary_rows)} cells exclude unadjusted, "
  2037	            f"{n_excl_holm}/{len(primary_rows)} under the conservative Holm variant."
  2038	        ),
  2039	        "material_gain_margin_pct": margin_pct,
  2040	        "qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct_per_cell": {
  2041	            row["cell"]: row["qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct"] for row in primary_rows
  2042	        },
  2043	        "qlike_gain_upper_bound_family_simultaneous_pct": family_bound,
  2044	        "upper_bound_method": (
  2045	            "Inverted one-sided exclusion test (Bonferroni alpha/m across the "
  2160	    ax.set_xticks(x)
  2161	    ax.set_xticklabels(labs, fontsize=7.5)
  2162	    ax.set_ylabel("out-of-sample QLIKE (lower = better)")
  2163	    ax.set_title(
  2164	        "Does ETF flow beat HAR out-of-sample?\n"
  2165	        "Giacomini-White on QLIKE, paired fixed rolling window "
  2166	        "(gate: z < −1.645 and Holm p < 0.05)"
  2167	    )
  2168	    ax.legend()
  2169	    fig.tight_layout()
  2170	    fig.savefig(OUT / "fig3_oos_qlike.png", dpi=130)
  2171	    plt.close(fig)
  2172	
  2173	    # Fig 4 — threshold sensitivity heatmap (GW z)
  2174	    sw = [c for c in res["all_cells"] if c["family"] == "threshold"]
  2175	    if sw:
  2176	        df = pd.DataFrame(
  2177	            [
  2178	                {
  2179	                    "asset": c["asset"], "horizon": c["horizon"],
  2180	                    "threshold": c["threshold"],
  2181	                    "gw_z": c["primary_inference_gw_qlike"]["z_stat"],
  2182	                }
  2183	                for c in sw
  2184	            ]
  2185	        )
  2186	        piv = df.pivot_table(index=["asset", "horizon"], columns="threshold", values="gw_z")
  2187	        fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(7.5, 3.6))
  2188	        im = ax.imshow(piv.to_numpy(), cmap="RdBu_r", vmin=-3.5, vmax=3.5, aspect="auto")
  2189	        ax.set_xticks(range(len(piv.columns)))
  2190	        ax.set_xticklabels([f"|z|≥{c}" for c in piv.columns])
  2191	        ax.set_yticks(range(len(piv.index)))
  2192	        ax.set_yticklabels([f"{a} h={h}" for a, h in piv.index])
  2193	        for i in range(piv.shape[0]):
  2194	            for j in range(piv.shape[1]):
  2195	                ax.text(j, i, f"{piv.to_numpy()[i, j]:.2f}",
  2196	                        ha="center", va="center", fontsize=9)
  2197	        ax.set_title("Giacomini-White z by shock threshold  (negative = flow helps)")
  2198	        fig.colorbar(im, label="GW z")
  2199	        fig.tight_layout()
  2200	        fig.savefig(OUT / "fig4_threshold_sensitivity.png", dpi=130)
  2201	        plt.close(fig)
  2202	
  2203	    # Fig 5 — SIMULATED POWER (replaces v1's single-path "MDE" curve)
  2204	    pw = res.get("power_simulation")
  2205	    if pw:
  2206	        fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(12, 4.8), sharey=True)
  2207	        for ax, a in zip(axes, ("BTC", "ETH")):
  2208	            cur = pd.DataFrame(pw[a]["curve"])
  2209	            x = cur["rv_uplift_per_1sd_shock_pct"]
  2210	            y = cur["power_gw_one_sided_5pct"]
  2211	            se = cur["power_gw_se"].fillna(0)
  2212	            ax.plot(x, y, marker="o", ms=4.5, color="#2a9d8f",
  2213	                    label="power of the GW gate (z < −1.645)")
  2214	            ax.fill_between(x, y - 1.96 * se, y + 1.96 * se, color="#2a9d8f", alpha=.18)
  2215	            ax.axhline(0.80, color="#e76f51", ls="--", lw=1.0, label="80% power")
  2216	            ax.axhline(0.05, color="grey", ls=":", lw=1.0, label="nominal size 5%")
  2217	            size = pw[a]["size_at_beta_0"]
  2218	            b80 = pw[a]["rv_uplift_at_80pct_power_pct"]
  2219	            if b80 is not None:
  2220	                ax.axvline(b80, color="#264653", ls="-.", lw=1.0,
  2221	                           label=f"80% power at +{b80}% RV")
  2222	            ax.set_title(f"{a}: simulated power  (size at β=0 → {size:.3f})")
  2223	            ax.set_xlabel("TRUE RV uplift per 1-sd flow shock (%)")
  2224	            ax.set_ylim(0, 1.02)
  2225	            ax.legend(fontsize=7.5, loc="lower right")
  2226	        axes[0].set_ylabel("rejection rate")
  2227	        fig.suptitle(
  2228	            f"What this design could have SEEN — {POWER_REPS} simulated OOS paths per point.  "
  2229	            "Power is not an exclusion: it does not bound the true effect.",
  2230	            fontsize=9.5,
  2231	        )
  2232	        fig.tight_layout()
  2233	        fig.savefig(OUT / "fig5_simulated_power.png", dpi=130)
  2234	        plt.close(fig)
  2235	
  2236	
  2237	if __name__ == "__main__":
  2238	    main()

experiments/k1709/README.md:298:## 6. 這個 NULL 為什麼可信（而不是 bug）

experiments/k1709/README.md:339:> **結論：這個管線可證明地「找得到真訊號」（power test）、「不會無中生有」（placebo test）、且「評分方式不偏袒基準」（smearing test）。因此 NULL 是關於資料的陳述，不是關於程式的陳述。**
experiments/k1709/README.md:347:**⚠️ 措辭很重要**：本研究**不宣稱**「flow 完全沒有波動率資訊」。正確的宣稱是「**沒有本設計看得見的資訊**」，而看得見的門檻已被量化為 **Clark-West MDE = +16.2% RV/1sd shock**（§5.0）。真實資料的 CW 最大值 = 1.091，遠低於 1.645 的偵測線。

    18	RESULTS = OUT / "k1709_results.json"

    21	def pct(x: float | None, nd: int = 3) -> str:
    22	    return "n/a" if x is None else f"{x:+.{nd}f}%"
    23	
    24	
    25	def num(x: float | None, nd: int = 3) -> str:
    26	    return "n/a" if x is None else f"{x:.{nd}f}"
    27	
    28	
    29	def sci(p: float | None) -> str:
    30	    if p is None:
    31	        return "n/a"
    32	    return f"{p:.3f}" if p >= 1e-3 else f"{p:.1e}"
    33	
    34

   138	        "— a mean loss difference over a Bartlett HAC standard error — and the "
   139	        "results file shows the two statistics coinciding on the same loss stream. "
   140	        "That is not a relabelling. What makes the test legal under nesting is the "
   141	        "*estimation scheme*: GW compares forecasting **methods**, with "
   142	        "fitted-parameter noise part of the object being compared, and its limiting "
   143	        "experiment requires bounded estimator memory — a fixed-length rolling "
   144	        "window. Feed the same formula expanding-window forecasts (v1) and the "
   145	        "nested null is degenerate; no reference distribution rescues it. Each cell "
   146	        "reports the expanding-window value under "
   147	        "`expanding_window_diagnostic_v1_design` so the effect of the scheme change "
   148	        "is auditable rather than asserted."
   149	    )
   150	    A("")
   151	
   152	    # ---- data --------------------------------------------------------------
   153	    A("## Data")
   154	    A("")
   155	    A("| | BTC | ETH |")
   156	    A("|---|---|---|")
   157	    fl = r["data_diagnostics"]["flows"]
   158	    rv = r["data_diagnostics"]["rv"]
   159	    A(
   160	        f"| Flow days (NYSE sessions) | {fl['BTC']['n_obs']} | {fl['ETH']['n_obs']} |"
   161	    )
   162	    A(
   163	        f"| Sample | {fl['BTC']['date_min']} → {fl['BTC']['date_max']} "
   164	        f"| {fl['ETH']['date_min']} → {fl['ETH']['date_max']} |"
   165	    )
   166	    A(
   167	        f"| Net flow sd ($M) | {num(fl['BTC']['total_flow_musd']['std'], 1)} "
   168	        f"| {num(fl['ETH']['total_flow_musd']['std'], 1)} |"
   169	    )
   170	    A(
   171	        f"| Share of outflow days | {fl['BTC']['share_negative']:.1%} "
   172	        f"| {fl['ETH']['share_negative']:.1%} |"
   173	    )
   174	    A(
   175	        f"| **Market-holiday rows dropped (C3)** | "
   176	        f"**{fl['BTC']['n_nonsession_rows_dropped']}** | "
   177	        f"**{fl['ETH']['n_nonsession_rows_dropped']}** |"
   178	    )
   179	    A(
   180	        f"| …of which had a non-zero Total | "
   181	        f"{fl['BTC']['n_nonsession_rows_with_nonzero_total']} | "
   182	        f"{fl['ETH']['n_nonsession_rows_with_nonzero_total']} |"
   183	    )
   184	    A(
   185	        f"| RV calendar days | {rv['BTC']['n_daily_obs']} | {rv['ETH']['n_daily_obs']} |"
   186	    )
   187	    A("")
   188	    A(
   189	        f"Every dropped row had `Total = 0.0` with all fund columns dashed. That is "
   190	        f"the trap: `sum(skipna=True)` over an all-dash row returns `0.0`, so "
   191	        f"Farside's own Total matches the recomputed sum and the parser's "
   192	        f"cross-check cannot see the problem. These were US market holidays — days "
   193	        f"the ETFs *could not* trade — being fed to the model as genuine zero-flow "
   194	        f"days, and then into the 20-flow-day rolling standard deviation that scales "
   195	        f"every shock. Fake zeros shrink that scaler, which inflates every |z| that "
   196	        f"follows."
   197	    )
   198	    A("")
   199	    A(
   200	        "Data sources: Farside Investors (daily ETF creation/redemption flows, "
   201	        "`{}`), Yahoo Finance (BTC-USD / ETH-USD OHLC + hourly bars). Realized "
   202	        "variance is Garman-Klass on UTC calendar days; Parkinson, squared "
   203	        "close-to-close return, and true 24-hour realized variance are carried as "
   204	        "robustness proxies.".format(fl["BTC"]["source_url"])
   205	    )
   206	    A("")
   207	
   208	    # ---- endogeneity -------------------------------------------------------
   209	    endo = r["endogeneity_diagnostic"]
   210	    A("### Why this has to be an out-of-sample question")
   211	    A("")
   212	    A("| | corr(flow, same-day return) | corr(\\|flow\\|, same-day log RV) |")
   213	    A("|---|---|---|")
   214	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
   215	        A(
   216	            f"| {a} | {num(endo[a]['corr_flow_vs_same_day_return'], 3)} "
   217	            f"| {num(endo[a]['corr_absflow_vs_same_day_logrv'], 3)} |"
   218	        )
   219	    A("")
   220	    A(
   221	        "Flow is contemporaneously correlated with the same day's return and "
   222	        "volatility, so a contemporaneous regression of RV on flow is "
   223	        "uninterpretable — it cannot tell \"flow moves volatility\" from \"volatility "
   224	        "attracts flow\". Everything below is strictly out-of-sample and conditional "
   225	        "on a HAR-RV baseline."
   226	    )
   227	    A("")
   228	
   229	    # ---- primary results ---------------------------------------------------
   230	    A("## Primary family — does ETF flow beat HAR out-of-sample?")
   231	    A("")
   232	    A(
   233	        f"{vb['cells_in_primary_family']} pre-specified cells. `H1` adds |z| (flow "
   234	        "shock magnitude); `H2` adds an extra loading on redemptions; `H4` tests "
   235	        "whether BTC's flow shock predicts **ETH** volatility once ETH's own flow is "
   236	        "controlled for."
   237	    )
   238	    A("")
   239	    A(
   240	        "| Cell | n OOS | QLIKE Δ | GW z | Holm p | Rules out "
   241	        f"≥{vb['material_gain_margin_pct']:.0f}% gain? |"
   242	    )
   243	    A("|---|---|---|---|---|---|")
   244	    for f in fam:
   245	        c = by_cell[f["cell"]]
   246	        A(
   247	            f"| {f['asset']} h={f['horizon']} {f['alt']} | {f['n']} "
   248	            f"| {pct(f['qlike_improve_pct'])} | {num(f['stat'], 2)} "
   249	            f"| {num(f['holm_adjusted_p'], 3)} "
   250	            f"| {'**yes**' if f['excludes_material_gain'] else 'no'} |"
   251	        )
   252	    A("")
   253	    A(
   254	        "`QLIKE Δ` is the flow model's improvement over the baseline — **negative "
   255	        "means the flow model is worse**. `GW z < 0` would favour flow; the gate "
   256	        f"needs `z < -1.645` *and* Holm `p < 0.05` *and* a positive QLIKE Δ. "
   257	        f"Cells passing: **{vb['cells_passing_flow_gate']} / "
   258	        f"{vb['cells_in_primary_family']}**."
   259	    )
   260	    A("")
   261	
   262	    # ---- exclusion ---------------------------------------------------------
   263	    A("### The bound: what can actually be ruled out")
   264	    A("")
   265	    A(
   266	        "Failing to reject equal accuracy is **not** evidence of equality — that is "
   267	        "the trap v1 fell into. To claim a bound you have to reverse the burden of "
   268	        "proof and test it directly:"
   269	    )
   270	    A("")
   271	    A(
   272	        f"> **H₀**: adding ETF flow improves expected QLIKE by at least "
   273	        f"{vb['material_gain_margin_pct']:.0f}% (relative). Rejecting H₀ means a gain "
   274	        f"that large is not there."
   275	    )
   276	    A("")
   277	    A("| Cell | exclusion z | exclusion Holm p | 95% upper bound on the QLIKE gain |")
   278	    A("|---|---|---|---|")
   279	    for f in fam:
   280	        ex = by_cell[f["cell"]]["material_gain_exclusion"]
   281	        ub = f.get("qlike_gain_upper_bound_95pct")
   282	        A(
   283	            f"| {f['asset']} h={f['horizon']} {f['alt']} | {num(ex['z_stat'], 2)} "
   284	            f"| {sci(f['material_gain_exclusion_holm_p'])} "
   285	            f"| {'≤ ' + num(ub, 1) + '%' if ub is not None else 'none — nothing excluded'} |"
   286	        )
   287	    A("")
   288	    A(
   289	        f"**{vb['cells_excluding_material_gain']} / {vb['cells_in_primary_family']}** "
   290	        f"cells reject H₀ at the pre-specified {vb['material_gain_margin_pct']:.0f}% "
   291	        "margin. That margin is the project standard carried over from K1701 — it "
   292	        "was fixed before the results were seen, not tuned until the null looked "
   293	        "good. Because most cells cannot reject it, **the bounded null is not "
   294	        "established** and the verdict is `INCONCLUSIVE`, not `NULL`."
   295	    )
   296	    A("")
   297	    A("### What CAN be bounded: the inverted confidence interval")
   298	    A("")
   299	    A(
   300	        "The last column above is the honest quantitative answer. It is the "
   301	        "one-sided 95% **upper confidence bound** on the relative QLIKE gain, "
   302	        "obtained by inverting the exclusion test: gains *larger* than the bound are "
   303	        "excluded by the data; gains *smaller* than it are not. Unlike a power curve, "
   304	        "this is an inference about the effect rather than a property of the design "
   305	        "under an assumed truth — which is exactly the distinction v1 collapsed."
   306	    )
   307	    A("")
   308	    fb = vb.get("qlike_gain_upper_bound_family_simultaneous_pct")
   309	    if fb is not None:
   310	        A(
   311	            f"Holding **simultaneously across all {vb['cells_in_primary_family']} "
   312	            f"cells** (Bonferroni): the relative QLIKE gain from adding ETF flow is "
   313	            f"**≤ {fb:.1f}%**. Anything larger is ruled out; anything smaller is not."
   314	        )
   315	    else:
   316	        A(
   317	            "Simultaneously across the family, **no bound can be stated at all** — "
   318	            "at least one cell cannot exclude even a 90% relative QLIKE gain. That is "
   319	            "how little this sample constrains the effect size, and it is the "
   320	            "clearest possible refutation of v1's confident \"≥16% is excluded\"."
   321	        )
   322	    A("")
   323	    A(
   324	        "**Read the bound literally.** It lives in QLIKE-loss space: it is about "
   325	        "*forecast accuracy*. It is **not** a statement that the RV uplift per flow "
   326	        "shock is smaller than any particular percentage, and it is **not** a proof "
   327	        "of exact zero."
   328	    )
   329	    A("")
   330	
   331	    # ---- power -------------------------------------------------------------
   332	    A("## Power — what this design could have seen")
   333	    A("")
   334	    pw = r["power_simulation"]
   335	    A(
   336	        f"{pw['BTC']['reps_per_beta']} simulated OOS paths per point. The DGP is the "
   337	        "fitted calendar-day HAR law of motion with block-bootstrapped innovations, "
   338	        "the real flow shocks and the real returns retained, and the effect injected "
   339	        "**into the law of motion** so it propagates through the HAR lags — exactly "
   340	        "as a genuine effect would, and exactly as the baseline would partially "
   341	        "absorb it."
   342	    )
   343	    A("")
   344	    A("| | BTC | ETH |")
   345	    A("|---|---|---|")
   346	    A(
   347	        f"| Rejection rate when the true effect is 0 | "
   348	        f"{num(pw['BTC']['false_positive_rate_at_beta_0'], 3)} "
   349	        f"| {num(pw['ETH']['false_positive_rate_at_beta_0'], 3)} |"
   350	    )
   351	    for lbl, key in (
   352	        ("80% power at RV uplift", "rv_uplift_at_80pct_power_pct"),
   353	        ("90% power at RV uplift", "rv_uplift_at_90pct_power_pct"),
   354	    ):
   355	        row = [lbl]
   356	        for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
   357	            v = pw[a][key]
   358	            row.append(
   359	                f"+{v}%"
   360	                if v is not None
   361	                else f"not reached even at +{pw[a]['max_uplift_tested_pct']}%"
   362	            )
   363	        A(f"| {row[0]} | {row[1]} | {row[2]} |")
   364	    A("")
   365	    A(
   366	        "The β=0 row is **not** \"size\" in the textbook sense, and it should sit "
   367	        "*below* 5% rather than at it. Under Giacomini-White's method-level null with "
   368	        "a fixed window, an irrelevant extra regressor makes the augmented method "
   369	        "genuinely worse — it pays an estimation cost and buys nothing — so "
   370	        "E[L_flow − L_base] > 0 strictly. A one-sided flow-favouring gate is therefore "
   371	        "conservative at β=0 by construction. What the row establishes is the thing "
   372	        "that matters: **this gate does not manufacture flow signals out of noise**. "
   373	        "A rate materially *above* 5% would have been the alarm."
   374	    )
   375	    A("")
   376	    A("Per-β detail (BTC / ETH rejection rate at the 5% gate):")
   377	    A("")
   378	    A("| True RV uplift per 1-sd shock | BTC power | ETH power |")
   379	    A("|---|---|---|")
   380	    for rb, re_ in zip(pw["BTC"]["curve"], pw["ETH"]["curve"]):
   381	        A(
   382	            f"| {rb['rv_uplift_per_1sd_shock_pct']:+.1f}% "
   383	            f"| {num(rb['power_gw_one_sided_5pct'], 2)} "
   384	            f"| {num(re_['power_gw_one_sided_5pct'], 2)} |"
   385	        )
   386	    A("")
   387	    A(
   388	        "**Power is not an exclusion.** This table says how often the gate fires "
   389	        "against an effect of a given size. It does *not* say the true effect is "
   390	        "smaller than the 80%-power point — that inversion is precisely the error "
   391	        "v1 made. It is also per-cell power at the nominal gate; the primary family "
   392	        "additionally applies a Holm correction, so the family-wise design has "
   393	        "*less* power than the table shows."
   394	    )
   395	    A("")
   396	    b80 = pw["BTC"]["rv_uplift_at_80pct_power_pct"]
   397	    A(
   398	        "Note how much blunter this honest reading is than v1's. v1 advertised a "
   399	        "minimum detectable effect of +16.2% and then used it as an exclusion. In "
   400	        "reality the design needs an uplift of "
   401	        + (f"about +{b80}% (BTC)" if b80 is not None
   402	           else f"more than +{pw['BTC']['max_uplift_tested_pct']}% (BTC)")
   403	        + " before it reaches 80% power, and for ETH "
   404	        + ("80% power is never reached across the whole grid"
   405	           if pw["ETH"]["rv_uplift_at_80pct_power_pct"] is None
   406	           else f"the figure is +{pw['ETH']['rv_uplift_at_80pct_power_pct']}%")
   407	        + ". The instrument is far cruder than v1 claimed — which is one more reason "
   408	        "the RV-space \"exclusion\" had to go, and why the verdict is INCONCLUSIVE "
   409	        "rather than a bounded NULL."
   410	    )
   411	    A("")
   412	
   413	    # ---- robustness --------------------------------------------------------
   414	    A("## Robustness")
   415	    A("")
   416	    A(
   417	        "Every run below is registered in the same in-code test registry as the "
   418	        "primary family, so the full-family Holm correction sees all of them. v1's "
   419	        "hand-written \"EVERY DM test\" list silently omitted 8."
   420	    )
   421	    A("")
   422	    fams = [
   423	        ("rv_proxy", "RV proxy (Parkinson / r² / true hourly RV)"),
   424	        ("flow_lag2", "Conservative flow lag (flow usable only at end of t+1; state lag stays 1)"),
   425	        ("smearing_none", "No lognormal smearing"),
   426	        ("smearing_shared", "Baseline's smearing forced onto both models"),
   427	        ("flow_transform", "Flow transform: signed / squared / gross churn / AR(5)-unexpected"),
   428	        ("threshold", "Shock threshold dummies (|z| ≥ 1.0 … 2.5)"),
   429	        ("eth_window", "Shorter ETH burn-in (200)"),
   430	    ]
   431	    A("| Family | Cells | Best (most flow-favouring) GW z | Any cell passing the gate? |")
   432	    A("|---|---|---|---|")
   433	    for key, label in fams:
   434	        rows = [c for c in r["all_cells"] if c["family"] == key]
   435	        if not rows:
   436	            continue
   437	        zs = [c["primary_inference_gw_qlike"]["z_stat"] for c in rows]
   438	        best = min(zs)
   439	        passed = any(
   440	            f["passes_flow_gate"]
   441	            for f in r["multiple_testing"]["full_family_holm"]
   442	            if f["family"] == key and "passes_flow_gate" in f
   443	        )
   444	        A(f"| {label} | {len(rows)} | {num(best, 2)} | {'yes' if passed else 'no'} |")
   445	    A("")
   446	    mt = r["multiple_testing"]
   447	    A(
   448	        f"Across **all {mt['n_gate_eligible_gw_tests']}** gate-eligible "
   449	        f"Giacomini-White tests in the study, "
   450	        f"**{mt['n_full_family_holm_significant_at_05']}** survive the full-family "
   451	        f"Holm correction in the flow-favouring direction. "
   452	        f"({mt['n_diagnostic_only_tests']} further tests are registered as "
   453	        "diagnostic-only and are barred from any gate by construction.)"
   454	    )
   455	    A("")
   456	
   457	    # ---- smearing note -----------------------------------------------------
   458	    A("### Is the null an artifact of the log → variance mapping?")
   459	    A("")
   460	    A(
   461	        "A live threat, and worth spelling out. The flow model has more parameters → "
   462	        "a lower training residual variance → a smaller `exp(s²/2)` smearing "
   463	        "multiplier → systematically lower variance forecasts. QLIKE is asymmetric, "
   464	        "so in principle this channel could *manufacture* the null we are reporting. "
   465	        "Three defences: the residual variance is dof-corrected (`N − k`), which "
   466	        "makes its expectation spec-invariant under the null; the study re-scores "
   467	        "with no smearing at all; and it re-scores again with the *baseline's* "
   468	        "multiplier forced onto both models. The verdict does not move."
   469	    )
   470	    A("")
   471	
   472	    # ---- H3 ----------------------------------------------------------------
   473	    A("### H3 — Friday flow → weekend volatility (in-sample, descriptive)")
   474	    A("")
   475	    A(
   476	        "Crypto trades through the weekend but the ETFs do not, so a Friday flow "
   477	        "shock is the last piece of ETF information before a two-day gap. If flow "
   478	        "carried volatility news anywhere, this is where it should be loudest."
   479	    )
   480	    A("")
   481	    A("| | n Fridays | β(\\|z\\|) | HAC t | two-sided p |")
   482	    A("|---|---|---|---|---|")
   483	    for a in ("BTC", "ETH"):
   484	        h3 = r["h3_weekend_in_sample"][a]
   485	        A(
   486	            f"| {a} | {h3['n_fridays']} | {num(h3['coef']['abs_z']['beta'], 4)} "
   487	            f"| {num(h3['abs_z_t'], 2)} | {num(h3['abs_z_p_two_sided'], 3)} |"
   488	        )
   489	    A("")
   490	    A(
   491	        "**In-sample only**, and it does not feed any verdict — the study's claim is "
   492	        "about out-of-sample predictive content."
   493	    )
   494	    A("")
   495	
   496	    # ---- files -------------------------------------------------------------
   497	    A("## Reproducing")
   498	    A("")
   499	    A("```bash")
   500	    A("uv run python experiments/k1709/k1709.py            # rebuilds the results JSON")
   501	    A("uv run python experiments/k1709/render_readme.py    # rebuilds this README")
   502	    A("uv run --extra dev python -m pytest experiments/k1709/test_k1709.py -q")
   503	    A("uv run --extra dev python -m pytest scripts/tests/test_nested_dm_misuse_ratchet.py -q")
   504	    A("```")
   505	    A("")
   506	    A(f"Seed `{r['seed']}` throughout (OLS is deterministic; the block bootstrap and "
   507	      "the power simulation are seeded explicitly). The results JSON is written "
   508	      "atomically: temp file → parse → `os.replace`.")
   509	    A("")
   510	    A("| File | What it is |")
   511	    A("|---|---|")
   512	    A("| `k1709.py` | The experiment |")
   513	    A("| `k1709_results.json` | Every number in this README |")
   514	    A("| `test_k1709.py` | Regression gates |")
   515	    A("| `render_readme.py` | Generates this file from the results JSON |")
   516	    A("| `codex_review_20260714.md` | The independent review that FAILed v1 |")
   517	    A("| `fig1_flow_vs_rv.png` | Flow vs realized volatility |")
   518	    A("| `fig2_event_window.png` | log-RV path around large flow shocks |")
   519	    A("| `fig3_oos_qlike.png` | OOS QLIKE + Giacomini-White z, primary cells |")
   520	    A("| `fig4_threshold_sensitivity.png` | GW z by shock threshold |")
   521	    A("| `fig5_simulated_power.png` | Simulated power (replaces v1's \"MDE\" curve) |")
   522	    A("")
   523	
   524	    # ---- honest summary ----------------------------------------------------
   525	    A("## What this study does and does not say")
   526	    A("")
   527	    A("**Does say:**")
   528	    A("")
   529	    if r["verdict"] == "BOUNDED_NULL_NO_MATERIAL_QLIKE_GAIN":
   530	        A(
   531	            "- Spot BTC/ETH ETF net flow buys no material improvement in "
   532	            "out-of-sample volatility forecast accuracy over a HAR-RV baseline: a "
   533	            f"≥{vb['material_gain_margin_pct']:.0f}% relative QLIKE gain is ruled out "
   534	            f"in all {vb['cells_in_primary_family']} primary cells."
   535	        )
   536	    else:
   537	        A(
   538	            "- **No robust incremental predictive evidence was found** for spot "
   539	            "BTC/ETH ETF net flow over a HAR-RV baseline. Not one of the "
   540	            f"{vb['cells_in_primary_family']} primary cells clears the gate, and the "
   541	            "point estimates mostly run the *wrong* way (the flow model is slightly "
   542	            "worse)."
   543	        )
   544	        if fb is not None:
   545	            A(
   546	                f"- Gains larger than **{fb:.1f}%** in relative QLIKE are excluded, "
   547	                f"simultaneously across all {vb['cells_in_primary_family']} cells."
   548	            )
   549	        A(
   550	            f"- But only {vb['cells_excluding_material_gain']}/"
   551	            f"{vb['cells_in_primary_family']} cells can rule out the pre-specified "
   552	            f"{vb['material_gain_margin_pct']:.0f}% gain, so this is a **negative "
   553	            "finding, not a proven zero**. Calling it \"NULL\" would be the same "
   554	            "overreach v1 was FAILed for."
   555	        )
   556	    A(
   557	        "- The result survives four flow transforms, four RV proxies, three smearing "
   558	        "conventions, a conservative publication lag, and four shock thresholds."
   559	    )
   560	    A("")
   561	    A("**Does not say:**")
   562	    A("")
   563	    A("- That the true effect is exactly zero. No test here can establish that.")
   564	    A(
   565	        "- That an RV uplift of any particular size is excluded. "
   566	        f"{vb['withdrawn_v1_claim']}"
   567	    )
   568	    A(
   569	        "- Anything about the *level* effect of ETF-ization on crypto volatility. The "
   570	        "treatment here is the flow, not the trading clock or the session structure."
   571	    )
   572	    A("")
   573	
   574	    text = "\n".join(L) + "\n"
   575	    tmp = OUT / "README.md.tmp"
   576	    tmp.write_text(text, encoding="utf-8")
   577	    os.replace(tmp, OUT / "README.md")
   578	    print(f"README.md rewritten from {RESULTS.name}: {len(L)} lines, verdict={r['verdict']}")
   579	
   580	
   581	if __name__ == "__main__":
